At Sunday Matins (§ 3), the Canons unite the Resurrection Canon with those of the Saints; after Ode 3, the Kathisma of Saint Barbara (*Τὸν νυμφίον σου Χριστὸν ἀγαπήσασα* / "Having loved Christ thy Bridegroom") is joined with that of Saint John (*Τὴν τοῦ βίου ἀπάτην* / "The deceit of this life") and the Theotokion (*Τὸ ἐξαίσιον θαῦμα* / "The astounding miracle"); and the Katavasiai of the Nativity (*Χριστὸς γεννᾶται* / "Christ is born, give ye glory") are chanted, followed by the complete Sunday Morning Gospel cursus. At the Praises, 4 Resurrection Stichira are joined with 4 of the Venerable Father (transferred from the Aposticha of Vespers), concluding with the appointed Sunday Eothinon Doxastikon.

[^186]: Commemoration of our Venerable and God-bearing Father Savvas the Sanctified and Sunday Concurrence (Violakis, pp. 238–241, December 4 § 4 concl.; December 5, §§ 1–4). December 5 commemorates Saint Savvas the Sanctified (439–532), founder of the Great Laura (Mar Saba) in the Judean wilderness and patron of the ancient Sabaitic monastic typikon that forms the liturgical foundation of the Byzantine rite. Concluding December 4 (§ 4), the Divine Liturgy assigns 4 Beatitudes to the Tone and 4 to Ode 6 of the Canon of Saint Barbara, with her Epistle (Galatians 3:23–4:5) preceding the Sunday Gospel. For December 5 on a weekday (§ 1), the service is chanted according to the Menaion, concluding the Aposticha of Vespers with the Theotokion *Δέσποινα, πρόσδεξαι* ("Lady, accept the prayers of Thy servants"). When December 5 falls on a Sunday (§§ 2–4), Saturday Great Vespers prescribes 6 Resurrection Stichira and 4 of Saint Savvas at "Lord, I have cried", followed at "Glory" by the Doxastikon of the Saint and at "Both now and ever" by the First Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone; three Old Testament composite readings from the Wisdom books (Wisdom 3:1–9; 5:15–6:3; 4:7–15) are read; and the Aposticha conclude with "Glory" of Saint Savvas and "Both now and ever" *Ἀνύμφευτε Παρθένε* ("O unwedded Virgin"). At Sunday Matins (§ 3), the Litiya of Saint Savvas is performed after Psalm 50 [LXX]; at the Psalter, the first two stichologiai take the Resurrection Kathismata while the third assigns the Kathisma of the Saint; the canons combine the Resurrection Canon with that of the Saint; after Ode 3, the Kontakion, Ikos, and Kathisma of Saint Savvas are chanted; after Ode 6, the Resurrection Kontakion and Ikos are chanted with the Menaion Synaxarion; and the Katavasiai are *Χριστὸς γεννᾶται*. At the Divine Liturgy (§ 4), the Beatitudes assign 4 to the Tone and 4 to Ode 6 of the Saint's canon, with the Epistle of the Saint (Galatians 5:22–6:2, the fruits of the Spirit) read before the Sunday Gospel.

[^187]: Feast of Saint Nicholas the Wonderworker, Archbishop of Myra in Lycia, and Patriarchal Textual Criticism on Nicholas Malaxos (Violakis, pp. 240–243, December 5 § 4 concl.; December 6, §§ 1–3, Note 1). December 6 marks the universal solemnity of Saint Nicholas the Wonderworker, Archbishop of Myra in Lycia (ca. 270–343). At Great Vespers on a weekday (§ 1), following the First Stasis of the Psalter (*Blessed is the man*, Pss. 1–3 LXX), 6 Prosomoia are sung at "Lord, I have cried" to the model melody *Μύροις παροικήσας αἰσθητῶς* ("Having dwelt in Myra in the flesh"), followed by three Old Testament lections (Proverbs 10:7, 6; 3:13–16; 8:6 etc.; Proverbs 10:31–11:12; Wisdom 4:7–15). At Matins (§ 2), after the Polyeleos and the First Antiphon of Tone 4 (*Ἐκ νεότητός μου* / "From my youth"), the Prokeimenon is Psalm 115:6 LXX (*Τίμιος ἐναντίον Κυρίου ὁ θάνατος τῶν ὁσίων αὐτοῦ* / "Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints"), the Gospel is John 10:9–16, and the canons unite the Theotokion Canon *ᾨδὴν ἐπινίκιον* with the Saint's Canon *Ἀπόρῳ γλώττῃ καὶ χείλεσιν* ("With halting tongue and lips"). Crucially, printed Note 1 on page 240 records a fundamental patriarchal textual decree: the other three Prosomoia composed to the melody *Ποίοις εὐφημιῶν* ("With what garlands of praise") are explicitly prohibited because their author, Nicholas Malaxos (Presbyter of Peloponnesus and Protopresbyter of Nauplion, fl. ca. 1580–1640), had arbitrarily inserted his own compositions into the Venetian printed Menaia without patriarchal synodal sanction. The Holy Synod in Constantinople formally condemned Malaxos as an ecclesiastical innovator (*καινοτόμος*) and permanently banned his interpolated hymns from liturgical cantillation across the Orthodox Church.

[^188]: Feast of Saint Nicholas the Wonderworker: Sunday Concurrence Propers and Liturgical Distribution (Violakis, pp. 242–245, December 6, §§ 4–6). When the feast of Saint Nicholas coincides with a Sunday, the Typikon establishes the full rubrical synthesis between the Resurrectional dominical cursus and the feast of the Hierarch. At Saturday Great Vespers (§ 4), 4 Resurrection Stichira and 6 of Saint Nicholas (*Μύροις παροικήσας*) are chanted at "Lord, I have cried", followed at "Glory" by the Doxastikon of the Saint and at "Both now and ever" by the First Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone; the Old Testament lections of the Hierarch are read; and at the Aposticha, the Resurrection Stichira are joined with "Glory" of the Saint and the Forefeast Theotokion *Ἀνύμφευτε Παρθένε, πόθεν ἥκεις;* ("O unwedded Virgin, whence art thou come?"). At Sunday Matins (§ 5), following the Polyeleos, the Kathismata are distributed across three stichologiai: at the first two, one Resurrection Kathisma and one of Saint Nicholas with its Theotokion; at the third, the Kathisma of the Saint with its Theotokion. The canons combine the Resurrection Canon with that of the Hierarch; after Ode 3, the Resurrection Kontakion and Ikos are recited plain, followed by the Kathisma and Theotokion of Saint Nicholas; after Ode 6, the Kontakion and Ikos of the Saint are chanted with the Menaion Synaxarion; and the Katavasiai are *Χριστὸς γεννᾶται*. At the Praises, 4 Resurrection Stichira and 4 of the Saint are sung, followed at "Glory" by the Saint's Doxastikon *Σαλπίσωμεν ἐν σάλπιγγι ᾀσμάτων* ("Let us blow the trumpet of praise") and at "Both now and ever" by *Ὑπερευλογημένη* ("Most blessed art Thou, O Virgin Theotokos").

[^189]: Feast of the Conception of Saint Anna, the Mother of the Theotokos, and Dedication of the Church of the Resurrection in Jerusalem (Violakis, pp. 244–245, December 6 § 6 concl.; December 9, §§ 1–3). December 9 celebrates the Conception by Saint Anna of the Most Holy Theotokos, joined with the commemoration of the Dedication (*Enkainia*) of the Church of the Holy Sepulchre (the Anastasis) in Jerusalem. On a weekday (§ 1), both offices are combined according to the Menaion. When December 9 coincides with a Sunday (§§ 2–3), Saturday Great Vespers appoints 6 Resurrection Stichira and 4 of Saint Anna at "Lord, I have cried", followed at "Glory" by *Τὸ ἀπόρρητον τοῖς Ἀγγέλοις* ("That which was inexpressible to the Angels") and at "Both now and ever" by the First Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone; at the Aposticha, the Resurrection Stichira conclude at "Glory; Both now and ever" with *Σήμερον ἐκ ῥίζης τοῦ Δαβίδ* ("Today from the root of David"); and the Dismissal Troparion of Saint Anna is *Σήμερον τῆς ἀτεκνίας δεσμὰ διαλύονται* ("Today the bonds of barrenness are loosed"). At Sunday Matins (§ 3), the Polyeleos is chanted; the Kathismata interleave 2 of the Resurrection and one of Saint Anna (*Ὁ νέος οὐρανός* / "The new heaven") at the first stichologia, and 2 of the Resurrection and one of Saint Anna (*Ἀναβόησον, Δαυίδ* / "Cry aloud, O David") at the second; the canons unite the Resurrection Canon with that of Saint Anna (*Τὴν σύλληψιν σήμερον* / "Thy conception today"); after Ode 3, the Kathisma *Χορὸς προφητικός* ("The prophetic choir") is sung with the Theotokion *Ἰωακεὶμ ὁ Ἱερεὺς καὶ ἡ Ἄννα* ("Joachim the Priest and Anna"); after Ode 6, the Resurrection Kontakion and Ikos are chanted with the Synaxarion; and the Katavasiai are *Χριστὸς γεννᾶται*. At the Praises, 4 Resurrection Stichira and 4 of Saint Anna (*Πληροφοροῦ, Ἀδάμ* / "Rejoice, O Adam") are sung, with the leaf terminating mid-sentence at "Both now and ever: Most", which resolves seamlessly into Cohort 27 on physical leaf p261.


## Cohort 27 Footnotes

[^190]: Feast of the Conception of Saint Anna Concluded (Violakis, pp. 246–247 § 4) and the Sunday of the Holy Forefathers (§§ 1–2). Physical leaf p261 (printed page 246) and facing English leaf p262 (printed page 247) conclude the feast of the Conception by Saint Anna of the Most Holy Theotokos (December 9) by completing the Divine Liturgy propers (§ 4), assigning 4 Beatitudes to the Tone and 4 to Ode 6 of the Canon of Saint Anna, the Kontakion *Ἑορτάζει σήμερον ἡ Οἰκουμένη* ("Today the universe celebrateth"), and the Epistle Galatians 4:22–31. The text then establishes the liturgical cursus for the Sunday of the Holy Forefathers (the Ancestors of Christ according to the flesh, celebrated on the Sunday falling between December 11 and 17). This solemn dominical observance honors all the righteous patriarchs, righteous men, and holy women of the Old Covenant from Adam, Abel, Seth, Enosh, Enoch, Noah, and Melchizedek, through Abraham, Isaac, Jacob, the Twelve Patriarchs, Moses, Aaron, Joshua, Samuel, David, and all the Holy Prophets down to Saint John the Forerunner. At Saturday Great Vespers (§ 1), following the Psalter, at "Lord, I have cried" 6 Resurrection Stichira are joined with 4 of the Forefathers, concluding at "Glory" with the celebrated idiomelic Doxastikon in Tone 6 *Τοὺς πρὸ τοῦ νόμου Πατέρας* ("The Fathers before the Law"), at "Both now and ever" with the First Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone, at the Aposticha with the Doxastikon *Τῶν Προπατόρων τὸ σύστημα* ("The assembly of the Forefathers"), and the Dismissal Troparion in Tone 2 *Ἐν πίστει τοὺς Προπάτορας ἐδικαίωσας* ("Through faith Thou didst justify the Forefathers"). At Sunday Matins (§ 2), the canons combine the Resurrection Canon with that of the Forefathers composed by Saint Germanus of Constantinople (*Πατράσιν αἶνον προσάξωμεν* / "Let us offer praise to the Fathers"); after Ode 3, the Hypakoe *Εἰς δρόσον τοῖς παισὶ* ("Unto dew for the Children") and the Resurrection Kontakion are recited; after Ode 6, the Kontakion of the Forefathers (*Χειρόγραφον εἰκόνα μὴ σεβασθέντες* / "Refusing to worship the graven image") and Ikos are chanted together with the Menaion Synaxarion and the special Hypomnema of the Sunday of the Forefathers; the Katavasiai of the Nativity (*Χριστὸς γεννᾶται* / "Christ is born, give ye glory") are sung; and at the Praises, 4 Resurrection Stichira are combined with 4 of the Forefathers (*Τὴν τῶν σεπτῶν* / "The memory of the venerable"), concluding with the idiomelon *Δεῦτε ἅπαντες πιστοὶ πανηγυρίσωμεν* ("Come, all ye faithful, let us feast").

[^191]: Sunday of the Forefathers Concluded (Violakis, pp. 248–249 § 3) and the Feast of Saint Spyridon the Wonderworker of Trimythous with Sunday Concurrence (December 12, §§ 1–3). Physical leaf p263 (printed page 248) and facing English leaf p264 (printed page 249) conclude the Sunday of the Holy Forefathers with the Divine Liturgy propers (§ 3), prescribing the Epistle Colossians 3:4–11 (the renewal of the new man in Christ) and the Gospel of the Great Banquet (Luke 14:16–24, the call of the Gentiles to the Messianic feast following the excuses of those invited). The text then presents the feast of our Father among the Saints Spyridon the Wonderworker, Bishop of Trimythous in Cyprus (ca. 270–348), one of the 318 Holy Fathers of the First Ecumenical Council of Nicaea (325). When December 12 falls on a weekday (§ 1), the service is sung according to the Menaion. When it coincides with the Sunday of the Holy Forefathers (§§ 2–3), a complex rubrical integration is executed: Saturday Great Vespers assigns 4 Resurrection Stichira, 3 of the Forefathers, and 3 of Saint Spyridon at "Lord, I have cried", followed at "Glory" by the Doxastikon of the Forefathers (*Τοὺς πρὸ τοῦ νόμου Πατέρας*) and at "Both now and ever" by the First Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone. Violakis notes that while the Great Church Typikon omits Old Testament lections for this concurrence, the printed Menaia recommend chanting the three readings of Saint Nicholas (from Dec 6). At the Aposticha, the Resurrection Stichira conclude at "Glory" with the Saint's Doxastikon *Ἱεραρχῶν τὸ θεῖον κειμήλιον* ("The divine treasure of hierarchs") and at "Both now and ever" with *Τῶν Προπατόρων τὸ σύστημα* ("The assembly of the Forefathers"). At Sunday Matins (§ 3), the daily Psalter is joined with the Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX), and the Kathismata are distributed across three stichologiai (two of the Octoechos and the third of the Saint); three canons are chanted (Resurrection, Forefathers, and Saint Spyridon); after Ode 3, the Hypakoe *Εἰς δρόσον τοῖς παισὶ* is followed by the Resurrection Kontakion and Ikos (recited plain); after Ode 6, the Kontakion and Ikos of the Forefathers are sung with the dual Synaxarion; and the Katavasiai are *Χριστὸς γεννᾶται*. Leaf p264 terminates mid-sentence at "that of the...", which resolves on physical leaf p265.

[^192]: Feast of Saint Spyridon Concluded (Violakis, pp. 250–251 § 4), Commemorations of Saint Eleutherios (Dec 15), the Prophet Daniel and the Three Holy Youths (Dec 17), and Forefeast of the Nativity with Saint Ignatius the God-bearer (Dec 20 § 1). Physical leaf p265 (printed page 250) and facing English leaf p266 (printed page 251) complete the Praises and Divine Liturgy of Saint Spyridon concurring with the Sunday of the Forefathers (§ 4), assigning 4 Beatitudes to the Tone and 4 to Ode 6 of the Forefathers' Canon, the Epistle of the Hierarch (2 Timothy 1:8–18) and the Gospel of the Forefathers (Luke 14:16–24). The Typikon then provides concise directives for three subsequent mid-December commemorations: (1) Saint Eleutherios, Hieromartyr Bishop of Illyricum (December 15), whose service on a weekday follows the Menaion, and on the Sunday of the Forefathers follows the exact template of Saint Spyridon (§§ 2–4); (2) The Holy Prophet Daniel and the Three Holy Youths Ananias, Azarias, and Misael (December 17), where the Typikon formulates a major liturgical principle: when December 17 coincides with the Sunday of the Forefathers, their independent Menaion service is completely omitted because Daniel and the Three Children are already explicitly numbered and commemorated within the primary Choir of the Forefathers celebrated on this Sunday; and (3) Saint Ignatius the God-bearer, Bishop of Antioch (December 20), which opens the five-day Forefeast period of the Nativity of Christ. On a weekday (§ 1), the Forefeast and the Saint's office are combined according to the Menaion. If December 20 falls on the Sunday before the Nativity, paragraph 2 begins detailing the transfer of the Saint's service to Saturday, December 19, breaking off mid-sentence at "transferred to the...", which resolves on physical leaf p267.

[^193]: Transfer of Saint Ignatius the God-bearer (December 20, §§ 2–3) and the Sunday before the Nativity of Christ (The Holy Fathers, Case 1, § 1). Physical leaf p267 (printed page 252) and facing English leaf p268 (printed page 253) detail the rubrics when December 20 coincides with the Sunday before the Nativity of Christ (the Sunday of the Holy Fathers). In this occurrence, the commemoration of Hieromartyr Ignatius is transferred and sung in advance on Saturday, December 19, combined with the commemoration of the Martyr Boniface of Tarsus. On Friday evening at Great Vespers (§ 2), 3 Prosomoia of Saint Ignatius and 3 of Saint Boniface are chanted at "Lord, I have cried", followed at "Glory" by *Θεοφόρε Ἰγνάτιε* ("God-bearing Ignatius") and at "Both now and ever" by *Ὁ Βασιλεὺς τῶν οὐρανῶν* ("The King of heaven"). Here Violakis inserts an important rubrical commentary in parenthetical text, observing that *Ὁ Βασιλεὺς τῶν οὐρανῶν* is the First Dogmatic Theotokion of Tone Plagal 4 (Tone 8) from Saturday Great Vespers, and highlighting the liturgical anomaly of why this specific Tone 8 Theotokion was prescribed in the Constantinopolitan tradition rather than the Theotokion of the Tone of the current week (as mandated by the General Rubrics in Protheoria § 8). On Saturday morning (§ 3), Matins and the Eucharistic Liturgy of the Saturday before Nativity are celebrated with the propers of Saint Ignatius and Saint Boniface (Galatians 3:8–12 and Luke 13:19–29). The text then introduces the Sunday before the Nativity of Christ (the Sunday of the Holy Fathers, celebrated between December 18 and 24, focusing on the genealogy of Christ and the Old Testament righteous), articulating Case 1 (§ 1) for when this Sunday falls early on December 18 or 19. Leaf p268 concludes mid-sentence at "Glory"..., resolving on physical leaf p269.

[^194]: Sunday before the Nativity of Christ (The Holy Fathers, Case 1, §§ 2–3 concluded). Physical leaf p269 (printed page 254) and facing English leaf p270 (printed page 255) conclude Case 1 of the Sunday before the Nativity of Christ (when occurring on December 18 or 19). At Saturday Great Vespers (§ 1 concl.), the Doxastikon at "Lord, I have cried" is the idiomelic hymn in Tone 6 *Δανιὴλ ἀνὴρ ἐπιθυμιῶν* ("Daniel, a man of desires"), three Old Testament composite readings are read (Genesis 14:14–20, Melchizedek blessing Abram; Deuteronomy 1:8–11, 15–17, Moses appointing judges; and Deuteronomy 10:14–21, the majesty of God and circumcision of the heart), and the Dismissal Troparion in Tone 2 is *Μεγάλα τὰ τῆς πίστεως κατορθώματα* ("Great are the accomplishments of faith"). At Sunday Matins (§ 2), after the Polyeleos, the canon is the renowned Menaion composition *Κύματι θαλάσσης* ("By the wave of the sea"), borrowing the incipit and acrostic of the Great Saturday Canon. Violakis specifies a rare stichological directive: before the first Troparion following each Heirmos, the singers chant the Resurrection verse *Δόξα τῇ ἁγίᾳ Ἀναστάσει σου, Κύριε* ("Glory to Thy holy Resurrection, O Lord"), while before all subsequent Troparia they chant the hagiographical verse *Ἅγιοι τοῦ Θεοῦ, πρεσβεύσατε ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν* ("Saints of God, intercede for us"), concluding with "Glory" and "Both now and ever". After Ode 3, the Hypakoe is *Ἄγγελος Παίδων ἐδρόσισε κάμινον* ("An Angel cooled the furnace of the Children"); after Ode 6, the Kontakion and Ikos of the Fathers are sung; and at the Praises, 4 Resurrection Stichira are joined with 4 of the Fathers, concluding at "Glory" with *Τῶν νομικῶν διδαγμάτων ὁ σύλλογος* ("The assembly of legal teachings"). At the Divine Liturgy (§ 3), the Epistle is Hebrews 11:9–10, 32–40 (the cosmic catalogue of the faith of the Old Testament Saints, Abraham dwelling in tents, Gideon, Barak, Samson, Jephthae, David, Samuel, and the Prophets who through faith subdued kingdoms and stopped the mouths of lions) and the Gospel is Matthew 1:1–25 (the historical and theological Genealogy of Jesus Christ from Abraham through forty-two generations, culminating in the Virginal Conception and Birth of the Incarnate Word).


## Cohort 28 Footnotes

[^195]: Sunday before the Nativity of Christ (The Holy Fathers, Case 2: December 20–23). Physical leaf p271 (printed page 256) and facing English leaf p272 (printed page 257) set forth the liturgical rubrics when the Sunday before the Nativity of Christ occurs between December 20 and 23. In this occurrence, the dominical Resurrection office is combined with both the Fathers and the Forefeast of the Nativity (which commences on December 20). At Saturday Great Vespers (§ 4), at "Lord, I have cried", 4 Resurrection Stichira are joined with 3 Forefeast Prosomoia (*Παρθένε πανάμωμε Θεοῦ* / "O all-blameless Virgin of God") and 3 of the Fathers (*Τὰ τῶν Πατέρων σήμερον πιστοί* / "Today, ye faithful, let us celebrate the memory of the Fathers"), concluding at "Glory" with the Doxastikon in Tone 6 *Δανιὴλ ἀνὴρ ἐπιθυμιῶν* ("Daniel, a man of desires") and at "Both now and ever" with the First Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone; three Old Testament composite readings of the Fathers are read (Genesis 14:14–20; Deuteronomy 1:8–11, 15–17; Deuteronomy 10:14–21); at the Aposticha, the Resurrection Stichira conclude at "Glory" with *Χαίρετε, προφῆται τίμιοι* ("Rejoice, ye honored Prophets") and at "Both now and ever" with the Forefeast Theotokion *Ἰδοὺ καιρὸς ἤγγικε* ("Behold, the time is at hand"); and the Dismissal Troparia combine the Resurrection, the Fathers (*Μεγάλα τὰ τῆς πίστεως κατορθώματα*), and the Forefeast (*Ἑτοιμάζου Βηθλεέμ*). At Sunday Matins (§ 5), after Psalm 50 [LXX], the Triadic Canon is followed at the Lite by "Glory": *Βολίδες ἀστράπτοντες* ("Flashing like lightning bolts") and "Both now and ever": *Ἄκουε οὐρανέ* ("Hear, O heaven"); at the Kathismata following the Polyeleos, two Resurrection Kathismata are joined with one Forefeast Kathisma (*Τῶν προφητῶν αἱ ῥήσεις* / "The sayings of the Prophets") at the first stichologia, and two Resurrection with another Forefeast Kathisma (*Αὐλῶν ποιμενικῶν* / "While shepherds were piping") at the second stichologia; the Canon is the Menaion Canon of the Fathers (*Κύματι θαλάσσης*); after Ode 3, the Hypakoe *Ἄγγελος Παίδων ἐδρόσισε κάμινον* and Resurrection Kontakion are recited; after Ode 6, the Forefeast Kontakion *Εὐφραίνου Βηθλεέμ* ("Be glad, O Bethlehem") and Ikos *Τῆς σῆς, Παρθένε, κυήσεως* ("Of Thy conception, O Virgin") are chanted with the dual Synaxarion; and at the Praises, 4 Resurrection Stichira are combined with 4 of the Fathers. Leaf p272 concludes mid-sentence at § 6 ("After the Little Entrance,"), resolving on physical leaf p273.

[^196]: Sunday before the Nativity of Christ (Case 2 Concluded & Case 3: December 24 Eve Concurrence). Physical leaf p273 (printed page 258) and facing English leaf p274 (printed page 259) complete the Eucharistic Liturgy of Case 2 (§ 6), specifying the entrance Apolytikia (Resurrection, Forefeast, Fathers, Temple Patron), the Kontakion *Ἡ Παρθένος σήμερον* ("Today the Virgin cometh unto the cave"), and the appointed Epistle (Hebrews 11:9–10, 32–40) and Gospel (Matthew 1:1–25). The text then articulates Case 3 (§§ 7–8): when the Sunday before the Nativity falls on December 24 itself (the Paramony / Eve of the Nativity). Here Violakis formulates a crucial liturgical exception: because December 24 is the final and most intensive day of the Forefeast, the dominical Resurrection hymns are entirely suppressed at Saturday Great Vespers (§ 7), and the service is sung exclusively from the Menaion for the Fathers and the Forefeast. At "Lord, I have cried", 4 Prosomoia of the Fathers (*Τὰ τῶν Πατέρων σήμερον πιστοί*) are combined with 4 of the Forefeast (*Προεορτάσωμεν λαοὶ Χριστοῦ τὰ γενέθλια* / "Let us celebrate beforehand, O ye peoples, the Nativity of Christ", transferred from Dec 20); at "Glory": *Δανιὴλ ἀνὴρ ἐπιθυμιῶν*; at "Both now and ever": *Σπήλαιον εὐτρεπίζου* ("Prepare thyself, O cave"); at the Aposticha, 3 Forefeast Prosomoia (*Παρθένε πανάμωμε Θεοῦ*) are chanted with Habakkuk psalm verses (*Ὁ Θεὸς ἀπὸ Θαιμὰν ἥξει* / "God shall come from Teman" and *Κύριε, εἰσακήκοα τὴν ἀκοήν σου* / "O Lord, I have heard Thy report"); and the Dismissal Troparia are those of the Fathers (*Μεγάλα τὰ τῆς πίστεως κατορθώματα*) and the Forefeast (*Ἀπεγράφετό ποτε* / "Mary once, with aged Joseph, went to be enrolled"). At Sunday Matins (§ 8), instead of the Triadic Canon after Psalm 50 [LXX], the Canon *Κύματι θαλάσσης* appointed after Vespers of the 24th is sung; at "God is the Lord", the Resurrection Dismissal Troparion is restored alongside those of the Fathers and Forefeast; and the Kathismata integrate the Resurrection with Forefeast hymnography. Leaf p274 concludes mid-sentence at "then the Litany and the Hypakoe of the Tone, the...", resolving on physical leaf p275.

[^197]: Sunday before the Nativity Concluded (Case 3, §§ 8–9) and Feast of the Nativity of Christ (December 25, Case 1: Friday Occurrence, §§ 1–2). Physical leaf p275 (printed page 260) and facing English leaf p276 (printed page 261) complete Case 3 of the Sunday before the Nativity. At Sunday Matins (§ 8 concl.), two canons are combined: the Canon of the Fathers (*Κύματι θαλάσσης*) and the Forefeast Canon (*Χριστὸν σαρκὶ νηπιάσαντα* / "Christ being an infant in the flesh"); after Ode 3, the Hypakoe *Ἄγγελος Παίδων ἐδρόσισε κάμινον* is recited; after Ode 6, the Kontakion of the Three Children (*Χειρόγραφον εἰκόνα μὴ σεβασθέντες* / "Refusing to worship the graven image") and Ikos (*Ἔκτεινόν σου τὴν χεῖρα* / "Stretch out Thy hand") are chanted; and at the Praises, 4 Resurrection Stichira are joined with 4 Troparia of the Fathers (*Ἆρόν σου τὴν φωνὴν ἀληθῶς* / "In truth lift up thy voice, O Zion"). In the Divine Liturgy (§ 9), the Beatitudes combine Troparia from Ode 3 of the Fathers' Canon and Ode 6 of the Forefeast Canon. The Typikon then opens the solemn Feast of the Nativity according to the Flesh of our Lord, God, and Savior Jesus Christ (December 25). Case 1 (§ 1) governs when the Feast of the Nativity occurs on a Friday. In this occurrence, Thursday (December 24) is the Paramony (Eve): in the morning, following daily Matins, the Great Hours (Royal Hours: First, Third, Sixth, and Ninth) are chanted, immediately followed by Great Vespers joined with the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great. Leaf p276 concludes mid-sentence at § 2 ("Following the service of Matins, around the First Hour of the morning [7:00 a.m.],..."), resolving on physical leaf p277.

[^198]: Feast of the Nativity of Christ: The Royal Hours (First Hour Concluded & Third Hour, §§ 2–3). Physical leaf p277 (printed page 262) and facing English leaf p278 (printed page 263) provide the ceremonial ordotikon for the First and Third Royal Hours of the Nativity Eve when the Feast falls on Friday. At the First Hour (§ 2 concl.), the Patriarch or presiding Hierarch descends into the nave and begins the opening blessing (*Δόξα σοι, ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν... Βασιλεῦ Οὐράνιε...*); three special psalms are read (Psalms 5, 44, and 45 LXX); following the Troparion *Ἀπεγράφετό ποτε* and Theotokion, three idiomela are chanted antiphonally by the two choirs (*Βηθλεὲμ ἑτοιμάζου*, *Νῦν προφητικὴ πρόρρησις*, and *Τάδε λέγει Ἰωσήφ*); the First Canonarch reads the Prophecy (Micah 5:2–4, prophesying the birth in Bethlehem Ephrathah) and the Epistle (Hebrews 1:1–12, proclaiming the divine Sonship of Christ superior to the angels); and the Priest proclaims the Holy Gospel (Matthew 1:18–25, the Virginal Conception and birth of Emmanuel) before the Holy Doors. In an important rubrical note, Violakis specifies that during the chanting of the idiomela of the Third Hour, the Great Archdeacon (or Deacon, or Priest who read the Gospel) comes forth from the North Door dressed in a black mandyas, bearing a katzion (hand-censer with bells) preceded by a taper-bearer (Lambadouchos), and censes the people, a ceremony repeated at the Sixth and Ninth Hours by other deacons (in the absence of a Hierarch, the Priest censes robed in a phelonion). At the Third Hour (§ 3), following the psalms (Psalms 28, 66, and 50 LXX) and the Troparion of the Forefeast, the choirs chant the idiomela *Οὗτος ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν* ("This is our God"), *Πρὸ τῆς γεννήσεως τῆς σῆς* ("Before Thy birth, O Lord"), and *Ἰωσὴφ εἰπὲ ἡμῖν* ("Tell us, Joseph"). Leaf p278 breaks off mid-sentence at "Then the...", resolving on physical leaf p279.

[^199]: Feast of the Nativity of Christ: The Royal Hours Concluded (Third Hour Concl., Sixth and Ninth Hours, §§ 3–5). Physical leaf p279 (printed page 264) and facing English leaf p280 (printed page 265) conclude the Royal Hours of the Nativity. At the Third Hour (§ 3 concl.), the Second Canonarch proclaims the Prophecy (Baruch 3:35–4:4, Christ appearing upon earth and conversing with men) and the Epistle (Galatians 3:23–29, the Law as tutor unto Christ, and adoption as sons through faith), and the Priest reads the Gospel (Luke 2:1–20, the Caesar Augustus census, the manger, and the Angelic Gloria heard by the shepherds). At the Sixth Hour (§ 4), after Psalms 71, 131, and 90 LXX, the idiomela are *Δεῦτε πιστοὶ ἐπαρθῶμεν ἐνθέως* ("Come, ye faithful, let us be inspired divinely"), *Ἄκουε, οὐρανέ* ("Hear, O heaven, and give ear, O earth"), and *Δεῦτε, χριστοφόροι λαοί* ("Come, ye Christ-bearing peoples"); the Prophecy is Isaiah 7:10–16; 8:1–4, 9–10 (the virgin conceiving Emmanuel), the Epistle is Hebrews 2:11–18 (Christ partaking of flesh and blood to destroy the devil), and the Gospel is Matthew 2:1–12 (the Adoration of the Magi). At the Ninth Hour (§ 5), after Psalms 109, 110, and 85 LXX, the idiomela are *Ἐξεπλήττετο ὁ Ἡρώδης* ("Herod was struck with amazement") and *Ὅτε Ἰωσήφ, Παρθένε* ("When Joseph, O Virgin"); then the Canonarch stands in the midst of the nave facing east and solemnly chants the celebrated idiomelon *Σήμερον γεννᾶται ἐκ Παρθένου ὁ δρακὶ τὴν πᾶσαν ἔχων κτίσιν* ("Today He Who holds all creation in the hollow of His hand is born of a Virgin") in Gospel recitative style thrice, continuing with the strophes, repeating thrice *Προσκυνοῦμέν σου τὴν γέννησιν, Χριστέ* ("We worship Thy Nativity, O Christ"), and once *Δεῖξον ἡμῖν καὶ τὰ θεῖά σου Θεοφάνεια* ("Show us also Thy divine Theophany"), whereupon the choirs repeat the idiomelon melodiously; the Prophecy is Isaiah 9:6–7 (Unto us a Child is born), the Epistle is Hebrews 2:1–10 (Jesus crowned with glory and honor), and the Gospel is Matthew 2:13–23 (the Flight into Egypt and Slaughter of the Holy Innocents). In Note 3, Violakis explains that historically the Typika were read after the Ninth Hour, but in modern practice are omitted to transition directly to Great Vespers with the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great.

Following the Ninth Hour, Great Vespers opens with the Priest in phelonion blessing: *Εὐλογημένη ἡ Βασιλεία...* ("Blessed is the Kingdom..."); after the Prooemiac Psalm [Psalm 103 LXX] and Great Synapte, at "Lord, I have cried" are chanted 8 Stichira Idiomela in Tone 2 (*Δεῦτε ἀγαλλιασώμεθα τῷ Κυρίῳ*, *Τὰ κατ' εἰκόνα καὶ καθ' ὁμοίωσιν*, etc.), repeating the first two to make 8, concluding at "Glory... Both now and ever" with *Αὐγούστου μοναρχήσαντος ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς* ("When Augustus reigned alone upon the earth"). Matins (§ 7) opens at the early dawn; at "God is the Lord", the Troparion of the Feast is chanted thrice.

[^201]: Feast of the Nativity of Christ: Matins Concluded (§ 7 concl.). Physical leaf p283 (printed page 268) and facing English leaf p284 (printed page 269) continue the Festal Matins. Following the Kathismata (*Δεῦτε ἴδωμεν πιστοί* / "Come, ye faithful, let us see" and *Τί θαυμάζεις Μαριάμ* / "Why art thou amazed, O Mary"), the Polyeleos (Psalms 134 and 135 LXX) is chanted, followed by the Kathisma *Ὁ ἀχώρητος παντί* ("He Whom naught can contain"). The First Antiphon of the Anavathmoi in Tone 4 (*Ἐκ νεότητός μου* / "From my youth") is followed by the Prokeimenon in Tone 4: *Ἐκ γαστρὸς πρὸ ἑωσφόρου ἐγέννησά σε* ("From the womb before the morning star have I begotten Thee", Psalm 109:3 LXX); the Festal Gospel is Matthew 1:18–25; and after Psalm 50 [LXX] the Idiomelon *Δόξα ἐν ὑψίστοις Θεῷ* ("Glory to God in the highest") is chanted. At Ode 9, the Megalynaria are chanted (*Μεγάλυνον, ψυχή μου, τὴν τιμιωτέραν... Μυστήριον ξένον* / "Magnify, O my soul, her that is more honorable... A strange and most glorious mystery"); followed by the Exapostilarion *Ἐπεσκέψατο ἡμᾶς* ("The Dayspring from on high hath visited us") thrice. At the Praises, 4 Stichira Idiomela (*Εὐφράνθητε δίκαιοι*, *Ὁ Πατὴρ εὐδόκησεν*, etc.) are sung.

[^202]: Feast of the Nativity of Christ: Praises Concluded and Divine Liturgy Antiphons (§§ 7 concl., 8). Physical leaf p285 (printed page 270) and facing English leaf p286 (printed page 271) conclude the Praises with the Doxastikon in Tone 2 (*Ὅτε καιρὸς τῆς ἐπὶ γῆς παρουσίας σου* / "When the time of Thy coming upon earth") and "Both now and ever": *Σήμερον ὁ Χριστὸς ἐν Βηθλεὲμ γεννᾶται ἐκ Παρθένου* ("Today Christ is born of a Virgin in Bethlehem"), transitioning into the Great Doxology and the Apolytikion of the Feast (*Ἡ γέννησίς σου, Χριστὲ ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν*). [^203]: Feast of the Nativity Concluded (Liturgy & Dodekahemeron Fasting Rule, § 8 concl.) and Feast of the Synaxis of the Most Holy Theotokos (December 26, §§ 9–10). Note 6 sets forth the canonical fast dispensation of the Dodekahemeron (Twelve Days): from December 25 through January 4, there is a total fast dispensation for all foods on all days, including Wednesday and Friday. The text then introduces the Synaxis of the Most Holy Theotokos (December 26, §§ 9–10): at Great Vespers (§ 9), at "Lord, I have cried", 6 Stichira are chanted (3 of the Nativity and 3 of the Synaxis), concluding with the Great Prokeimenon in Tone 7 (*Τίς Θεὸς μέγας ὡς ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν* / "Who is so great a god as our God?", Psalm 76:14 LXX). [^204]: Synaxis of the Most Holy Theotokos Concluded (Matins & Liturgy, §§ 10 concl., 11) and Sunday after the Nativity of Christ with Saint Stephen the Protomartyr (December 26/27, § 12). Paragraph 12 then articulates the rubrics for when the Sunday after the Nativity of Christ falls on December 26 or 27, commemorating the Holy and Righteous Ancestors of God (David the King, Joseph the Betrothed, and James the Brother of the Lord) together with Saint Stephen the Protomartyr and Archdeacon.

At the Divine Liturgy (§ 20), Festal Antiphons are sung with refrain *ὁ ἐκ Παρθένου τεχθείς* ("Who wast born of a Virgin"); the Entrance Hymn is "Come, let us worship... Who art risen from the dead"; the Baptismal hymn *Ὅσοι εἰς Χριστόν* ("As many of you as have been baptized into Christ", Galatians 3:27) replaces the Trisagion; the Epistle (Galatians 1:11–19) and Gospel (Matthew 2:13–23) are of the Sunday after Nativity; Megalynarion is the Heirmos of Ode 9 (*Στέργειν μὲν ἡμᾶς*); Communion Verse is Psalm 110:9 LXX (*Λύτρωσιν ἀπέστειλε* / "The Lord hath sent redemption unto His people"). Paragraph 21 assigns the Apodosis to Friday, December 31. Paragraph 22 introduces Situation Three (Γ΄), where December 25 falls on Sunday: on Friday morning, the Royal Hours alone are sung without Divine Liturgy, using the Troparion *Ἑτοιμάζου, Βηθλεέμ* ("Prepare, Bethlehem"), since *Ἀπεγράφετό ποτε* ("Mary was registered once in a census") is reserved for the immediate Paramony.

[^208]: Situation Three Continued: Friday Evening Forefeast Vespers (§ 23); Saturday Paramony Matins and Liturgy (§ 24); Saturday Evening Great Festal Vespers (§ 25); Sunday Festal Matins and Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great (§ 26); Transfer of Sunday after the Nativity to Monday (§ 26 note). Physical leaf p297 (printed page 282) and facing English leaf p298 (printed page 283) continue Situation Three (Nativity on Sunday). On Friday evening (§ 23), daily Vespers is sung from the Menaion, followed after *Νῦν ἀπολύεις* by the Forefeast Canon (*Κύματι θαλάσσης* / "Waves of the sea") and the Forefeast Dismissal Troparion *Ἀπεγράφετό ποτε* ("Mary was registered once in a census"). On Saturday morning (§ 24), Matins is sung with Canons read plain, Heirmos of Ode 8 chanted, Magnificat (*Τὴν τιμιωτέραν*), Forefeast Exapostilaria, and Praises (*Ὦ τῶν ὑπὲρ νοῦν* / "What mysteries beyond understanding"); Divine Liturgy features the daily Antiphons (*Ἀγαθὸν τὸ ἐξομολογεῖσθαι* / "It is good to give praise unto the Lord", Psalm 91:2 LXX), Entrance Hymn *ὁ ἐν ἁγίοις θαυμαστός* ("Who art wondrous in Thy saints"), Forefeast Dismissal Troparion, Epistle and Gospel of the Saturday before the Nativity, and Communion Verse Psalm 64:5 LXX (*Μακάριοι οὓς ἐξελέξω* / "Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen"). On Saturday evening (§ 25), Great Festal Vespers of the Nativity is chanted with 6 Stichira Idiomela (*Δεῦτε ἀγαλλιασώμεθα*), Gospel Entrance, 8 Old Testament Readings, and immediately following the 8th reading (without the Litany) the Epistle and Gospel are proclaimed; then the Augmented Litany, Aposticha Idiomela (*Μέγα καὶ παράδοξον θαῦμα*), and Festal Dismissal Troparion thrice. On Sunday (§ 26), Festal Matins and the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great are celebrated as in Situation One. The concluding note explains that because the solitary Sunday between Nativity and Theophany coincides with January 1 (Circumcision of Christ and Saint Basil the Great), the commemoration of the Sunday after the Nativity (the Holy Ancestors of God) is transferred and joined to the Synaxis of the Theotokos on Monday, December 26.

[^209]: Combined Celebration of the Synaxis of the Most Holy Theotokos and Sunday after the Nativity (Ancestors of God) on Monday, December 26 (§§ 27–29). Physical leaf p299 (printed page 284) and facing English leaf p300 (printed page 285) provide the complete ordotikon for the joint celebration on Monday, December 26, when Nativity falls on Sunday. On Sunday evening (§ 27), Vespers features 6 Stichira at "Lord, I have cried" (3 Festal Idiomela *Δεῦτε ἀγαλλιασώμεθα* and 3 of the Ancestors of God *Δεῦτε συμφώνως ἅπαντες Δαυῒδ τὸν ἀοίδιμον* / "Come, let us extol David the king"), Doxastikon of the Ancestors (*Μνήμην ἐπιτελοῦμεν*), Theotokion (*Δόξα ἐν ὑψίστοις Θεῷ*), Entrance with Great Prokeimenon in Tone 7 (*Τίς Θεὸς μέγας*, Psalm 76:14 LXX), Festal Aposticha (*Παράδοξον μυστήριον*), and Dismissal Troparia of the Nativity and the Ancestors (*Εὐαγγελίζου, Ἰωσήφ*). At Monday Matins (§ 28), following the Hexapsalmos, "God is the Lord", and Festal Kathismata (*Δεῦτε ἴδωμεν, πιστοί* and *Τί θαυμάζεις, Μαριάμ;*), Psalm 50 [LXX] is read plain without Polyeleos; Canons combine the Nativity First Canon (*Χριστὸς γεννᾶται*) and that of the Betrothed (*Χριστοῦ θεράπων μακάριε*); after Ode 3, Kathismata *Πατρὸς ὡς ἀληθῶς* and *Χορὸς τῶν Προφητῶν*; after Ode 6, Kontakion, Ikos, and Synaxarion of the Ancestors; Ode 9 is chanted with the Festal Megalynaria and Katavasia *Στέργειν μὲν ἡμᾶς*; Exapostilaria of the Ancestors (*Σὺν Ἰακώβῳ μέλψωμεν*) and the Feast (*Ἐπεσκέψατο ἡμᾶς*); Praises with 4 Festal Stichira (*Εὐφραίνεσθε, δίκαιοι*), Doxastikon (*Αἷμα καὶ πῦρ*), and Great Doxology. At the Divine Liturgy (§ 29), the Festal Antiphons are sung as on the feast; Entrance Verse is Psalm 109:3 LXX (*Ἐκ γαστρὸς πρὸ Ἑωσφόρου*); Dismissal Troparia of the Nativity, the Ancestors, and Kontakion *Ἡ Παρθένος σήμερον τὸν ὑπερούσιον τίκτει*; Baptismal hymn *Ὅσοι εἰς Χριστόν* replaces the Trisagion; Epistle and Gospel are of the Sunday after the Nativity; Megalynarion is the Heirmos *Στέργειν μὲν ἡμᾶς*; and Communion Verse is Psalm 110:9 LXX (*Λύτρωσιν ἀπέστειλε* / "The Lord hath sent redemption unto His people").


## Cohort 31 Footnotes

[^210]: Conclusion of Situation Three (Apodosis date, § 30) and Situation Four: Nativity falling on Monday (§§ 31–35). Physical leaf p301 (printed page 286) and facing English leaf p302 (printed page 287) conclude the rubrics for Situation Three (Γ΄), where December 25 falls on a Sunday. Paragraph 30 assigns the Apodosis of the Feast to the following Saturday. The text notes the print erratum "ἤτοι τῇ Λ΄ τοῦ μηνός" (that is, on the 30th of the month), which is bracketed and corrected to "ὀρθότερον εἶναι τῇ ΛΑ΄ τοῦ μηνός" (more correctly, on the 31st of the month), as December contains 31 days. On this Saturday, the entire festal office is repeated as in Situation One § 15, with the Epistle (1 Timothy 3:13–4:5) and Gospel (Matthew 3:1–11) of the Saturday before Theophany. Situation Four (Δ΄) details the order when December 25 falls on a Monday: on Friday morning (Dec 22, § 31), following Matins, the Royal Hours alone are sung without the Divine Liturgy, following Situation One §§ 1–5; the Dismissal Troparion is *Ἑτοιμάζου, Βηθλεέμ* ("Prepare, Bethlehem"), because the Paramony is separated by the weekend and not immediately adjacent to the Feast (referencing Situation Three § 22). On Friday evening and Saturday morning (Dec 22–23, § 32), the Service of the Ten Holy Martyrs of Crete (Dec 23) is combined with the Forefeast from the Menaion; Dismissal Troparion *Ἑτοιμάζου, Βηθλεέμ*, Kontakion *Ἡ Παρθένος σήμερον τὸν προαιώνιον Λόγον*; Divine Liturgy includes the Epistle (Galatians 3:8–12) and Gospel (Luke 13:19–29) of the Saturday before the Nativity, and Communion Verse Psalm 64:5 LXX (*Μακάριοι οὓς ἐξελέξω* / "Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen"). On Saturday evening and Sunday morning (Dec 23–24, § 33), the Sunday before the Nativity (Holy Ancestors) is celebrated as in Situation Three §§ 7–9. On Sunday evening (Dec 24, § 34), Great Festal Vespers is chanted as in Situation Three § 25. On Monday (Dec 25, § 35), Festal Matins is sung as in Situation One §§ 7–8, together with the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great.

[^211]: Situation Four Continued: Synaxis of the Most Holy Theotokos (§ 36) and Celebration of the Sunday after the Nativity combined with the Apodosis of the Feast (§§ 37–38). Physical leaf p303 (printed page 288) and facing English leaf p304 (printed page 289) continue Situation Four (Nativity on Monday). Paragraphs 37–38 govern the case where the Sunday after the Nativity (Holy Ancestors: David the King, Joseph the Betrothed, and James the Brother of the Lord) coincides with the Apodosis of the Feast on Sunday, December 31. On Saturday evening (§ 37), Great Vespers at "Lord, I have cried" features 10 Stichira (3 Resurrectional, 4 Festal Idiomela *Δεῦτε ἀγαλλιασώμεθα*, and 3 of the Ancestors of God); "Glory" (*Μνήμην ἐπιτελοῦμεν*); "Both now and ever" (*Αὐγούστου μοναρχήσαντος*); Entrance, *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, Prokeimenon; Aposticha Resurrectional with "Glory" of the Ancestors (*Ἱερέων μνήμη*), "Both now and ever" (*Σπηλαίῳ παρῴκησας*); Dismissal Troparia: Resurrectional, of the Ancestors (*Εὐαγγελίζου, Ἰωσήφ*), and of the Feast (*Ἡ γέννησίς σου*). On Sunday Matins (§ 38), after Psalm 50 [LXX], the Triadic Canon and Triadic hymns (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*) are sung, followed by the Trisagion Prayers, Troparion *Ἡ γέννησίς σου*, and Hexapsalmos; "God is the Lord", Troparia as at Vespers; Psalter and Amomos (Kathisma 17 / Psalm 118 LXX); Resurrectional Kathismata with Festal Kathismata instead of Theotokia; Evlogitaria, Hypakoe, Anavathmoi of the Tone, Prokeimenon; Canons of the Resurrection, of the Feast (*Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν*), and of Joseph the Betrothed (*Χριστοῦ θεράπων μακάριε*); after Ode 3, Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos plain, Kathisma *Πατρὸς ὡς ἀληθῶς*, "Both now and ever" *Χορὸς τῶν Προφητῶν*; after Ode 6, Kontakion and Ikos of the Sunday after the Nativity, Menologion and Synaxarion; double Katavasiai of the Nativity (*Ἔσωσε λαόν* and *Στείβει θαλάσσης*); Morning Gospel order; Ode 9 from both festal canons with their Megalynaria and both Katavasiai of Ode 9; Exapostilaria of the Resurrection, Ancestors, and Feast; Praises with 4 Resurrectional and 4 Festal Stichira; "Glory" (*Αἷμα καὶ πῦρ*), "Both now and ever" (*Ὑπερευλογημένη*), and Great Doxology.

[^212]: Divine Liturgy of the Sunday after the Nativity and Apodosis (§ 39); Situation Five: Nativity falling on Tuesday, Wednesday, or Thursday (§ 40); and Sunday after the Nativity during the Afterfeast (Saturday Vespers § 41 and Sunday Matins § 42). Physical leaf p305 (printed page 290) and facing English leaf p306 (printed page 291) conclude the Liturgy for Situation Four and open Situation Five (Ε΄). Situation Five (§ 40) governs Christmas occurring on Tuesday, Wednesday, or Thursday: the Paramony Royal Hours, Great Vespers, and Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great, as well as the festal services and the Synaxis of the Theotokos, follow Situation One §§ 1–11. Paragraphs 41–42 detail the celebration of the Sunday after the Nativity when it occurs on an intermediate day of the afterfeast (between Dec 26 and 31). On Saturday evening (§ 41), Great Vespers at "Lord, I have cried" has 10 Stichira (4 Resurrectional, 3 Festal Idiomela *Δεῦτε ἀγαλλιασώμεθα*, 3 of the Ancestors of God); "Glory" (*Μνήμην ἐπιτελοῦμεν*); "Both now and ever": First Theotokion Dogmatikon of the Tone (distinct from Situation Four, where the festal Doxastikon is sung); Entrance, *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, Prokeimenon; Aposticha Resurrectional with "Glory" of the Ancestors (*Ἱερέων μνήμη*), "Both now and ever" (*Χορεύουσιν Ἄγγελοι*); Dismissal Troparia: Resurrectional, Ancestors (*Εὐαγγελίζου, Ἰωσήφ*), Festal (*Ἡ γέννησίς σου*), Dismissal. On Sunday Matins (§ 42), after Psalm 50 [LXX], Triadic Canon and Triadic hymns (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*), Trisagion Prayers, Troparion *Ἡ γέννησίς σου*, Hexapsalmos; "God is the Lord", Troparia as at Vespers; Psalter and Amomos; Resurrectional Kathismata with afterfeast Kathismata instead of Theotokia; Evlogitaria, Hypakoe, Anavathmoi of the Tone, Prokeimenon; Canons of the Resurrection and of Joseph the Betrothed (the festal canon being omitted here); after Ode 3, Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos plain, Kathisma *Πατρὸς ὡς ἀληθῶς*...

[^213]: Situation Five Concluded: Matins and Divine Liturgy of the Sunday after the Nativity (§§ 42 concl., 43); Month of January: January 1, Circumcision of our Lord Jesus Christ and Commemoration of Saint Basil the Great (Vespers § 1 and opening of Matins § 2). Physical leaf p307 (printed page 292) and facing English leaf p308 (printed page 293) conclude the five Situations of the Nativity and begin the Month of January. Matins of the Sunday after the Nativity under Situation Five (§ 42 concl.) concludes with Kathisma "Both now and ever" *Χορὸς τῶν Προφητῶν*; after Ode 6: Kontakion of the Sunday after the Nativity (*Εὐφροσύνης σήμερον* / "Of joy today") and Ikos (*Ἀπορρήτῳ βουλῇ* / "By ineffable counsel"), Menologion and Synaxarion; Katavasiai *Ἔσωσε λαόν* (single canon only); Morning Gospel order; Magnificat (*Τὴν τιμιωτέραν* is sung); Exapostilaria of Resurrection, Ancestors, and Feast; Praises with 4 Resurrectional and 4 Festal Stichira (*Εὐφραίνεσθε, δίκαιοι*); "Glory" (*Αἷμα καὶ πῦρ*), "Both now and ever" (*Ὑπερευλογημένη*), Great Doxology, and *Σήμερον σωτηρία*. At the Divine Liturgy (§ 43): Typika and 4 Beatitudes of the Tone, and 4 from Ode 6 of Canon of the Betrothed; Entrance with Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion, *Ἡ γέννησίς σου*, *Εὐαγγελίζου, Ἰωσήφ*, Temple Patron, Kontakion *Ἡ Παρθένος σήμερον*; Epistle Galatians 1:11–19, Gospel Matthew 2:13–23; at "Especially": *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν* (normal Megalynarion, since it is an intermediate day and not the Apodosis); Communion Verse Psalm 148:1 LXX (*Αἰνεῖτε τὸν Κύριον* / "Praise the Lord from the heavens"); *Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*, Dismissal. Formal rubric closes: *End of the five Situations of the Feast of the Nativity of Christ.* The Month of January opens on January 1 with the Circumcision of Christ and Saint Basil the Great. At Vespers (§ 1): Introductory Psalm [Ps. 103 LXX], First Stasis of the First Kathisma [Ps. 1–3 LXX]; at "Lord, I have cried", 6 Stichira (2 Festal Idiomela repeating the first, 3 Prosomoia of Saint Basil); "Glory" of the Saint (*Σοφίας ἐραστής*); "Both now and ever" (*Συγκαταβαίνων ὁ Σωτήρ*); Entrance, *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, Prokeimenon of the day, 3 Old Testament Readings (Genesis 17:1–14; Proverbs 8:22–30; Proverbs 10:31–11:12); Aposticha: 3 Idiomela of the Saint, "Glory" (*Ὁ τὴν χάριν τῶν θαυμάτων*), "Both now and ever" (*Οὐκ ἐπῃσχύνθη ὁ πανάγαθος Θεός*); Dismissal Troparion of the Saint (*Εἰς πᾶσαν τὴν γῆν*), then "Glory... Both now and ever...": of the Feast once (*Μορφὴν ἀναλλοιώτως*), and the festal Dismissal (*Ὁ ἐν τῇ ὀγδόῃ ἡμέρᾳ σαρκὶ περιτμηθῆναι καταδεξάμενος διὰ τὴν ἡμῶν σωτηρίαν Χριστὸς ὁ ἀληθινὸς Θεὸς ἡμῶν...*). At Matins (§ 2): after Psalm 50 [LXX], Lite of the Saint; Trisagion Prayers, Troparion *Μορφὴν ἀναλλοιώτως*, Hexapsalmos.

[^214]: January 1 Matins Concluded (§ 2) and Complete Festal Megalynaria of Ode 9 for the Circumcision of Christ and Saint Basil the Great. Physical leaf p309 (printed page 294) and facing English leaf p310 (printed page 295) conclude Matins for January 1. At "God is the Lord": Dismissal Troparion of Saint Basil twice, and of the Feast once; Psalter of the day and Polyeleos; Kathismata in order; Anavathmoi: First Antiphon of Tone 4 (*Ἐκ νεότητός μου*); Prokeimenon: Violakis notes *Εἰς πᾶσαν τὴν γῆν ἐξῆλθεν ὁ φθόγγος αὐτοῦ* [Ps. 18:5 LXX], adding the critical rubrical note that the Menaion and standard liturgical sources indicate *Τὸ στόμα μου λαλήσει σοφίαν* ("My mouth shall speak wisdom", Psalm 48:4 LXX); *Πᾶσα πνοή*; Morning Gospel (John 10:1–9, from November 13); Psalm 50 [LXX] plain; "Glory": *Ταῖς τοῦ Ἱεράρχου*; "Both now and ever": *Ταῖς τῆς Θεοτόκου*; Verse Psalm 50:3 LXX (*Ἐλέησόν με, ὁ Θεός*); Idiomelon *Ἐξεχύθη ἡ χάρις ἐν χείλεσί σου* (Tone 6); Canon of the Feast *Δεῦτε λαοί* with Verse *Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν*, and Canon of Saint Basil *Σοῦ τὴν φωνήν* with Verse *Ἅγιε τοῦ Θεοῦ*; after Ode 3, Kathismata of Saint Basil and the Feast; after Ode 6, Kontakion and Ikos of Saint Basil, and Menologion; double Katavasiai of Theophany (*Βυθοῦ ἀνεκάλυψε πυθμένα* and *Στείβει θαλάσσης*); Ode 9 of both Feast and Saint sung with their respective Megalynaria. The leaf provides the complete text of the Megalynaria for Ode 9: for Canon 1 (Circumcision), 5 refrains (including the Menaion addition in brackets: "Today the Master is circumcised in the flesh, fulfilling the Law as an infant"); and for Canon 2 (Saint Basil), 4 refrains (including the Menaion additions in brackets: "Magnify, O my soul, the most bright luminary of the universe" and "Magnify, O my soul, the adorner of the Church of Christ"). The footnote preserves Violakis's editorial asterisk indicating that these bracketed refrains are commonly chanted in contemporary practice.

[^215]: January 1 (Circumcision of our Lord Jesus Christ and Commemoration of Saint Basil the Great) Continued: Conclusion of Matins Ode 9 Megalynaria, Praises, Great Doxology, and Dismissal; Divine Liturgy Opening and Antiphons 1 and 2 (§ 3). Physical leaf p311 (printed page 296) and facing English leaf p312 (printed page 297) continue the festal office of January 1.
