Second Antiphon verses from Psalm 95:11 LXX (*Εὐφραινέσθωσαν οἱ οὐρανοὶ καὶ ἀγαλλιάσθω ἡ γῆ* / "Let the heavens rejoice and let the earth be glad"), Psalm 97:1 LXX (*῎Ασατε τῷ Κυρίῳ ᾆσμα καινόν* / "Sing unto the Lord a new song"), Psalm 49:2 LXX (*Ἐκ Σιὼν ἡ εὐπρέπεια τῆς ὡραιότητος αὐτοῦ* / "Out of Sion is the majesty of His beauty"), Psalm 113:11 LXX (*Ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν ἐν τῷ οὐρανῷ καὶ ἐν τῇ γῇ, πάντα ὅσα ἠθέλησεν ἐποίησεν* / "Our God is in heaven and on earth; all things whatsoever He hath willed, He hath done"); refrain: *Σῶσον ἡμᾶς Υἱὲ Θεοῦ, ὁ σαρκὶ περιτμηθείς* ("Save us, O Son of God, Who wast circumcised in the flesh, who sing to Thee: Alleluia"), followed by *Ὁ μονογενὴς Υἱὸς* ("Only-begotten Son and Immortal Word of God").

[^216]: January 1 Continued: Third Antiphon, Little Entrance, Troparia, Epistle, Gospel, Liturgy of Saint Basil (§ 3 concl.); and Celebration of the Feast when January 1 falls on a Sunday (Saturday Vespers § 4 and Sunday Matins § 5 opening). Physical leaf p313 (printed page 298) and facing English leaf p314 (printed page 299) conclude the standard weekday order and present the combined Sunday order for January 1. At the Divine Liturgy (§ 3 concl.), the Third Antiphon consists of verses from Psalm 88:2 LXX (*Τὰ ἐλέη σου, Κύριε, εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα ᾄσομαι* / "Thy mercies, O Lord, will I sing forever"), Psalm 88:3 LXX (*᾿Αναγγελῶ τὴν ἀλήθειάν σου ἐν τῷ στόματί μου* / "With my mouth will I declare Thy truth unto all generations"), Psalm 44:8 LXX (*Ἠγάπησας δικαιοσύνην καὶ ἐμίσησας ἀνομίαν* / "Thou hast loved righteousness and hated iniquity"); refrain: Dismissal Troparion of the Circumcision (*Μορφὴν ἀναλλοιώτως*). At the Little Entrance, the Introit Verse is: *Δεῦτε προσκυνήσωμεν καὶ προσπέσωμεν Χριστῷ· σῶσον ἡμᾶς Υἱὲ Θεοῦ, ὁ σαρκὶ περιτμηθείς...* ("O come, let us worship and fall down before Christ; save us, O Son of God, Who wast circumcised in the flesh..."). Dismissal Troparia: Circumcision (*Μορφὴν ἀναλλοιώτως*), Saint Basil (*Εἰς πᾶσαν τὴν γῆν*), and Temple Patron; Kontakion of the Circumcision (*Ὁ τῶν ὅλων Κύριος περιτομὴν ὑπομένει* / "The Lord of all endureth circumcision"); Trisagion Hymn; Epistle: Colossians 2:8–12 (*᾿Αδελφοί, βλέπετε μή τις ὑμᾶς ἔσται ὁ συλαγωγῶν* / "Brethren, beware lest any man cheat you"); Gospel: Luke 2:20–21, 40–52 (*Ὑπέστρεψαν οἱ ποιμένες* / "The shepherds returned, glorifying and praising God"); Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great; at the Diptychs, the Megalynarion of Saint Basil is chanted (*Τὸν οὐρανοφάντορα τοῦ Χριστοῦ μύστην τοῦ Δεσπότου* / "Let us all extol the great Basil, revealer of heavenly things, initiate of the Master"); Communion Verse Psalm 148:1 LXX (*Αἰνεῖτε τὸν Κύριον* / "Praise the Lord from the heavens"); instead of *Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*, Dismissal Troparion *Μορφὴν ἀναλλοιώτως*, and Dismissal. Paragraphs 4–5 govern the order when January 1 falls on a Sunday: on Saturday evening (§ 4), at "Lord, I have cried", 10 Stichira are sung (4 Resurrectional, 3 Festal Idiomela repeating the first, and 3 Prosomoia of Saint Basil); "Glory" of Saint Basil (*Σοφίας ἐραστής*); "Both now and ever": First Theotokion Dogmatikon of the Tone; Entrance, *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, Prokeimenon of the day, and 3 Old Testament Readings of the Feast; Aposticha: Resurrectional Stichira, "Glory" of Saint Basil (*Ὁ τὴν χάριν τῶν θαυμάτων*), "Both now and ever" (*Οὐκ ἐπῃσχύνθη ὁ πανάγαθος Θεός*); Dismissal Troparia: Resurrectional, Saint Basil (*Εἰς πᾶσαν τὴν γῆν*), Circumcision (*Μορφὴν ἀναλλοιώτως*), and Dismissal. On Sunday Matins (§ 5 opening): after Psalm 50 [LXX], Triadic Canon and Lite of Saint Basil; "Glory... Both now and ever...": *Οὐκ ἐπῃσχύνθη*; Triadic hymns *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*; Trisagion Prayers; Troparion *Μορφὴν ἀναλλοιώτως*; Hexapsalmos; "God is the Lord", Troparia as at Vespers; Psalter and Polyeleos; Kathismata: one Resurrectional, one of Saint Basil, and one of the Feast at the first and second stichologia; at the third stichologia, those of the Menaion in order; Evlogitaria, Hypakoe, Anavathmoi of the Tone, Prokeimenon; Canons of the Resurrection, Circumcision, and Saint Basil.

[^217]: January 1 falling on Sunday Concluded: Matins (§ 5 concl.) and Divine Liturgy (§ 6); and Sunday before Theophany: Situation One: January 2, 3, or 4 (Saturday Vespers § 1 and Sunday Matins § 2 opening). Physical leaf p315 (printed page 300) and facing English leaf p316 (printed page 301) conclude the Sunday order for January 1 and open the rubrics for the Sunday before Theophany. At Sunday Matins (§ 5 concl.), Canons of the Resurrection, Circumcision, and Saint Basil are sung with 4 troparia each; after Ode 3, Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos, Kathismata of Saint Basil and the Feast; after Ode 6, Kontakion and Ikos of Saint Basil, Menologion of the day; double Katavasiai of Theophany (*Βυθοῦ ἀνεκάλυψε* and *Στείβει θαλάσσης*); Morning Gospel order; Ode 9 of both Feast and Saint with their Megalynaria and both Katavasiai of Ode 9; Exapostilaria: Resurrectional, Saint Basil, Feast; Praises: 4 Resurrectional and 4 Festal Stichira; "Glory" of Saint Basil (*Ἐξεχύθη ἡ χάρις*), "Both now and ever" (*Συγκαταβαίνων ὁ Σωτήρ*), Great Doxology, and *Σήμερον σωτηρία*. At the Divine Liturgy on Sunday (§ 6): Antiphons of the Circumcision, with Second Antiphon refrain *Ὁ σαρκὶ περιτμηθείς*; Little Entrance with Sunday introit *Δεῦτε προσκυνήσωμεν . . . ὁ ἀναστὰς ἐκ νεκρῶν*; Dismissal Troparia: Resurrectional, Circumcision, Saint Basil, Temple Patron; Kontakion *Ὁ τῶν ὅλων Κύριος*; Epistle of Saint Basil (Colossians 2:8–12), Gospel of the Circumcision (Luke 2:20–21, 40–52); Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great; at the Diptychs, Megalynarion of Saint Basil; Communion Verse Psalm 148:1 LXX; Dismissal Troparion *Μορφὴν ἀναλλοιώτως*, and Dismissal. The Sunday before Theophany (Κυριακὴ πρὸ τῶν Φώτων), Situation One (§§ 1–2), covers the case when the Sunday occurs on January 2, 3, or 4: on Saturday evening (§ 1), following the Introductory Psalm [Ps. 103 LXX] and Psalter Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried" 10 Stichira are sung (6 Resurrectional, 4 Forefeast Idiomela from January 2 *Προεόρτια ᾄσματα*); "Glory" (*Ἔρχεται πρὸς Ἰορδάνην* / "He cometh unto Jordan"); "Both now and ever": First Theotokion Dogmatikon of the Tone; Entrance, *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, Prokeimenon; Aposticha: Resurrectional Stichira, "Glory... Both now and ever...": Forefeast Doxastikon from January 2 (*Ἰωάννη Βαπτιστά* / "O John the Baptist"); Dismissal Troparia: Resurrectional, Forefeast *Ἑτοιμάζου, Ζαβουλών* ("Prepare, O Zebulun"), and Dismissal. On Sunday Matins (§ 2 opening): after Psalm 50 [LXX], Triadic Canon and Triadic hymns *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*; Trisagion Prayers; Dismissal Troparion *Ἑτοιμάζου, Ζαβουλών*; Hexapsalmos; "God is the Lord", Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion twice and Forefeast once; Psalter and Amomos (Kathisma 17 / Psalm 118 LXX); Resurrectional Kathismata in order, Evlogitaria...

[^218]: Sunday before Theophany: Situation One Concluded (Matins § 2 concl., Divine Liturgy § 3) and Situation Two: Sunday before Theophany falling on Paramony, January 5 (Saturday Vespers § 4, Sunday Matins § 5 opening). Physical leaf p317 (printed page 302) and facing English leaf p318 (printed page 303) complete Situation One and commence Situation Two. Sunday Matins for Situation One continues (§ 2 concl.) with the Hypakoe of the Tone, Anavathmoi, Prokeimenon; Canons of the Resurrection and Forefeast; after Ode 3, Kathisma *Ἀόρατε Θεέ* ("Invisible God") once; after Ode 6, Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos, Menologion of the day; Katavasiai of Theophany (*Βυθοῦ ἀνεκάλυψε πυθμένα*); Morning Gospel order; Magnificat (*Τὴν τιμιωτέραν*); Exapostilaria: Resurrectional and Forefeast *Πῶς ῥεῖθρά σε ποτάμια* ("How shall river streams receive Thee"); Praises: 4 Resurrectional and 4 Forefeast Stichira (*Αἱ ἀγγελικαὶ προπορεύεσθε δυνάμεις* / "O angelic hosts, go before"); "Glory": Morning Eothinon Doxastikon; "Both now and ever": *Ὑπερευλογημένη*; Great Doxology, and *Σήμερον σωτηρία*. At the Divine Liturgy (§ 3): Typika and Beatitudes (4 of the Tone, 4 from Ode 6 of Forefeast Canon); Entrance with Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion, Forefeast Troparion *Ἑτοιμάζου, Ζαβουλών*, Temple Patron; Kontakion of the Forefeast *Ἐν τοῖς ῥείθροις σήμερον* ("Today in the streams of the Jordan"); Epistle (2 Timothy 4:5–8) and Gospel (Mark 1:1–8) of the Sunday before Theophany; at "Especially": *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*; Communion Verse Psalm 148:1 LXX (*Αἰνεῖτε τὸν Κύριον*); *Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*, and Dismissal. Situation Two (§§ 4–5) applies when the Sunday before Theophany falls on January 5, the Eve (Paramony) of the Feast: on Saturday evening (§ 4), at "Lord, I have cried", 10 Stichira are sung (6 Resurrectional, 4 Forefeast Idiomela *Προεόρτια ᾄσματα* [the Greek text contains a print duplication 'Προεόρτια α΄ Προεόρτια ᾄσματα', emended to 4 Forefeast Stichira as witnessed in the English column]); "Glory" (*Ἔρχεται πρὸς Ἰορδάνην*); "Both now and ever": First Theotokion Dogmatikon of the Tone; Entrance, *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, Prokeimenon; Aposticha: Resurrectional Stichira, "Glory... Both now and ever...": Forefeast Doxastikon from January 3 (*Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ ποταμῷ* / "In the River Jordan"); Dismissal Troparia: Resurrectional, Forefeast *Ἀπεστρέφετό ποτε* ("The Jordan formerly turned back"), and Dismissal. On Sunday Matins (§ 5 opening): after Psalm 50 [LXX], Triadic Canon, Triadic hymns *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*, Trisagion Prayers, Troparion *Ἀπεστρέφετό ποτε*, Hexapsalmos; "God is the Lord", Resurrectional Troparion twice, Forefeast once; Psalter and Amomos; Resurrectional Kathismata in order, Evlogitaria, Hypakoe of the Tone, Anavathmoi, Prokeimenon; Canons of the Resurrection and Forefeast (*Χριστὸς μολεῖ πρὸς τὸ βάπτισμα* / "Christ cometh unto baptism") with Verse "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee"; after Ode 3, Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos...

[^219]: Sunday before Theophany: Situation Two Concluded (Matins § 5 concl., Divine Liturgy § 6), Great Blessing of the Waters on Paramony; and Feast of Holy Theophany (January 6): Situation One: Feast falling on Friday (Royal Hours on Thursday, Vespers and Liturgy of Saint Basil § 1). Physical leaf p319 (printed page 304) and facing English leaf p320 (printed page 305) conclude the Sunday before Theophany and commence the Feast of the Holy Theophany of our Lord and God and Savior Jesus Christ. Sunday Matins of Situation Two concludes (§ 5 concl.): after Ode 3, Kathisma *Ὁ μέγας ὑετός* ("The great rain" from January 5); after Ode 6, Kontakion of the Forefeast *Ἐν τοῖς ῥείθροις σήμερον*, Ikos, Menologion of the day; Katavasiai *Βυθοῦ ἀνεκάλυψε πυθμένα*; Morning Gospel order; Magnificat (*Τὴν τιμιωτέραν*); Exapostilaria: Resurrectional and Forefeast *Πῶς ῥεῖθρά σε ποτάμια*; Praises: 4 Resurrectional and 4 Forefeast Stichira (*Ἰδοὺ ὁ Βασιλεύς* / "Behold the King"); "Glory": Forefeast Doxastikon *Ἰωάννη Βαπτιστά*; "Both now and ever": *Ὑπερευλογημένη*; Great Doxology, and *Σήμερον σωτηρία*. At the Divine Liturgy (§ 6): Typika and Beatitudes (4 of Tone, 4 from Ode 6 of Forefeast Canon); Entrance with Resurrectional Troparion, Forefeast Troparion *Ἀπεστρέφετό ποτε*, Temple Patron, Kontakion *Ἐν τοῖς ῥείθροις σήμερον*; Epistle (2 Timothy 4:5–8) and Gospel (Mark 1:1–8) of the Sunday before Theophany; at "Especially": *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*; Communion Verse Psalm 148:1 LXX; *Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*. Immediately following the Prayer Behind the Ambon and *Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου* ("Blessed be the name of the Lord"), the clergy proceed to the solemn Great Blessing of the Waters (Μέγας Ἁγιασμός): the choir chants the Idiomela *Φωνὴ Κυρίου ἐπὶ τῶν ὑδάτων* ("The voice of the Lord is upon the waters"), and the office is performed according to the Menaion; Dismissal Troparion of Theophany *Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ βαπτιζομένου σου, Κύριε* ("When Thou, O Lord, wast baptized in the Jordan") and Kontakion *Ἐν τοῖς ῥείθροις σήμερον*; during the blessing and sprinkling of the faithful with holy water, the Second Choir chants the Idiomelon *᾿Ανυμνήσωμεν οἱ πιστοί* ("Let us, the faithful, praise"), followed by the Dismissal. At the trapeza, wine and olive oil are permitted in honor of the Sunday, relaxing the strict fast otherwise observed on the Eve of Theophany. The Feast of Holy Theophany (January 6) opens with Situation One (when January 6 falls on a Friday, § 1): on Thursday morning (January 5), following prescribed Matins, the Royal Hours alone are sung according to the order of the Eve of the Nativity (referencing Christmas Situation One § 2). Great Vespers joined to the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great with the Great Blessing of the Waters at the conclusion of the Liturgy are celebrated on Thursday afternoon according to the prescribed order.


## Cohort 33 Footnotes

[^220]: Eve of Holy Theophany (January 5) Concluded: Great Vespers Joined to the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great, Old Testament Readings, and Great Blessing of the Waters (§ 2); and Feast of Holy Theophany (January 6): Friday Matins Opening (§ 3). Physical leaf p321 (printed page 306) and facing English leaf p322 (printed page 307) govern the festive eve and opening of Holy Theophany when January 6 falls on a Friday. Great Vespers joined to the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great (§ 2) begins following the Dismissal of the Ninth Hour: the Deacon intones the opening blessing (*Εὐλόγησον, Δέσποτα* / "Bless, Master"), the Priest gives the doxological exclamation *Εὐλογημένη ἡ βασιλεία τοῦ Πατρός* ("Blessed is the kingdom of the Father..."), and the Patriarch (or presiding Hierarch) reads the Introductory Psalm [Ps. 103 LXX], followed by the Great Litany. At "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), 6 Stichira are chanted: the 4 Idiomela of the Feast (*Τὸν φωτισμὸν ἡμῶν* / "Our illumination") repeating the first two; "Glory... Both now and ever...": *Ὑπέκλινας κάραν τῷ Προδρόμῳ* ("Thou hast bowed Thy head unto the Forerunner"). The Little Entrance is made with the Holy Gospel Book, followed by *Φῶς ἱλαρόν* ("O Gladsome Light") and the thirteen Old Testament Readings of the Eve. Following Reading 3 (Genesis 1:1–13: *Ἐν ἀρχῇ ἐποίησεν ὁ Θεός* / "In the beginning God created"), the First Choir intones the Troparion *Ἐπεφάνης ἐν τῷ κόσμῳ* ("Thou hast appeared in the world"), and the Second Choir responds with the choral refrain *Ἵνα φωτίσῃς τοὺς ἐν σκότει καθημένους, Φιλάνθρωπε, δόξα σοι* ("To give light to those who sit in darkness; O Lover of mankind, glory to Thee!"), sung alternately between the verses of Psalm 66 LXX until the Canonarch concludes. Following Reading 6 (4 Kingdoms 2:6–14 LXX / 2 Kings 2:6–14: *Εἶπεν Ἠλίας τῷ Ἐλισσαιέ* / "And Elijah said unto Elisha"), the Second Choir intones the Troparion *Ἁμαρτωλοῖς καὶ τελώναις* ("Unto sinners and tax-collectors"), and the choral refrain *Ποῦ γὰρ εἶχε τὸ φῶς σου λάμψαι* ("Where indeed should Thy light have shone...") is alternated between the verses of Psalm 92 LXX. Following Reading 13 (4 Kingdoms 5:1–14 LXX / 2 Kings 5:1–14: *Παραγένετο Νεεμὰν βασιλέως Ἀσσυρίων* / "So Naaman, captain of the host of the king of Syria [printed Ἀσσυρίων]"), the Small Litany is intoned, the Trisagion Hymn is chanted, followed by the Epistle (1 Corinthians 9:19–27), "Alleluia", and the Holy Gospel (Luke 3:1–18) proclaimed from the ambo, followed by the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great. The Communion Verse is Psalm 148:1 LXX (*Αἰνεῖτε τὸν Κύριον* / "Praise the Lord from the heavens"), followed by *Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς τὸ ἀληθινόν* ("We have seen the true light"). After the Prayer behind the Ambon, the clergy proceed to the solea for the solemn Great Blessing of the Waters (*Μέγας Ἁγιασμός*): the choirs chant the Idiomela *Φωνὴ Κυρίου ἐπὶ τῶν ὑδάτων* ("The voice of the Lord is upon the waters"), and the office is celebrated according to the Menaion; during the sprinkling of the faithful, the Second Choir sings the Idiomelon *Ἀνυμνήσωμεν οἱ πιστοί* ("Let us, the faithful, praise"), followed by the solemn festal Dismissal with its proper characteristic incipit: *Ὁ ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ ὑπὸ Ἰωάννου βαπτισθῆναι καταδεξάμενος διὰ τὴν ἡμῶν σωτηρίαν, Χριστὸς ὁ ἀληθινὸς Θεὸς ἡμῶν* ("May He Who deigned to be baptized by John in the Jordan for our salvation, Christ our true God..."). The rubrics prescribe xerophagy (*ξηροφαγία*, strict fast without wine or oil) at table on this eve. Paragraph 3 opens Friday Matins for the feast: following Psalm 50 [LXX], the Lite and the Idiomela of the Aposticha (omitted on the eve due to the vigil Liturgy) are chanted; then the Trisagion Prayers, the Dismissal Troparion of Theophany *Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ βαπτιζομένου σου, Κύριε* ("When Thou, O Lord, wast baptized in the Jordan"), and the Hexapsalmos; at "God is the Lord", the Dismissal Troparion is chanted thrice, followed by the Psalter Kathismata and the festal Polyeleos (*Αἰνεῖτε τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*, Psalm 134 LXX; the printed English column incorrectly labels this Psalm 135 LXX by confusing Masoretic numbering).

[^221]: Feast of Holy Theophany (January 6) Continued: Friday Matins Gospel Order, Canons, and Festive Megalynaria of the Ninth Ode (§ 3 cont.). Physical leaf p323 (printed page 308) and facing English leaf p324 (printed page 309) provide the rubrical sequence of Friday Matins from the Kathismata through the Ninth Ode. Following the 3 Kathisma sedalia, the Anavathmoi of Tone 4 (First Antiphon *Ἐκ νεότητός μου* / "From my youth") are sung, followed by the Prokeimenon from Psalm 113:3 LXX (*Ἡ θάλασσα εἶδε καὶ ἔφυγεν, ὁ Ἰορδάνης ἐστράφη εἰς τὰ ὀπίσω* / "The sea saw and fled, Jordan was driven back"). After *Πᾶσα πνοὴ αἰνεσάτω τὸν Κύριον* ("Let everything that hath breath praise the Lord"), the Matins Gospel is proclaimed (Mark 1:9–11), followed by Psalm 50 [LXX] read simply; "Glory": *Τὰ σύμπαντα σήμερον ἀγαλλιάσθω* ("Today let all things greatly rejoice"); "Both now and ever": the same; Verse: *Ἐλέησόν με, ὁ Θεός* (Ps. 50:3 LXX) and the Idiomelon *Θεὸς λόγος ἐπεφάνη ἐν σαρκί* ("God the Word appeared in the flesh"). Both Canons of the Feast are chanted with 4 troparia each, without verses except for "Glory... Both now and ever..." in each Ode: the First Canon by Saint Cosmas the Melodist in Tone 2 (*Βυθοῦ ἀνεκάλυψε πυθμένα* / "He uncovered the bottom of the deep") and the Second Iambic Canon by Saint John Damascene in Tone 2 (*Στείβει θαλάσσης* / "The Lord, working wonders"). After Ode 3, the Hypakoe *Ὅτε τῇ ἐπιφανείᾳ σου* ("When at Thy manifestation"); after Ode 6, the Kontakion *Ἐπεφάνης σήμερον τῇ οἰκουμένῃ* ("Thou hast appeared today to the universe"), the Ikos, and the Menologion. Both Katavasiai are sung in full at each Ode. At Ode 9, the Magnificat (*Τὴν τιμιωτέραν*) is not sung; instead, the festive Megalynaria (*Μεγαλυνάρια*) are appointed for each troparion. For the First Canon, four primary Megalynaria are given: (1) *Μεγάλυνον ψυχή μου, τὴν τιμιωτέραν τῶν ἄνω στρατευμάτων* ("Magnify, O my soul, Her that is more honorable than the heavenly hosts"); (2) *Μεγάλυνον ψυχή μου, τὸν ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ ἐλθόντα βαπτισθῆναι* ("Magnify, O my soul, Him Who hath come to be baptized in the Jordan"); (3) *Μεγάλυνον ψυχή μου, τὸν ὑπὸ Προδρόμου τὸ βάπτισμα αἰτοῦντα [λαβόντα]* ("Magnify, O my soul, Him Who asketh [receiveth] baptism from the Forerunner"); (4) *Μεγάλυνον ψυχή μου, τὸν ἐκ τῆς Πατρῴας φωνῆς μαρτυρηθέντα* ("Magnify, O my soul, Him to Whom the voice of the Father bore witness"). Three additional bracketed Megalynaria marked with asterisks are preserved from the modern printed Athenian Menaion tradition (ed. Phos, 1960; Apostoliki Diakonia, 1991), although absent from the classical textus receptus of the Violakis Typikon: (5) *Μεγάλυνον ψυχή μου, τὸν ἕνα τῆς Τριάδος, κλίναντα τὸν αὐχένα, καὶ βάπτισμα λαβόντα* ("Magnify, O my soul, One of the Trinity Who bowed His head and received baptism"); (6) *Προφῆτα, δεῦρο πρός με, ἔκτεινον τὴν χεῖρα, καὶ βάπτισόν με τάχος* ("O Prophet, come unto Me: stretch out thy hand and baptize Me swiftly"); (7) *Προφῆτα, ἄφες ἄρτι, καὶ βάπτισόν με θέλων· πληρῶσαι καὶ γὰρ ἦλθον, πᾶσαν δικαιοσύνην* ("O Prophet, suffer it to be so now, and baptize Me willing: for I have come to fulfill all righteousness"). For the Second Iambic Canon, five Megalynaria are provided: (1) *Σήμερον ὁ Δεσπότης, κλίνει τὸν αὐχένα, χειρὶ τῇ τοῦ Προδρόμου* ("Today the Master bows His head beneath the hand of the Forerunner"); (2) *Σήμερον Ἰωάννης, βαπτίζει τὸν Δεσπότην, ἐν ῥείθροις Ἰορδάνου* ("Today John baptizes the Master in the streams of the Jordan"); and three bracketed Menaion additions: (3) *Σήμερον ὁ Δεσπότης, νάμασιν ἐνθάπτει, βροτῶν τὴν ἁμαρτίαν* ("Today the Master buries in the waters the sin of mortal man"); (4) *Σήμερον ὁ Δεσπότης, ἄνωθεν μαρτυρεῖται, Υἱὸς ἠγαπημένος* ("Today the Master receives testimony from on high, that He is the beloved Son"); (5) *Σήμερον ὁ Δεσπότης, ἦλθεν ἁγιάσαι, τὴν φύσιν τῶν ὑδάτων* ("Today the Master has come to sanctify the nature of the waters").

[^222]: Feast of Holy Theophany (January 6) Continued: Conclusion of Matins Ode 9, Praises, Great Doxology, and Great Blessing of the Waters Rite (§ 3 concl.). Physical leaf p325 (printed page 310) and facing English leaf p326 (printed page 311) conclude the Ninth Ode and detail the Great Blessing of the Waters celebrated at the climax of the Great Doxology. Ode 9 concludes with the final Megalynaria: *Σήμερον ὁ Δεσπότης, τὸ βάπτισμα λαμβάνει, ὑπὸ χειρὸς Προδρόμου* ("Today the Master receives baptism at the hand of the Forerunner"); "Glory": *Μεγάλυνον ψυχή μου, τῆς τρισυποστάτου, καὶ ἀδιαιρέτου, θεότητος τὸ κράτος* ("Magnify, O my soul, the power of the Godhead in three Persons and undivided"); "Both now and ever": *Μεγάλυνον ψυχή μου, τὴν λυτρωσαμένην, ἡμᾶς ἐκ τῆς κατάρας* ("Magnify, O my soul, Her who hath delivered us from the curse"). Matins continues with the Exapostilarion chanted thrice; at the Praises: 6 Idiomela (*Φῶς ἐκ φωτός* / "Light from Light"); "Glory": *Νάματα Ἰορδάνεια* ("O Savior, Who clothedst Thyself with the waters of Jordan"); "Both now and ever": *Σήμερον ὁ Χριστός* ("Today Christ"), and the Great Doxology. In the Great Doxology, an ancient Constantinopolitan rubrical custom replaces the customary opening verse *Δόξα σοι τῷ δείξαντι τὸ φῶς* ("Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light") with the concluding refrain of the Theotokion: "We who have been enlightened cry aloud: Glory to God made manifest, Who hath appeared upon earth and brought light unto the world." Immediately following the third repetition of the Trisagion (*Ἅγιος ἀθάνατος, ἐλέησον ἡμᾶς*) at the end of the Doxology, the clergy and choirs transition directly into the solemn Great Blessing of the Waters: the choirs chant the 3 Idiomela of Saint Sophronius of Jerusalem (*Φωνὴ Κυρίου ἐπὶ τῶν ὑδάτων* / "The voice of the Lord is upon the waters"), "Glory... Both now and ever...": *Πρὸς τὴν φωνὴν τοῦ βοῶντος ἐν τῇ ἐρήμῳ* ("At the voice of one crying in the wilderness"). The Priest exits through the Holy Doors carrying the Precious Cross on his head, preceded by candle-bearers and Deacons; the Patriarch (or presiding Hierarch), vested in epitrachilion and small omophorion, descends from his Throne to the center of the temple where the water vessel has been prepared. The Canonarch reads the three Prophecies (Isaiah 35:1–10; 55:1–13; 12:3–6), followed by the Epistle (1 Corinthians 10:1–4) and the Gospel (Mark 1:9–11) proclaimed by the Patriarch. Following the polychronion (*Εἰς πολλὰ ἔτη*), the Great Archdeacon intones the Great Litany of the Blessing while the Patriarch recites silently the secret prayer *Κύριε Ἰησοῦ Χριστέ* ("O Lord Jesus Christ"), culminating in the aloud exclamation *Τριὰς ὑπερούσιε* ("O Trinity transcending essence") and the great festive prayer *Μέγας εἶ, Κύριε, καὶ θαυμαστὰ τὰ ἔργα σου* ("Great art Thou, O Lord, and marvelous are Thy works"), repeated thrice with the choral acclamation *Δόξα σοι, Κύριε, δόξα σοι!* ("Glory to Thee, O Lord, glory to Thee!"). A critical apparatus note in both Greek and English facsimile columns addresses a numbering error in the 1888 edition, where Violakis misnumbered this footnote as footnote 4 instead of 1; the note documents the pastoral practice emerging in parish churches of delaying the Great Blessing of the Waters until after the Divine Liturgy to prevent disorder and accommodate late-arriving faithful.

[^223]: Feast of Holy Theophany (January 6) Continued: Conclusion of the Great Blessing of the Waters and Divine Liturgy Antiphons 1 & 2 (§ 4 opening). Physical leaf p327 (printed page 312) and facing English leaf p328 (printed page 313) conclude the solemn sanctification of waters and commence the festal Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom. Following the thrice-repeated prayer *Μέγας εἶ, Κύριε*, the celebrant immerses the Precious Cross into the water vessel three times with the sprinkler (*ἁγιαστήρ*), chanting each time the Dismissal Troparion of Theophany: *Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ βαπτιζομένου σου, Κύριε* ("When Thou, O Lord, wast baptized in the Jordan"), repeated by each choir. He then sprinkles the congregation cruciformly with the newly consecrated water while chanting the Kontakion *Ἐπεφάνης σήμερον τῇ οἰκουμένῃ* ("Thou hast appeared today to the universe"). As the Patriarch ascends the Throne amidst choral acclamations (*Εἰς πολλὰ ἔτη*), he sprinkles the concelebrating Hierarchs and clergy; during this sprinkling, the choirs chant slowly and melodiously the Troparion from Ode 8 of the Canon: *Τριάδος ἡ φανέρωσις* ("The manifestation of the Trinity"). The Second Choir then chants the Dismissal Troparion *Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*, and the Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom commences immediately. At the Divine Liturgy (§ 4), the special festive Antiphons of Theophany are chanted in Tone 2: First Antiphon verses from Psalm 113:1, 2, 3, 5 LXX (*Ἐν ἐξόδῳ Ἰσραὴλ ἐξ Αἰγύπτου, οἴκου Ἰακώβ* / "When Israel went out of Egypt, the house of Jacob from a barbarian people"; *Ἐγενήθη Ἰουδαία ἁγίασμα αὐτοῦ, Ἰσραὴλ ἐξουσία αὐτοῦ* / "Judea became His sanctuary, and Israel His dominion"; *Ἡ θάλασσα εἶδε καὶ ἔφυγεν· ὁ Ἰορδάνης ἐστράφη εἰς τὰ ὀπίσω* / "The sea saw and fled; the Jordan turned back"; *Τί σοί ἐστι θάλασσα, ὅτι ἔφυγες; καὶ σὺ Ἰορδάνη, ὅτι ἐστράφης εἰς τὰ ὀπίσω;* / "What ailed thee, O sea, that thou didst flee? And thou, Jordan, that thou didst turn back?"); refrain: *Ταῖς πρεσβείαις τῆς Θεοτόκου, Σῶτερ, σῶσον ἡμᾶς* ("Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, Savior, save us"). Second Antiphon verses from Psalm 114:1, 2, 3, 5 LXX (*Ἠγάπησα, ὅτι εἰσακούσεται Κύριος τῆς φωνῆς τῆς δεήσεώς μου* / "I am well pleased that the Lord will hear the voice of my prayer"; *Ὅτι ἔκλινε τὸ οὖς αὐτοῦ ἐμοί, καὶ ἐν ταῖς ἡμέραις μου ἐπικαλέσομαι* / "For He hath inclined His ear unto me, and in my days will I call upon Him"; *Περιέσχον με ὠδῖνες θανάτου, κίνδυνοι ᾅδου εὕροσάν με* / "The pangs of death compassed me, the perils of Hades found me; I found tribulation and sorrow"; *Ἐλεήμων ὁ Κύριος καὶ δίκαιος, καὶ ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν ἐλεεῖ* / "Merciful is the Lord, and righteous; yea, our God is merciful").

[^224]: Feast of Holy Theophany (January 6) Concluded: Divine Liturgy Antiphon 3, Little Entrance, Epistle, Gospel, and Dismissal (§ 4 concl.); and Synaxis of the Honorable and Glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John (January 7): Friday Vespers (§ 5). Physical leaf p329 (printed page 314) and facing English leaf p330 (printed page 315) conclude the festal Divine Liturgy of Theophany and commence the post-festal Synaxis of the Forerunner celebrated on the following day. At the Divine Liturgy (§ 4 concl.), the Second Antiphon concludes with the refrain *Σῶσον ἡμᾶς Υἱὲ Θεοῦ, ὁ ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ ὑπὸ Ἰωάννου βαπτισθείς, ψάλλοντάς σοι, Ἀλληλούϊα* ("Save us, O Son of God, Who wast baptized by John in the Jordan, who sing to Thee: Alleluia"), followed by *Ὁ Μονογενὴς Υἱὸς καὶ Λόγος τοῦ Θεοῦ* ("Only-begotten Son and Immortal Word of God"). The Third Antiphon is chanted in Tone 1 to verses from Psalm 117:1–4 LXX (*Ἐξομολογεῖσθε τῷ Κυρίῳ, ὅτι ἀγαθός, ὅτι εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα τὸ ἔλεος αὐτοῦ* / "O give thanks unto the Lord, for He is good, for His mercy endureth forever"; *Εἰπάτω δὴ οἶκος Ἰσραήλ...* / "Let the house of Israel now say..."; *Εἰπάτω δὴ οἶκος Ἀαρών...* / "Let the house of Aaron now say..."; *Εἰπάτωσαν δὴ πάντες οἱ φοβούμενοι τὸν Κύριον...* / "Let all that fear the Lord now say..."); refrain: Dismissal Troparion of Theophany *Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*. At the Little Entrance, the festive Introit Verse is chanted from Psalm 117:26, 27 LXX: *Εὐλογημένος ὁ ἐρχόμενος ἐν ὀνόματι Κυρίου, Θεὸς Κύριος καὶ ἐπέφανεν ἡμῖν. Σῶσον ἡμᾶς Υἱὲ Θεοῦ, ὁ ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ ὑπὸ Ἰωάννου βαπτισθείς, ψάλλοντάς σοι, Ἀλληλούϊα* ("Blessed is He that cometh in the name of the Lord; the Lord is God, and hath appeared unto us. Save us, O Son of God, Who wast baptized by John in the Jordan, who sing to Thee: Alleluia"). Troparia: Dismissal Troparion *Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*, and Kontakion *Ἐπεφάνης σήμερον*. Instead of the Trisagion Hymn, the baptismal hymn is chanted: *Ὅσοι εἰς Χριστὸν ἐβαπτίσθητε, Χριστὸν ἐνεδύσασθε. Ἀλληλούϊα* ("All of you who have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia", Galatians 3:27). The Epistle of the Feast is Titus 2:11–14; 3:4–7; the Gospel is Matthew 3:13–17. At "Especially", instead of the Axion Estin, the Heirmos of Ode 9 is chanted: *Μεγάλυνον ψυχή μου τὴν τιμιωτέραν καὶ ἐνδοξοτέραν τῶν ἄνω στρατευμάτων. Ἀπορεῖ πᾶσα γλῶσσα* ("Magnify, O my soul, Her that is more honorable and more glorious than the heavenly hosts. Every tongue is at a loss to praise Thee worthily..."). The Communion Verse is Titus 2:11: *Ἐπεφάνη ἡ χάρις τοῦ Θεοῦ ἡ σωτήριος πᾶσιν ἀνθρώποις* ("The grace of God that bringeth salvation hath appeared unto all men"); instead of *Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*, Dismissal Troparion *Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*, and the Dismissal. On Friday evening (§ 5), the Synaxis of the Honorable and Glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John (*Ἡ Σύναξις τοῦ τιμίου Προδρόμου*) is celebrated at Great Vespers: after the Introductory Psalm [Ps. 103 LXX], at "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), 6 Stichira are chanted: 3 Idiomela of the Feast (*Τὸν φωτισμὸν ἡμῶν*) and 3 Prosomoia of the Forerunner (*Πανεύφημε Πρόδρομε Χριστοῦ*); "Glory": *Ἔνσαρκε λύχνε* ("O torch incarnate"); "Both now and ever": *Θεὸς λόγος ἐπεφάνη ἐν σαρκί* ("God the Word appeared in the flesh"). Entrance with *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, followed by the Great Prokeimenon in Tone 7 from Psalm 113:11 LXX: *Ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν ἐν τῷ οὐρανῷ καὶ ἐν τῇ γῇ, πάντα ὅσα ἠθέλησεν ἐποίησεν* ("Our God is in heaven and on earth; all things whatsoever He hath willed, He hath done") with its four verses. At the Aposticha: 3 Prosomoia Stichira beginning *Ὡς εἶδέ σε Δέσποτα* ("When he saw Thee, O Master"); "Glory": *Ὡς τοῦ Πνεύματος ἐραστής* ("As a lover of the Spirit"); "Both now and ever": *Δεῦτε μιμησώμεθα* ("Come, let us imitate the wise virgins"); Dismissal Troparia: Theophany (*Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*), and Forerunner (*Μνήμη δικαίου μετ’ ἐγκωμίων* / "The memory of the righteous is celebrated with hymns of praise").


## Cohort 34 Footnotes

[^225]: Synaxis of the Honorable and Glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John (January 7) Concluded: Saturday Matins (§ 6), Divine Liturgy (§ 7); and Opening of Sunday after the Lights / Epiphany: Saturday Great Vespers (§ 8). Physical leaf p331 (printed page 316) and facing English leaf p332 (printed page 317) govern the conclusion of the feast of the Synaxis of the Forerunner and the opening of the Sunday after the Lights when January 6 falls on a Friday (Situation 1). Saturday Matins (§ 6) opens following Psalm 50 [LXX] with the Dismissal Troparion of the Forerunner (*Μνήμη δικαίου μετ’ ἐγκωμίων* / "The memory of the righteous is celebrated with hymns of praise") and the Hexapsalmos. At "God is the Lord" (*Θεὸς Κύριος*), the Dismissal Troparia are chanted as at Vespers: Theophany (*Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*) twice, and Forerunner (*Μνήμη δικαίου*) once; followed by the Psalter Kathisma of Saturday; two Kathismata: *Τοῦ Ἰησοῦ γεννηθέντος* ("When Jesus was born") and *Ἐν τοῖς ῥείθροις βλέψας σε τοῦ Ἰορδάνου* ("When he saw Thee in the streams of the Jordan"), chanted twice each; and Psalm 50 [LXX] read simply. Canons: Canon of the Feast (*Βυθοῦ ἀνεκάλυψε πυθμένα* / "He uncovered the bottom of the deep") with the verse *Δόξα σοι, ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, δόξα σοι* ("Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee"), and Canon of the Forerunner (*Βαπτιζόμενον* / "Being baptized") with the verse *Ἅγιε τοῦ Θεοῦ, πρέσβευε ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν* ("Holy forerunner of God, pray for us"). After Ode 3: Kathismata *Ὁ μέγας ὑετός* ("The great rain") and *Ἑκὼν προῆλθεν ἐπὶ γῆς* ("Willingly He came forth upon earth"). After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Forerunner, and the Menologion. Ὦ τῶν ὑπὲρ νοῦν* ("Magnify, O my soul... O the marvels surpassing understanding"). Exapostilaria: of the Forerunner and of the Feast. At the Praises: Idiomela of the Feast as yesterday; "Glory": *Ἄγγελος ἐκ στειρωτικῶν ὠδίνων* ("An angel from barren pangs"); "Both now and ever": *Σήμερον ὁ Χριστὸς ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ* ("Today Christ is in Jordan"); Great Doxology, and Dismissal Troparion *Μνήμη δικαίου*. Instead of the Trisagion Hymn, the baptismal hymn *Ὅσοι εἰς Χριστὸν ἐβαπτίσθητε* ("All of you who have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ", Galatians 3:27) is sung; the Epistle (Acts 19:1–8) and the Gospel (John 1:29–34) of the Forerunner. At "Especially", the Heirmos of Ode 9 *Μεγάλυνον ἡ ψυχή μου... Ὦ τῶν ὑπὲρ νοῦν*. Communion Verse: Psalm 111:6 LXX (*Εἰς μνημόσυνον αἰώνιον ἔσται δίκαιος* / "The righteous shall be in everlasting remembrance"); *Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*, and Dismissal. The section for Sunday after the Lights / Epiphany opens at § 8: on Saturday evening at Great Vespers, following the Introductory Psalm [Ps. 103 LXX] and Kathisma 1 of the Psalter, at "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), 10 Stichira are sung: 6 Resurrectional and 4 of the Afterfeast in appointed sequence; "Glory": *Κύριε πληρῶσαι βουλόμενος* ("O Lord, wishing to fulfill...", seek at the Glory of the Lite); "Both now and ever": first Theotokion of the Tone; Entrance with *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, Prokeimenon *Ὁ Κύριος ἐβασίλευσεν* (Ps. 92:1 LXX). At the Aposticha, the Resurrectional Stichira are sung.

[^226]: Sunday after the Lights / Epiphany Continued: Sunday Matins (§ 9), Divine Liturgy (§ 10); Apodosis of the Feast in Situation 1 (§ 11); and Heading for Situation 2. Physical leaf p333 (printed page 318) and facing English leaf p334 (printed page 319) govern the completion of the Sunday after Epiphany in Situation 1. At the Aposticha of Saturday Vespers (§ 8 concl.): Resurrectional Stichira; "Glory... Both now and ever...": *Σήμερον ἡ κτίσις φωτίζεται* ("Today creation is illumined", seek in the Lite); Dismissal Troparia: Resurrectional, and Theophany (*Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*), and Dismissal. Sunday Matins (§ 9) opens following Psalm 50 [LXX] with the Triadic Canon and the Triadic hymns *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν* ("It is truly meet"); Trisagion Prayers, Dismissal Troparion *Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*, and Hexapsalmos. At "God is the Lord" (*Θεὸς Κύριος*), the Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion is chanted twice, and *Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ* once; Psalter Kathismata and the Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX); Resurrectional Kathismata and those of the Feast in place of the Theotokia. A critical apparatus note highlights a typographical omission in the 1888 Violakis editio princeps, where the Evlogitaria, Hypakoe, Anavathmoi of the Tone, and Prokeimenon were inadvertently skipped in the printed text. Canons: Resurrectional Canon and First Canon of the Feast with the verse *Δόξα σοί, ὁ Θεός*. After Ode 3: pre-festal Kathisma; after Ode 6: Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos, and Menologion of the day. At the Praises: 4 Resurrectional Stichira and 4 of the Afterfeast; "Glory": the appointed Eothinon Doxastikon; "Both now and ever": *Ὑπερευλογημένη ὑπάρχεις*; Great Doxology, and *Σήμερον σωτηρία*. At the Divine Liturgy (§ 10): Typika and the Beatitudes of the Tone for 4 troparia, and 4 from Ode 6 of the festal Canon. After the Little Entrance: Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion, *Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*, and Holy Patron of the Temple; Kontakion *Ἐπεφάνης σήμερον*; Epistle (Ephesians 4:7–13) and Gospel (Matthew 4:12–17) of the Sunday after the Lights. At "Especially", *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*; Communion Verse: Psalm 148:1 LXX (*Αἰνεῖτε τὸν Κύριον* / "Praise the Lord from the heavens"); *Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς τὸ ἀληθινόν*, and Dismissal. At § 11, the Apodosis of the Feast in Situation 1 (Theophany on Friday) falls on the Saturday of the following week (January 14): the entire festal office of Theophany is repeated, omitting the Old Testament Readings at Vespers, and the Lite, Polyeleos, and festal Gospel at Matins; at the Divine Liturgy, the Epistle (Colossians 1:3–6) and Gospel (Luke 16:10–15) of the Saturday after the Lights are appointed. Leaf p333 concludes with the heading for Situation Two (*Β΄*), governing the calendar when Holy Theophany occurs on a Saturday.

[^227]: Situation 2 (Theophany on Saturday): Friday Royal Hours, Vespers, Liturgy of St. Basil (§ 12); Saturday Theophany Matins, Great Blessing of Waters, Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom (§ 13); Sunday after the Lights & Synaxis of the Forerunner: Saturday Great Vespers (§ 14) and Sunday Matins through Ode 9 (§ 15 opening). Physical leaf p335 (printed page 320) and facing English leaf p336 (printed page 321) formulate the rubrical order when Holy Theophany falls on Saturday. At § 12: on Friday morning (January 5), following the daily Matins office, the Great Royal Hours of Theophany are chanted according to the rubrics of Situation 1, § 1 of the Eve of the Nativity of Christ (i.e. celebrated consecutively in the morning without the Divine Liturgy). Great Vespers joined to the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great, followed by the Great Blessing of the Waters at the conclusion of the Liturgy, is celebrated in the afternoon according to Situation 1, § 2. At § 13: on Saturday (January 6), the festal Matins, the Great Blessing of the Waters, and the Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom are celebrated according to Situation 1, § 3. At § 14, Great Vespers on Saturday evening combines the Resurrection, the Afterfeast of Theophany, and the Synaxis of the Honorable Forerunner: Introductory Psalm [Ps. 103 LXX]; at "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), 10 Stichira: 4 Resurrectional, 3 of the Feast (*Τὸν φωτισμὸν ἡμῶν*), and 3 of the Forerunner (*Πανεύφημε Πρόδρομε Χριστοῦ*); "Glory": *Ἔνσαρκε λύχνε* ("O torch incarnate"); "Both now and ever": *Θεὸς λόγος ἐπεφάνη ἐν σαρκί* ("God the Word appeared in the flesh"). Entrance with *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, Great Prokeimenon in Tone 7: Psalm 113:11 LXX (*Ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν ἐν τῷ οὐρανῷ καὶ ἐν τῇ γῇ* / "Our God is in heaven and on earth..."). Aposticha: Resurrectional Stichira; "Glory": *Ὡς τοῦ Πνεύματος ἐραστής* ("As a lover of the Spirit"); "Both now and ever": *Δεῦτε μιμησώμεθα* ("Come, let us imitate the wise virgins"); Dismissal Troparia: Resurrectional, Forerunner (*Μνήμη δικαίου*), and Theophany (*Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*), and Dismissal. Canons: Resurrectional, Feast (*Δόξα σοι, ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, δόξα σοι*), and Forerunner (*Ἅγιε τοῦ Θεοῦ*). After Ode 3: Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos, and Kathismata of the Forerunner *Ὁ μέγας ὑετός* and *Ἑκὼν προῆλθεν ἐπὶ γῆς*. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Forerunner, and Menologion. Katavasiai *Στείβει θαλάσσης* and Morning Gospel. Ode 9: festal Ninth Ode *Ἀπορεῖ πᾶσα γλῶσσα* with Megalynaria, and of the Forerunner with the verse *Ἅγιε τοῦ Θεοῦ*.

Praises: 4 Resurrectional Stichira and 4 of the Feast (*Φῶς ἐκ φωτός*); "Glory": *Ἄγγελος ἐκ στειρωτικῶν ὠδίνων* ("An angel from barren pangs"); "Both now and ever": *Ὑπερευλογημένη ὑπάρχεις*; Great Doxology, and *Σήμερον σωτηρία*. At § 16: Divine Liturgy of the combined solemnity: festal Antiphons of Theophany as yesterday; Second Antiphon refrain *Σῶσον ἡμᾶς... ὁ ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ ὑπὸ Ἰωάννου βαπτισθείς*. Dismissal Troparia: Resurrectional, Forerunner (*Μνήμη δικαίου*), and Feast (*Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*); critical apparatus note on the omission of the Troparion of the Holy Patron of the Temple (omitted on p. 149, § 15 of Violakis, but prescribed in § 39 of the Protheoria); Kontakion *Ἐπεφάνης σήμερον*; instead of Trisagion, *Ὅσοι εἰς Χριστὸν ἐβαπτίσθητε*; Epistle (Acts 19:1–8) and Gospel (John 1:29–34) of the Forerunner. At "Especially", Heirmos of Ode 9 *Μεγάλυνον ψυχή μου... Ὦ τῶν ὑπὲρ νοῦν*. Communion Verse: Psalm 111:6 LXX (*Εἰς μνημόσυνον αἰώνιον*), *Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*, and Dismissal. Critical Apparatus Note 2 preserves Violakis's rubrical rule for the lectionary when January 14 coincides with the opening of the Lenten Triodion (Sunday of the Publican and Pharisee): under standard conditions, the Epistle and Gospel of the Sunday after the Lights (Ephesians 4:7–13; Matthew 4:12–17) are transferred to the following Sunday (January 14), which is the Apodosis of Theophany. If, however, January 14 is the Sunday of the Publican and Pharisee, the Triodion lections cannot be superseded; therefore, on January 7, the Epistle of the Forerunner is read alongside the Gospel of the Sunday after the Lights. At § 17: the Apodosis of the Feast in Situation 2 falls on the following Sunday (January 14). At Saturday Great Vespers: Introductory Psalm [Ps. 103 LXX], Psalter Kathisma 1; at "Lord, I have cried", 4 Resurrectional Stichira and 6 of the Feast (*Τὸν φωτισμὸν ἡμῶν*); "Glory": *Ὑπέκλινας κάραν τῷ Προδρόμῳ*; "Both now and ever": first Theotokion of Tone; Entrance with *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, Prokeimenon *Ὁ Κύριος ἐβασίλευσεν*. Aposticha: Resurrectional Stichira; "Glory... Both now and ever...": *Τὸν ἐκ Παρθένου ἥλιον* ("The Sun born of a virgin"); Dismissal Troparia: Resurrectional, and Feast (*Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*) twice, and Dismissal. At § 18 (opening): Sunday Matins opens after Psalm 50 [LXX] with the Triadic Canon and the Triadic hymns *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*; Trisagion Prayers, *Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*, and Hexapsalmos. At "God is the Lord", Dismissal Troparia as at Vespers; Psalter and Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX); Resurrectional Kathismata and those of the Feast in place of Theotokia; Evlogitaria, Hypakoe of Tone, Anavathmoi, and Prokeimenon.

[^229]: Apodosis of the Feast in Situation 2 Concluded: Sunday Matins Conclusion (§ 18 concl.) and Divine Liturgy (§ 19); and Situation 3 (Theophany on Sunday): Friday Royal Hours (§ 20), Forefeast Vespers (§ 21), and Saturday Matins opening (§ 22). Physical leaf p339 (printed page 324) and facing English leaf p340 (printed page 325) conclude Situation 2 and inaugurate Situation 3 when Holy Theophany falls on Sunday. At § 18 (concl.): Sunday Matins of the Apodosis continues with the Canons: Resurrectional Canon and both Canons of the Feast with the verse *Δόξα σοι, ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, δόξα σοι*. After Ode 3: Hypakoe of the Feast *Ὅτε τῇ ἐπιφανείᾳ σου*; after Ode 6: Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos, and Menologion. Double Katavasiai (*Βυθοῦ ἀνεκάλυψε* and *Στείβει θαλάσσης*); entire order of the Morning Gospel. Ninth Odes of both Canons with Megalynaria. Exapostilaria: Resurrectional and of the Feast twice. Praises: 4 Resurrectional Stichira and 4 of the Feast (*Φῶς ἐκ φωτός*); "Glory": *Νάματα Ἰορδάνεια*; "Both now and ever": *Ὑπερευλογημένη ὑπάρχεις*; Great Doxology, and *Σήμερον σωτηρία*. At § 19: Divine Liturgy of the Apodosis: Antiphons of the Feast as in Situation 2; Second Antiphon refrain *Ὁ ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*; Introit of the Feast with *Ὁ ἀναστὰς ἐκ νεκρῶν*; Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion, and Feast (*Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*); critical apparatus note on the omission of the Troparion of the Holy Patron of the Temple (omitted in Violakis p. 150, § 19, but prescribed in Protheoria p. 33, § 39, and noted in Diptycha 1990 p. 20); Kontakion *Ἐπεφάνης σήμερον*; Trisagion Hymn; Epistle (Ephesians 4:7–13) and Gospel (Matthew 4:12–17) of the Sunday after the Lights (since on the preceding Sunday the lections of the Forerunner were proclaimed). At "Especially", Heirmos of Ode 9 *Μεγάλυνον ψυχή μου... Ὦ τῶν ὑπὲρ νοῦν*. Communion Verse: Titus 2:11 (*Ἐπεφάνη ἡ χάρις τοῦ Θεοῦ* / "The grace of God that bringeth salvation hath appeared unto all men"); *Ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ*, and Dismissal. The rubric transitions at centered heading *Γ΄* to Situation Three, when Holy Theophany falls on a Sunday. At § 20: on Friday morning (January 4), following daily Matins, only the Great Royal Hours are celebrated, with Troparion *Ἑτοιμάζου Ζαβουλών* ("Make ready, O Zebulun") and Kontakion *Ἐν τοῖς ῥείθροις σήμερον* ("Today the Lord hath appeared in the streams of the Jordan"); after the Prayer of the Ninth Hour, the Dismissal. At table, dispensation for all foods (*κατάλυσις εἰς πάντα*) is observed, without fasting, because the Paramony strict fast is transferred to Saturday. At § 21: on Friday evening, Forefeast Vespers is sung according to the Menaion; after *Νῦν ἀπολύεις* ("Lord, now lettest Thou Thy servant depart", Luke 2:29–32), the Canon is sung, and after the Heirmos of Ode 9, the Trisagion Prayers, the Dismissal Troparion *Ἀπεστρέφετό ποτε* ("The river Jordan once turned back"), and Dismissal. At § 22: Saturday Matins opens following the Midnight Service, with the reading of the Psalter Kathismata, followed by the Canons of the Forefeast and of the Saints.

[^234]: Concurrence of the Sunday after the Lights with the Opening of the Lenten Triodion (Sunday of the Publican and Pharisee, January 11): Paschal Limits Rubrical Note; Combined Office: Saturday Great Vespers (§ 42), Sunday Matins (§ 43), and Divine Liturgy Opening (§ 44). Physical leaf p349 (printed page 334) and facing English leaf p350 (printed page 335) detail the rare concurrence of the earliest possible start of the Lenten Triodion with the Sunday after the Lights and Saint Theodosios the Cenobiarch. The rubrical note explains that Holy Pascha varies between March 22 and April 24; consequently the Triodion begins between January 11 and February 13 according to the Paschal Tables [Heortodromion]. Exapostilaria: Resurrectional, Saint, and Triodion. Praises: 4 Resurrectional and 4 Afterfeast (or of Saint if celebrated solemnly); "Glory": of Triodion; "Both now and ever": *Ὑπερευλογημένη ὑπάρχεις*; Great Doxology, and *Σήμερον σωτηρία*. At § 44, the Divine Liturgy opens with Typika and 4 Beatitudes of the Tone, and 4 hymns from Ode 6 of the festal Canon.

Scholarly Rubrical Table "Notes Concerning the Epistle and Gospel Readings" (Σημειώσεις περὶ Ἀποστόλου καὶ Εὐαγγελίου) resolving the movable Lucan and Matthaean leaps: Case 1: If one Sunday intervenes between Sunday after the Lights and the First Sunday of the Triodion: Epistle of 32nd Sunday (1 Timothy 4:9–15) and Gospel of Zacchaeus (15th Sunday of Luke, Luke 19:1–10). Case 2: If two Sundays intervene: First Sunday: Epistle 29th (Colossians 3:4–11), Gospel of Ten Lepers (12th Sunday of Luke, Luke 17:12–19); Second Sunday: Epistle 32nd, Gospel of Zacchaeus. Case 3: If three Sundays intervene: First Sunday: Epistle 29th, Gospel of Ten Lepers; Second Sunday: Epistle 32nd, Gospel of Zacchaeus; Third Sunday: Epistle 17th (2 Corinthians 6:16–7:1), Gospel of Canaanite Woman (17th Sunday of Matthew, Matthew 15:21–28). Case 4: If four Sundays intervene: First Sunday: Epistle 29th, Gospel of Ten Lepers; Second Sunday: Epistle 31st (1 Timothy 1:15–17), Gospel 13th of Luke (The Rich Ruler, Luke 18:18–27); Third Sunday: Epistle 32nd, Gospel of Zacchaeus; Fourth Sunday: Epistle 17th, Gospel of Canaanite Woman. Rubrical Note on Late Paschal Calculations (incipit): When Pascha is late and daily Liturgies lack Gospels after the Lucan cycle is completed before the Triodion begins, the remaining Matthaean weeks prior to the Elevation of the Holy Cross are counted and read; likewise for Epistles. Physical leaf p353 (printed page 338) and facing English leaf p354 (printed page 339) govern the delicate transition between the fixed Menaion cycle, the Lucan Gospel distribution, and the Triodion.

Textual and Hymnographical Note: The Heirmos of the Eighth Ode of the First Canon to Saint Euthymius, composed by Saint John of Damascus, appears in later printed editions as *Παῖδας εὐαγεῖς ἐν τῇ καμίνῳ* ("The holy Children in the furnace"); however, the alphabetical acrostic *Ὄλβος πέφηνας γηγενῶν Εὐθύμιε* ("A wealth to mortals hast thou appeared, O Euthymius") demands that the Eighth Ode begin with the letter *Y* (Upsilon). In the Venice 1533 Menaion of Stephanos Savios, the Heirmos begins *Υἱοὺς τῆς Σιὼν ἐν τῇ καμίνῳ* ("Sons of Zion in the furnace"), preserving the ancient and genuine poetic reading per the judgment of Patriarch Constantius I. Scholarly Rubrical Decree of Patriarch Constantine I the Byzantine concerning the Praises on Sundays: Explains the standard rubrical canon prescribing the Resurrectional Eothinon Doxastikon at "Glory..." at the Praises on ordinary Sundays of the year, but establishes that for the great Ecumenical Doctors Gregory the Theologian and John Chrysostom, out of just debt to their pastoral guidance and immortal writings illuminating the Catholic Church, when their commemorations coincide with a Sunday outside the Triodion, the Doxastikon of the Saint is chanted instead of the Eothinon, especially in the Great Church of Constantinople. Note on Footnote 3: When this commemoration occurs on a Sunday within the Triodion period, the Doxastikon of the Triodion is chanted at "Glory..." at the Praises. Physical leaf p365 (printed page 350) and facing English leaf p366 (printed page 351) record the patriarchal decrees governing the precedence of Doxastika at the Praises for the Great Hierarchs.

Ancient Patriarchal Ceremonial Rubric: In the Patriarchal Church of Constantinople, on the two feasts of Saint John Chrysostom (January 27 and November 13), when the Patriarch enters the church, he does not stand upon the central Patriarchal Throne, but in the Parathronion (side throne); upon the central Throne is enthroned the holy Icon of Chrysostom for veneration by the faithful; before the holy Icon stands the Patriarchal Dibamboulon (the great double-wick candelabrum), burning throughout the Divine Liturgy. § 4: Concurrence when Saint John Chrysostom falls on Sunday outside the Triodion: follows Saint Gregory (§§ 2, 3, 4). § 5: Concurrence when Saint John Chrysostom falls on Sunday within the Triodion: Saturday Great Vespers (4 Resurrectional Stichira, 2 of Triodion, 4 of Saint [on Meatfare Sunday: 3 of Triodion, 3 of Saint]; "Glory...": of Triodion; "Both now and ever...": first Theotokion of Tone; Little Entrance, Prokeimenon, Readings of Saint; Aposticha: Resurrectional Stichira, "Glory...": of Saint, "Both now and ever...": of Triodion; Dismissal Troparia: Resurrectional, Saint, Theotokion, Dismissal). § 6: Sunday Matins and Divine Liturgy within the Triodion: celebrated without alteration according to Saint Gregory (§§ 6, 7). § 7: Concurrence on the Saturday of Souls (Meatfare Saturday): office anticipated on Friday; Saturday of Souls celebrated on Saturday without transfer per Saint Gregory § 8. Note on Triodion Concurrence Range for Saint John Chrysostom (incipit): may occur from the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee until the Tuesday of Cheesefare Week... ## Cohort 39 Footnotes

[^250]: Feast of the Meeting of our Lord, God and Savior Jesus Christ (Hypapante, February 2) — Case I: Weekday Celebration (Conclusion of Matins: Ode 9 Megalynaria): Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p381 (printed page 366) and facing English leaf p382 (printed page 367). Following the initial Megalynarion *Ἀκατάληπτόν ἐστι* (inaugurated on leaf p380), the ninth ode of the Canon of the Meeting (composed by Saint Cosmas of Maiuma and embellished by the Byzantine sticheraric tradition) presents an alphabetical series of ten nine-syllable/twelve-syllable Megalynaria addressed alternately to the Theotokos, Christ, and the Righteous Symeon: (1) concluding verse of initial incipit: *Ἀγγέλοις καὶ βροτοῖς, Μητροπάρθενε ἁγνή* ["...unto angels and mortal men, O pure Virgin Mother"]; (2) *Ἀγκαλίζεται χερσίν, ὁ Πρεσβύτης Συμεών* ["Symeon the Elder takes in his arms the Maker of the Law and Master of all"]; (3) *Βουληθεὶς ὁ Πλαστουργός, ἵνα σώσῃ τὸν Ἀδάμ* ["The Creator, wishing to save Adam, took up His dwelling in thy pure and virgin womb"]; (4) *Γένος ἅπαν τῶν βροτῶν, μακαρίζει σε Ἁγνή* ["All the race of mortals blesseth thee, O Pure Virgin, and in faith glorifieth thee as Mother of God"]; (5) *Δεῦτε ἴδετε Χριστόν, τὸν Δεσπότην τοῦ παντός* ["Come and behold Christ, the Master of all, Whom Symeon beareth today in the temple"]; (6) *Ἐπιβλέπεις πρὸς τὴν γῆν, καὶ ποιεῖς τρέμειν αὐτήν* ["Thou lookest upon the earth and makest it tremble; how then can I, aged and weary, hold Thee in mine arms?"]; (7) *Ζήσας ἔτη Συμεών, ἕως εἶδε τὸν Χριστόν* ["Symeon lived for many years until he beheld Christ, and cried aloud unto Him: 'Now do I seek release'"]; (8) *Ἡ λαβὶς ἡ μυστική, ἡ τὸν ἄνθρακα Χριστόν* ["Thou art the mystic tongs, Mary, who hast conceived in thy womb Christ the live Coal" — drawing upon Isaiah 6:6]; (9) *Θέλων ἐνηνθρώπησας, ὁ προάναρχος Θεός* ["Of Thine own will Thou didst become man, O God without beginning, and art brought into the temple, forty days of age"]; (10) *Κατελθόντ’ ἐξ οὐρανοῦ, τὸν Δεσπότην τοῦ παντός* ["Symeon the priest received the Master of all, Who came down from heaven"]. Rubrical expansion of the 4 Troparia of Ode 9 to 6 with their proper appointed Megalynaria: Troparion 1 *Ἐν νόμῳ σκιᾷ* with Megalynarion *Λάμπρυνόν μου τὴν ψυχήν*; Troparion 2 with Megalynarion *Μητροπάρθενε ἁγνή, τί προσφέρεις*; Troparion 3 *Τοῖς πρὶν νεογνῶν* with Megalynarion *Νῦν ἀπόλυσιν ζητῶ*; Troparion 4 with Megalynarion *Ὃν οἱ ἄνω λειτουργοί*; Doxastikon (*Δόξα*) with Triadikon *Ἡ τῇ φύσει μὲν μονάς* ["O Thou Who art by nature One, but in Persons Three..."] paired with Megalynarion *Ἀπέδωκάς μοι*; and Theotokion (*Καὶ νῦν*) *Θεοτόκε ἡ ἐλπίς* ["O Theotokos, Hope of all Christians, shelter, guard, and preserve those whose hope is in thee..."] paired with Megalynarion *Ἱεροπρεπῶς ἀνθωμολογεῖτο*.

[^251]: Feast of the Meeting of our Lord Jesus Christ (Hypapante, February 2) — Matins Conclusion and Divine Liturgy (§ 3 incipit): Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p383 (printed page 368) and facing English leaf p384 (printed page 369). Matins concludes with the festal Katavasia of Ode 9 *Θεοτόκε ἡ ἐλπίς. . . Ἐν νόμῳ, σκιᾷ*; the Exapostilarion *Ἐν πνεύματι τῷ Ἱερῷ* ["The Elder, brought to the temple by the Spirit"] chanted thrice; at the Praises: the Stichira Prosomia *Νόμον τὸν ἐν γράμματι* ["Fulfilling the written Law"]; "Glory... Both now and ever...": *Ὁ ἐν χερσὶ πρεσβυτικαῖς* ["He Who in the arms of the Elder"]; Great Doxology, and Dismissal Troparion *Χαῖρε κεχαριτωμένη*. § 3: Divine Liturgy of the Feast: Celebration of the Three Proper Festal Antiphons drawn from Psalm 44 LXX (the royal Messianic epithalamium): First Antiphon: Psalm 44:2, 3 LXX (*Ἐξηρεύξατο ἡ καρδία μου λόγον ἀγαθόν... Ἡ γλῶσσά μου... Ἐξεχύθη χάρις... Διὰ τοῦτο εὐλόγησέ σε ὁ Θεὸς εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα*) with refrain *Ταῖς πρεσβείαις τῆς Θεοτόκου, Σῶτερ, σῶσον ἡμᾶς* ["Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, Savior, save us"], concluding with the full Father Paul Doxology Standard and general rubrical cross-reference to Protheoria p. 47; Second Antiphon: Psalm 44:4–7 LXX (*Περίζωσαι τὴν ῥομφαίαν σου... Καὶ ἔντεινε... Τὰ βέλη σου ἠκονημένα... Ῥάβδος εὐθύτητος...*) with proper festal Christological refrain: *Σῶσον ἡμᾶς, Υἱὲ Θεοῦ, ὁ ἐν ἀγκάλαις τοῦ Δικαίου Συμεὼν βασταχθείς, ψάλλοντάς Σοι, Ἀλληλούϊα* ["Save us, O Son of God, Who wast carried in the arms of the righteous Symeon, who sing to Thee: Alleluia"], concluding with "Glory... Both now and ever..." and the dogmatic hymn *Ὁ Μονογενὴς Υἱὸς καὶ Λόγος τοῦ Θεοῦ* ["Only-begotten Son and Word of God"].

Scholarly Note 1: Editorial note regarding the calendrical impossibility of the Apodosis of the Meeting occurring on a Sunday outside the Triodion period (under the Julian/Paschal calendar, Pascha falls between March 22 and April 25; the Triodion begins between January 11 and February 15; if February 9 falls on a Sunday, it is invariably within the Triodion, either as Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee, Prodigal Son, or Meatfare). § 11 incipit: Situation II: Concurrence when February 2 falls on a Sunday within the Triodion period: Saturday Great Vespers (at "Lord, I have cried": 4 Resurrectional Stichira, 2 of Triodion, and 4 of Feast [on Meatfare and Cheesefare Sundays: 4 Resurrectional, 3 of Triodion, 3 of Feast]; "Glory...": of Triodion; "Both now and ever...": of Feast; Little Entrance, Prokeimenon, Old Testament Readings; Aposticha: Resurrectional Stichira, "Glory...": of Triodion, "Both now and ever...": of Feast...).


## Cohort 40 Footnotes

[^255]: Feast of the Meeting of our Lord Jesus Christ (Hypapante, February 2) — Situation II Conclusion (§§ 12–13) and Sunday After the Feast Within the Triodion (§ 12 concl.): Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p391 (printed page 376) and facing English leaf p392 (printed page 377). Paragraph 12 completes the rubrical order for Sunday Matins when February 2 falls on a Sunday within the pre-Lenten Triodion period (Publican and Pharisee, Prodigal Son, Meatfare, or Cheesefare). Following the Triadic Canon and festal Litiya (with Triodion Doxastikon when appointed), the Hexapsalmos and "God is the Lord" (*Θεὸς Κύριος*) lead into the distribution of Kathismata across three Stichologiai: Resurrectional Kathismata combined with festal Kathismata *Χορὸς Ἀγγελικός* and *Ὁ ὢν σὺν τῷ Πατρί*, while the third festal Kathisma *Νηπιάζει δι’ ἐμέ* is sung twice. After the Hypakoe and Anavathmoi of the Tone, the Prokeimenon (Psalm 44:18 LXX) and festal Liturgy Gospel are proclaimed. The Canons of the Resurrection, Triodion, and Feast are chanted; after Ode 3, the Resurrectional Kontakion/Ikos and Kathismata; after Ode 6, Triodion Kontakion/Ikos, festal Menologion, and Triodion Synaxarion [Hypomnema]. Ode 9 features festal Megalynaria (*Ἀκατάληπτόν ἐστι*) with Troparia and Katavasia *Θεοτόκε ἡ ἐλπίς. . . Ἐν νόμῳ, σκιᾷ*. Praises combine 4 Resurrectional and 4 festal Stichira (modified to 3 Resurrectional, 2 Triodion, and 3 Feast on Meatfare and Cheesefare Sundays). Paragraph 13 establishes the Divine Liturgy order: festal Antiphons from Psalm 44 LXX with the Christological refrain *Σῶσον ἡμᾶς, Υἱὲ Θεοῦ, ὁ ἐν ἀγκάλαις τοῦ Δικαίου Συμεὼν βασταχθείς...* and Sunday Little Entrance Introit Psalm 97:2 LXX (*Ἐγνώρισε Κύριος* with *ὁ ἀναστὰς ἐκ νεκρῶν*); Dismissal Troparion *Χαῖρε κεχαριτωμένη*, Kontakion *Ὁ μήτραν παρθενικήν*, festal Epistle and Sunday Gospel, Ode 9 Megalynarion, Communion Verse Psalm 115:4 LXX, and the composite festal-resurrectional Dismissal.

Paragraph 16 inaugurates the order for the Apodosis of the Meeting when falling on a Sunday within the Triodion: Saturday Great Vespers combines 4 Resurrectional, 3 Triodion, and 3 festal Stichira at "Lord, I have cried", with Doxastikon of the Triodion and festal Theotokion; Aposticha Resurrectional with Triodion Doxastikon and festal Theotokion; Dismissal Troparion of the Resurrection and of the Feast twice.

[^257]: Apodosis of the Feast on a Sunday Within the Triodion (§§ 17–18) and Situation III: Concurrence with the Saturday of Souls (§ 19): Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p395 (printed page 380) and facing English leaf p396 (printed page 381). Paragraph 17 completes Sunday Matins for the festal Apodosis within the Triodion: after Psalm 50 LXX, Triadic Canon, and Triodion Litiya Idiomelon (if present), Kathismata across three Stichologiai (Resurrectional paired with festal); Canons of Resurrection, Triodion, and Feast; after Ode 3, Resurrectional Kontakion, Triodion and festal Kathismata; after Ode 6, Triodion Kontakion/Ikos and Menologion; Katavasiai *Χέρσον ἀβυσσοτόκον*, festal Megalynaria of Ode 9 (*Ἀκατάληπτόν ἐστι*) with Troparia and Katavasia *Θεοτόκε ἡ ἐλπίς. . . Ἐν νόμῳ, σκιᾷ*; Praises with 4 Resurrectional, 2 Triodion, and 3 festal Stichira; "Glory...": Triodion, "Both now and ever...": Feast (*Ὁ ἐν χερσὶ πρεσβυτικαῖς*). Paragraph 18 governs the Divine Liturgy: festal Antiphons from Psalm 44 LXX, Entrance Hymn Psalm 97:2 LXX with Sunday refrain, festal and Sunday Troparia/Kontakion, Sunday Epistle and Gospel, Ode 9 Megalynarion, and Communion Verse Psalm 115:4 LXX. Paragraph 19 resolves Situation III: when the Feast of the Meeting coincides with Meatfare Saturday (the Saturday of Souls): the ancestral rubrics of the ancient Typika and the authoritative rulings of Patriarchs Constantios I (1830–1834) and Gregory VI (1835–1840, 1867–1871) mandate that the entire Office of the Souls be transferred to the preceding Saturday (the Saturday before the Sunday of the Prodigal Son), so that the Feast of the Meeting may be celebrated on its own day without displacement or abbreviation according to the primary rubrics of §§ 1–3.

[^258]: Variable Calendrical Cases for the Apodosis of the Meeting (Cases 1–5) and Cheesefare Sunday Evening Vespers (§ 20 incipit): Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p397 (printed page 382) and facing English leaf p398 (printed page 383). The Typikon provides a rigorous 5-case combinatorial matrix for fixing the date of the Apodosis of the Meeting based upon its interaction with the movable pre-Lenten Triodion cycle: Case 1 [α΄]: Feast occurs between Sunday of Publican and Pharisee and Friday of that week -> Apodosis on regular 8th day (February 9); Case 2 [β΄]: Feast occurs between Saturday eve of Prodigal Son and Tuesday of Meatfare Week -> Apodosis celebrated early on Friday before the Saturday of Souls, because the ancestral Office of the Dead on Meatfare Saturday is inviolable and cannot be displaced for an Apodosis; Case 3 [γ΄]: Feast occurs between Wednesday of Meatfare Week and Saturday of Souls -> Apodosis postponed to Tuesday of Cheesefare Week; Case 4 [δ΄]: Feast occurs between Meatfare Sunday and Tuesday of Cheesefare Week -> Apodosis on Friday of Cheesefare Week; Case 5 [ε΄]: Feast occurs between Wednesday and Saturday of Cheesefare Week -> Apodosis celebrated on Cheesefare Sunday per §§ 16–18. When the Feast itself falls on Cheesefare Sunday (celebrated per §§ 11–13), the Apodosis is celebrated that same evening at Forgiveness Vespers (§ 20): "Lord, I have cried" combines 3 Stichira of the Feast and 3 of the Righteous Symeon; Great Prokeimenon *Μὴ ἀποστρέψῃς* (Psalm 68:18 LXX); Aposticha Idiomelon *Ἔλαμψεν ἡ χάρις σου* and festal Theotokion; proper festal Dismissal followed immediately by the Forgiveness rite without the customary priestly blessing *Δι’ εὐχῶν*.

[^259]: Cheesefare Sunday Evening Forgiveness Vespers Conclusion (§ 20 concl.) and Situation IV: Clean Monday Concurrence (§§ 21–22): Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p399 (printed page 384) and facing English leaf p400 (printed page 385). Paragraph 20 concludes the Forgiveness Vespers rite marking the opening of Great Lent: following the festal Dismissal of the Meeting (omitting *Δι’ εὐχῶν*), the Choirs chant the penitential Troparion *Θεοτόκε Παρθένε*, 40 *Κύριε ἐλέησον*, *Ὁ ὢν εὐλογητός*, the Prayer of Saint Ephrem with Great Prostrations [Metanias], the Deacon/Priest litany *Εὐξώμεθα ὑπὲρ εἰρήνης τοῦ κόσμου*, and the hymn *Πάντων προστατεύεις* while clergy and faithful exchange the kiss of peace and mutual forgiveness, formally concluding the Apodosis of the Feast. Paragraphs 21–22 formulate Situation IV: when February 2 coincides with Clean Monday (the first day of Great Lent): on Cheesefare Sunday evening at Vespers (§ 21), "Lord, I have cried" combines 3 Triodion fasting Idiomela (*Ἐγκρατείᾳ τὴν σάρκα*) and 3 festal Stichira (*Λέγε Συμεών*), Great Prokeimenon Psalm 133:1 LXX (*Ἰδοὺ δὴ εὐλογεῖτε τὸν Κύριον*), Old Testament Readings of the Feast, and festal Aposticha; on Clean Monday at Matins (§ 22), the festal Litiya and "God is the Lord" lead into Kathismata across three Stichologiai combining Triodion fasting hymns (*Τῆς νηστείας τῇ θείᾳ ἀπαρχῇ*, *Τὴν πάνσεπτον ἐγκράτειαν*) with festal Kathismata; Anavathmoi (Antiphon 1 of Tone 4), Prokeimenon (Psalm 44:18 LXX), and festal Morning Gospel; Canon to 6 with festal Megalynaria of Ode 9 (*Ἀκατάληπτόν ἐστι*) and festal Exapostilarion *Ἐν πνεύματι τῷ Ἱερῷ* thrice; Praises combining festal Stichira Prosomia *Νόμον τὸν ἐν γράμματι* and festal Doxastikon *Ὁ ἐν χερσὶ πρεσβυτικαῖς*, concluding with the Great Doxology.


## Cohort 41 Footnotes

[^260]: Feast of the Meeting of our Lord Jesus Christ (February 2) — Clean Monday Liturgy Conclusion (§ 23), Apodosis of the Meeting (§ 24), and February 10: Holy Hieromartyr Charalampos (Heading & Situation I, § 1): Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p401 (printed page 386) and facing English leaf p402 (printed page 387). Paragraph 23 completes the rubrical order for the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom when the Meeting coincides with Clean Monday (the first day of Great Lent): festal Antiphons from Psalm 44 LXX, Entrance Hymn Psalm 97:2 LXX, Dismissal Troparion *Χαῖρε κεχαριτωμένη*, Kontakion *Ὁ μήτραν παρθενικήν*, festal Epistle and Gospel, Ode 9 Megalynarion *Θεοτόκε ἡ ἐλπίς . . . Ἐν νόμῳ σκιᾷ*, Communion Verse Psalm 115:4 LXX (*Ποτήριον σωτηρίου*), and proper festal Dismissal with Christological incipit *Ὁ ἐν ἀγκάλαις τοῦ Δικαίου Συμεὼν βασταχθῆναι καταδεξάμενος διὰ τὴν ἡμῶν σωτηρίαν...*; the dispensation permitting wine and oil on Clean Monday is explicitly confirmed. Paragraph 24 establishes the Apodosis of the Meeting on Clean Monday evening: Vespers is chanted without variation per § 1, omitting Old Testament Readings; Dismissal Troparion thrice, Dismissal, and formal Apodosis; Great Compline is omitted out of festal reverence, replaced by Small Compline recited in cells in city churches, while monasteries observe their founder Typika (per Patriarchs Constantios I and Gregory VI). The general occurrence rule notes the Meeting may fall between the Sunday of the Publican and Pharisee and Clean Monday per the 34 Paschalia table. Commemoration of February 10 (Holy Hieromartyr Charalampos) begins with Situation I (§ 1): occurrence on any weekday from the beginning of the Triodion through Cheesefare Friday is governed entirely by the Menaion; Matins Katavasiai *Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου*; Liturgy Kontakion *Προστασία τῶν Χριστιανῶν*.

[^261]: February 10: Holy Hieromartyr Charalampos — Situation II: Concurrence on Sunday Outside the Triodion (§ 2) and Situation III: Concurrence on First Four Sundays of the Triodion (§§ 3–4, § 5 incipit): Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p403 (printed page 388) and facing English leaf p404 (printed page 389). Paragraph 2 governs Situation II when February 10 falls on a Sunday outside the Triodion cycle: the Saint's Office is combined with the Resurrectional Office as customary for ranked feasts on Sundays; Praises Doxastikon is the Eothinon Gospel Sticheron; Epistle of the Saint, Gospel of the Sunday; Kontakion *Προστασία τῶν Χριστιανῶν*; Communion Verse Psalm 148:1 LXX (*Αἰνεῖτε*). Paragraphs 3–5 govern Situation III when the feast occurs on one of the first four Sundays of the Triodion (Publican and Pharisee, Prodigal Son, Meatfare, or Cheesefare): on Saturday evening at Great Vespers (§ 3), 10 Stichira at "Lord, I have cried" combine 4 Resurrectional, 3 Triodion, and 3 of the Saint; Doxastikon of the Triodion, first Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone; Old Testament Readings of the Saint; Aposticha Resurrectional with Doxastikon of the Saint and Theotokion of the Triodion; Dismissal Troparia of Resurrection, Saint, and Theotokion. On Sunday at Matins (§ 4): after Psalm 50 LXX, Triadic Canon, festal Litiya of the Saint, Hexapsalmos, and "God is the Lord"; Psalter and Polyeleos; Resurrectional Kathismata with 3rd Kathisma of the Saint; Evlogitaria, Hypakoe, Anavathmoi of the Tone, Prokeimenon; Canons of Resurrection, Triodion, and Saint; after Ode 3, Resurrectional Kontakion/Ikos and Kathismata of the Saint, Triodion, and Theotokion; after Ode 6, Triodion Kontakion/Ikos, Menologion of the Saint, and Sunday Synaxarion; Triodion Katavasiai; Eothinon Gospel sequence; *Τὴν τιμιωτέραν*; Exapostilaria of Resurrection, Triodion, Saint, and Theotokion; Praises with 3 Resurrectional, 2 Triodion, and 3 Saint Stichira; Triodion Doxastikon, *Ὑπερευλογημένη*, Great Doxology, and *Σήμερον σωτηρία*. Paragraph 5 begins the Divine Liturgy order with Typika and Beatitudes (4 of Tone, 4 of Ode 6 of Triodion Canon).

[^262]: February 10: Holy Hieromartyr Charalampos — Situation III Divine Liturgy Conclusion (§ 5 concl.) and Situation IV: Anticipated Celebration on Friday Before Saturday of Souls (§§ 6–7, § 8 incipit): Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p405 (printed page 390) and facing English leaf p406 (printed page 391). Paragraph 5 concludes the Divine Liturgy for Situation III: following the Little Entrance, Troparia of the Resurrection, the Saint, the Temple Patron, and Triodion Kontakion; Epistle of the Saint, Sunday Gospel; *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*; Communion Verse Psalm 148:1 LXX (*Αἰνεῖτε*), and Dismissal. Paragraphs 6–8 resolve Situation IV when February 10 coincides with Meatfare Saturday (Saturday of Souls): the commemoration of St. Charalampos is anticipated and celebrated on Friday together with the Apodosis of the Meeting so as not to displace the ancestral Office of the Dead. On Thursday evening at Great Vespers (§ 6): at "Lord, I have cried", 3 festal Idiomela of the Meeting and 3 Prosomia of the Saint; Doxastikon of the Saint, festal Theotokion; Old Testament Readings of the Saint; Aposticha festal Idiomela with Doxastikon of the Saint and festal Theotokion; Dismissal Troparia of the Feast, the Saint, and the Feast again. On Friday at Matins (§ 7): after Psalm 50 LXX, Litiya of the Saint, Trisagion, festal Troparion, and Hexapsalmos; "God is the Lord" with Troparia as at Vespers; Psalter and Polyeleos of the Saint; Kathismata of the Saint with festal Kathismata in place of Theotokia; Anavathmoi (Antiphon 1 of Tone 4), Prokeimenon Psalm 91:13 LXX (*Δίκαιος ὡς φοῖνιξ*), festal Morning Gospel; Psalm 50 LXX, *Ταῖς τοῦ Ἀθλοφόρου* ("Through the intercessions of the prize-bearing champion"), Idiomelon *Φάλαγγα θεοστεφῆ*; Canons of Feast and Saint; Kathismata after Ode 3; Kontakion/Ikos and Menologion of the Saint after Ode 6; Katavasiai *Χέρσον ἀβυσσοτόκον* and Ode 9 Megalynaria of the Meeting (*Ἀκατάληπτόν ἐστι*); Exapostilaria of Feast, Saint, and Feast; Praises (3 Feast, 3 Saint), Doxastikon of the Saint, festal Theotokion, Great Doxology, and festal Dismissal Troparion. Paragraph 8 opens the Divine Liturgy with festal Antiphons and Entrance Hymn Psalm 97:2 LXX (*Ἐγνώρισε Κύριος*).

Paragraph 9 establishes Situation V when February 10 occurs on Wednesday or Friday of Cheesefare Week: celebrated entirely per the Menaion without Triodion fasting Idiomela; Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom is celebrated; Epistle and Gospel of the Saint; Katavasiai *Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου*; Kontakion *Προστασία τῶν Χριστιανῶν*. Paragraphs 10–11 govern Situation VI when February 10 coincides with Cheesefare Saturday (Commemoration of the Holy Ascetic Monks and Nuns): on Friday evening at Great Vespers (§ 10), at "Lord, I have cried", 3 Prosomia of the Saint and 3 of the Venerable Fathers; Doxastikon of the Saint (*Ἔθυσας τῷ Θεῷ*), Dogmatic Theotokion *Ὁ διὰ σὲ Θεοπάτωρ*; Old Testament Readings of the Saint; Aposticha Prosomia of the Saint with Doxastikon of the Fathers and Theotokion *Δέσποινα πρόσδεξαι*; Dismissal Troparia of the Saint, the Venerable Fathers, and Theotokion. On Saturday at Matins (§ 11): Morning Gospel of the Saint; Canons of the Theotokos (*Ὑγρὰν διοδεύσας*), the Saint, and the Venerable Fathers; Katavasiai *Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου*; Exapostilaria of the Saint, the Fathers, and Theotokion; Praises (3 Saint, 3 Fathers), Doxastikon of the Saint (*Ὥσπερ ἀλάβαστρον πλῆρες*), Theotokion *Μακαρίζομέν σε*, Great Doxology, Dismissal Troparion of the Saint; Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom with Kontakion *Προστασία τῶν Χριστιανῶν*, Epistle of the Saint, Gospel of the Fathers, and Communion Verse Psalm 111:6 LXX (*Εἰς μνημόσυνον αἰώνιον*).

Paragraph 12 governs Situation VII when February 10 falls on Clean Monday (first day of Great Lent): the entire Office of the Saint is transferred and celebrated on Cheesefare Sunday according to the order set forth in §§ 3–5. Paragraphs 13–14 inaugurate Situation VIII when February 10 falls on Tuesday of the First Week of Great Lent: on Monday morning at Vespers (§ 13), at "Lord, I have cried", 3 Triodion Prosomia and 3 of the Saint to melodic model *Ἔδωκας σημείωσιν* ("Thou hast given a sign", Tone 4); Doxastikon and Theotokion Prosomia; "O Gladsome Light" and Triodion Old Testament Readings of the day; Aposticha fasting Idiomelon twice with Martyrikon; Dismissal Troparia *Θεοτόκε Παρθένε* with prostrations. On Monday evening at Great Compline (§ 13 concl.): at the solemn choral Verses of *Παναγία Δέσποινα Θεοτόκε*, the proper petition for St. Charalampos is inserted after that of the Temple Patron: "O holy, glorious Hieromartyr and Wonderworker Charalampos, intercede for us sinners."
