Paragraph 22 details the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom: Typika and Beatitudes (4 from Ode 3 of the Menaion Canon, 4 from Ode 6 of the Triodion Canon); Little Entrance with Dismissal Troparia of the Saints of the day, Temple Patron, and Kontakion *Προστασία τῶν Χριστιανῶν*; Epistle of the Saint, Gospel of the day (First Saturday of Lent); *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*; Communion Verse Psalm 111:6 LXX (*Εἰς μνημόσυνον*), *Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*, and Dismissal.

[^268]: February 10: Holy Hieromartyr Charalampos — Situation XIII: Concurrence on the First Sunday of Great Lent (Sunday of Orthodoxy) (§§ 23–25): Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p417 (printed page 402) and facing English leaf p418 (printed page 403). Paragraph 23 establishes the order for Great Vespers on Saturday evening: after Introductory Psalm 103 LXX and Psalter (Kathisma 1), at "Lord, I have cried", 10 Stichira combine 4 Resurrectional, 3 Triodion, and 3 Prosomia of the Saint; Doxastikon of the Triodion, first Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone; Little Entrance, "O Gladsome Light", Prokeimenon, and Old Testament Readings of the Saint; Aposticha Resurrectional with Doxastikon of the Saint and Theotokion of the Triodion; Dismissal Troparia of the Resurrection, the Triodion (*Τὴν ἄχραντον εἰκόνα σου*, "Thy pure icon do we venerate", Tone 2), the Saint, the Theotokion, and Dismissal. On Sunday at Matins (§ 24): after Psalm 50 LXX, Triadic Canon, Litiya of the Saint, Doxastikon of the Saint, Theotokion of the Triodion *Χαίρετε προφῆται τίμιοι* ("Rejoice, ye honored prophets"), and Triadic hymns *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*; Trisagion Prayers, Dismissal Troparion *Τὴν ἄχραντον εἰκόνα σου*, and Hexapsalmos; "God is the Lord" with Troparia as at Vespers; Psalter and Polyeleos; Resurrectional Kathismata, 3rd Kathisma of the Saint with Theotokion; Evlogitaria, Hypakoe, Anavathmoi of the Tone, Prokeimenon; Canons of Resurrection, Triodion, and Saint; after Ode 3, Resurrectional Kontakion/Ikos and Kathismata of the Triodion, the Saint, and Theotokion; after Ode 6, Triodion Kontakion/Ikos, Menologion of the Saint, and Synaxarion of the Triodion; Katavasiai *Θαλάσσης τὸ ἐρυθραῖον πέλαγος* ("The Red Sea's abyssal depth"), and Sunday Morning Gospel sequence; *Τὴν τιμιωτέραν*; Exapostilaria of Resurrection, Triodion, Saint, and Theotokion; Praises with 2 Resurrectional, 3 Triodion, and 3 Saint Stichira; Triodion Doxastikon, *Ὑπερευλογημένη*, Great Doxology, and *Σήμερον σωτηρία*. Paragraph 25 details the Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great: Sunday Antiphons of Orthodoxy per the Horologion; Little Entrance with Dismissal Troparia of the Resurrection, the Triodion, the Saint, the Temple Patron, and Kontakion *Τῇ ὑπερμάχῳ* ("To thee, the champion leader"); Epistle and Gospel of the Sunday of Orthodoxy; Communion Verse Psalm 148:1 LXX (*Αἰνεῖτε τὸν Κύριον*), *Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*, and Dismissal.

[^269]: February 10: Holy Hieromartyr Charalampos — Situation XIV: Monday & Tuesday of Second Week of Lent (§§ 26–28); Triodion Feasibility Envelope; and February 24 Heading: Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p419 (printed page 404) and facing English leaf p420 (printed page 405). Paragraph 26 resolves concurrence on Monday of the Second Week of Great Lent: on Sunday evening at Vespers, the penitential Katanyktika of the Tone are omitted; at "Lord, I have cried", 10 Stichira combine 3 cycle Prosomia from the Triodion *Δίδου μοι κατάνυξιν* ("Grant me compunction") and two others, and 3 Prosomia of St. Charalampos *Ὅλος ἐκ νεότητος* ("Wholly from thy youth"); Doxastikon of the Saint *Ἔθυσας τῷ Θεῷ*, and Prosomion Theotokion; Little Entrance, "O Gladsome Light", and Great Prokeimenon Psalm 60:6 LXX (*Ἔδωκας κληρονομίαν*, "Thou hast given an inheritance") chanted thrice with its verses; at the Aposticha, the Triodion Idiomelon *Δεῦτε ἐκκαθάρωμεν* ("Come, let us cleanse ourselves") is chanted slowly twice, with the Martyrikon; Doxastikon of the Saint *Τὸν νοερὸν ἀδάμαντα* ("The noetic adamant"), Theotokion *Ἐκ παντοίων κινδύνων* ("From perils of every kind"), *Νῦν ἀπολύεις*, Trisagion Prayers, Dismissal Troparion of the Saint, Theotokion, and Dismissal; the solemn kneeling troparion *Θεοτόκε Παρθένε* is omitted because the Presanctified Liturgy is celebrated on Monday morning. On Monday morning (§ 27): Matins from Litiya to Doxology and the Hours follow § 14; at Vespers, after Psalm 103 LXX and Kathisma 18, at "Lord, I have cried", 10 Stichira combine the Aposticha Idiomelon *Τὴν σωματικὴν νηστείαν* ("Keeping bodily fasting", twice), Martyrikon, 3 cycle Prosomia *Τίνι ὡμοιώθης ταλαίπωρε* ("Unto what hast thou become likened, wretched soul"), and 4 Prosomia of the Saint *Ἔδωκας τὸ σῶμά σου* ("Thou didst surrender thy body"); Doxastikon of the Saint *Φάλαγγα θεοστεφῆ*, Prosomion Theotokion; Little Entrance with the Gospel Book, "O Gladsome Light", Triodion Readings, *Κατευθυνθήτω*, and Artoklasia; after *Πλούσιοι ἐπτώχευσαν*, Epistle and Gospel of the Saint, and the rest of the Presanctified Liturgy; Communion Verse Psalm 111:6 LXX (*Εἰς μνημόσυνον*), *Εὐλογήσω τὸν Κύριον*, and Dismissal. Paragraph 28 governs Tuesday of the Second Week per §§ 13–15. The concluding rubrical note outlines the occurrence envelope of St. Charalampos: falling between the Sunday of the Publican and Pharisee and Tuesday of the Second Week of Great Lent per the Heortodromion table. The monument then introduces the feast of February 24: First and Second Finding of the Precious Head of the Honorable Glorious Prophet, Forerunner and Baptist John (*Ἡ α΄ καὶ β΄ εὕρεσις τῆς τιμίας κεφαλῆς τοῦ Προδρόμου*).


## Cohort 43 Footnotes

[^270]: February 24: First and Second Finding of the Precious Head of the Forerunner — Situations I through V (§§ 1–5): Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p421 (printed page 406) and facing English leaf p422 (printed page 407). Paragraph 1 establishes Situation I: when February 24 falls on any weekday (Monday through Saturday) between Thursday of the Week of the Prodigal Son and Friday of Cheesefare Week, the service is chanted entirely as set forth in the Menaion. Paragraph 2 governs Situation II: when the feast concurs with the Saturday of Souls (Meatfare Saturday, the universal commemoration of all deceased Orthodox Christians), the service of the Forerunner is transferred and chanted in advance on Friday alongside the commemoration of the Holy Hieromartyr Polycarp of Smyrna (February 23), whereas the Saturday of Souls remains immoveable on Saturday. Paragraph 3 governs Situation III: when the feast falls on Meatfare Sunday or Cheesefare Sunday, the order of service conforms exactly to the typikon rubrics governing the commemoration of the Holy Hieromartyr Charalampos (February 10) under Situation Three, Paragraphs 3, 4, and 5 (combining Resurrectional hymns, Triodion propers, and feast hymns). Paragraph 4 governs Situation IV: when the feast falls on Cheesefare Saturday (the commemoration of all the Holy Ascetic Fathers and Mothers), the service follows the order of St. Charalampos under Situation Six, Paragraphs 10 and 11. Paragraph 5 governs Situation V: when February 24 falls on Clean Monday (the first day of the Great Fast), the service of the Forerunner cannot be celebrated on a strict Lenten aliturgical day and is chanted in advance on Cheesefare Sunday according to the order of St. Charalampos under Situation Three, Paragraphs 3, 4, and 5.

[^271]: February 24: First and Second Finding of the Precious Head of the Forerunner — Situation VI: Concurrence on Tuesday of the First Week of Great Lent (§§ 6–8): Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p423 (printed page 408) and facing English leaf p424 (printed page 409). Paragraph 6 details the service of Vespers celebrated on Clean Monday morning: after Introductory Psalm 103 LXX, at "Lord, I have cried", 6 Stichira combine 3 cycle Prosomia from the Triodion and 3 of the Forerunner (*Χαίροις ἡ ἱερὰ κεφαλή*, "Rejoice, O sacred head"); Doxastikon and Prosomion Theotokion; "O Gladsome Light", Old Testament Readings of the Triodion; at the Aposticha, the Triodion Idiomelon is chanted twice and the Martyrikon once, followed by "Glory... Both now and ever...", Theotokion, "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart" (Luke 2:29), Trisagion Prayers, the kneeling troparion *Θεοτόκε Παρθένε* ("O Virgin Theotokos"), and Dismissal. Paragraph 7 details Tuesday Matins and the Hours: after the Hexapsalmos, at "God is the Lord", the Dismissal Troparion of the Forerunner *Ἐκ γῆς ἀνατείλασα* ("Having arisen from the earth", Tone 4) is chanted twice, followed by the Theotokion; Psalter and Kathismata in order; Anavathmoi (Tone 4, First Antiphon); Prokeimenon Psalm 131:17 LXX (*Ἐκεῖ ἐξανατελῶ κέρας τῷ Δαβίδ*); "Let every breath praise the Lord" and the Morning Gospel (Luke 7:17–30); Psalm 50 LXX read plainly, *Ταῖς τοῦ σοῦ Προδρόμου* ("Through the intercessions of Thy Forerunner"), *Ταῖς τῆς Θεοτόκου*, Psalm 50:3 LXX, and the Idiomelon *Ἡ τῶν θείων ἐννοιῶν* ("The divine treasure"); Canons: the Theotokos Canon *Ὑγρὰν διοδεύσας* and the Canon of the Forerunner; Katavasiai *Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου*; after "More honorable than the Cherubim", the Exapostilarion of the Forerunner and Theotokion; at the Praises, 4 Prosomia *Ἤνοιξε Προπύλαια* ("He opened the fore-doors"), Doxastikon *Τὴν πανσεβάσμιον κάραν* ("The all-venerable head"), Theotokion *Θεοτόκε σὺ εἶ ἡ ἄμπελος* ("O Theotokos, thou art the true vine"), Great Doxology, and Dismissal Troparion; plain Hours are read with the Troparion and Kontakion *Προφῆτα Θεοῦ καὶ Πρόδρομε* ("O Prophet of God and Forerunner"); at the Ninth Hour, the Beatitudes and penitential troparia are chanted per St. Charalampos Situation Eight, Paragraph 14. Paragraph 8 details the Divine Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts celebrated on Tuesday: at Vespers, Kathisma 18 (Psalms 119–133 LXX), 10 Stichira at "Lord, I have cried" (Triodion Aposticha Idiomelon twice, Martyrikon, 3 Triodion Prosomia, 4 Forerunner *Δεῦτε τὴν τιμίαν κεφαλήν*, "Come, let us honor the precious head"), Doxastikon *Ἡ τῶν θείων ἐννοιῶν*, Stavrotheotokion; Little Entrance with the Gospel Book, "O Gladsome Light", Triodion Readings, *Κατευθυνθήτω* (Psalm 140:2 LXX), Epistle 2 Corinthians 4:6–15, Gospel Matthew 11:2–15, *Νῦν αἱ δυνάμεις*, Communion Verse Psalm 111:6 LXX (*Εἰς μνημόσυνον*), Psalm 33:2 LXX, and Dismissal.

[^272]: February 24: First and Second Finding of the Precious Head of the Forerunner — Situations VII and VIII (§§ 9–11): Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p425 (printed page 410) and facing English leaf p426 (printed page 411). Paragraph 9 governs Situation VII: when February 24 falls on Wednesday, Thursday, or Friday of the First Week of Great Lent, the typikon order follows the model of St. Charalampos under Situations Nine and Eleven, Paragraphs 16 and 18 (the printed Greek text exhibits a numerical reference citation to §§ 12 and 16, which corresponds structurally to the respective Wednesday and Friday rubrics of the St. Charalampos section). Paragraph 10 governs Situation VIII: when the feast falls on Saturday of the First Week of Great Lent (Theodore Saturday, commemorating the Miracle of the Kolyva wrought by the Great Martyr Theodore the Recruit): on Friday morning at Vespers, 10 Stichira at "Lord, I have cried" combine the day's Idiomelon *Δεῦτε πιστοὶ ἐπεργασώμεθα* ("Come, ye faithful, let us work", twice), 4 Prosomia of the Forerunner, and 4 of St. Theodore; Doxastikon of St. Theodore *Ὀργάνῳ χρησάμενος* ("Using the apostate emperor as his tool"), and the first Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone occurring; Little Entrance with the censer, "O Gladsome Light", Old Testament Readings of the Triodion, *Κατευθυνθήτω*, *Νῦν αἱ δυνάμεις*, Communion Verse Psalm 33:9 LXX (*Γεύσασθε καὶ ἴδετε*, "O taste and see"), Psalm 33:2 LXX, and Dismissal. Paragraph 11 details Saturday Matins and the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom: after the Midnight Office and Hexapsalmos, "God is the Lord" with Dismissal Troparia of the Forerunner, St. Theodore (*Μεγάλα τὰ τῆς πίστεως*), and Theotokion; Kathismata of both Saints; Tone 4 Anavathmoi (Antiphon 1); Prokeimenon Psalm 131:17 LXX; Morning Gospel of the Forerunner (Luke 7:17–30); Psalm 50 LXX, *Ταῖς τοῦ σοῦ Προδρόμου*, *Ταῖς τῆς Θεοτόκου*, Psalm 50:3 LXX, and Idiomelon *Ἡ τῶν θείων ἐννοιῶν*; Canons: Triodion Ode 1 and Forerunner; after Ode 3, Kontakion and Ikos of the Forerunner, and Kathismata of the Menaion and Triodion with Theotokia; after Ode 6, Kontakion and Ikos of the Triodion, Menologion of the Forerunner, and Synaxarion of the Triodion; Katavasiai *Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου*; after "More honorable than the Cherubim", Exapostilaria of the Forerunner, St. Theodore, and Theotokion; at the Praises, 3 Prosomia of the Forerunner and 3 of St. Theodore; Doxastikon of the Forerunner, Theotokion, Great Doxology, and Dismissal Troparion of the Forerunner; Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom with Kontakion *Προστασία τῶν χριστιανῶν*, Epistle of the Triodion (Hebrews 1:1–12), Gospel of the Forerunner (Matthew 11:2–15), Communion Verse Psalm 111:6 LXX (*Εἰς μνημόσυνον*), and Dismissal.

[^273]: February 24: First and Second Finding of the Precious Head of the Forerunner — Situation IX and Situation X Opening (§§ 12–14): Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p427 (printed page 412) and facing English leaf p428 (printed page 413). Paragraph 12 governs Situation IX: when February 24 concurs with the First Sunday of Great Lent (Sunday of Orthodoxy), the order follows the rubrics for St. Charalampos under Situation Thirteen, Paragraphs 23–25. Footnote 2 of the Greek source text notes an important historical witness: the Typikon of the Great Church compiled under Emperor Constantine VII Porphyrogenitus (*Τὸ τοῦ Κωνσταντίνου Τυπικὸν*) transfers the feast of the Forerunner to the Saturday of the Second Week of Great Lent (falling on March 1 or 2 in that calendar configuration) to prevent overshadowing the Sunday of Orthodoxy. Paragraph 13 establishes Situation X: when February 24 falls on the Second Sunday of Great Lent (commemoration of St. Gregory Palamas, Archbishop of Thessalonica): on Saturday evening at Great Vespers, after Introductory Psalm 103 LXX and Kathisma 1 of the Psalter, at "Lord, I have cried", 10 Stichira combine 4 Resurrectional of the Tone, 3 of the Forerunner, and 3 of St. Gregory; Doxastikon of the Triodion, and first Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone; Little Entrance, "O Gladsome Light", and Saturday evening Prokeimenon; at the Aposticha, the Resurrectional Stichira, Doxastikon of the Forerunner *Ἡ τῶν θείων ἐννοιῶν*, Theotokion *Ὢ θαύματος καινοῦ* ("O strange marvel"), and Dismissal Troparia of the Resurrection, the Forerunner, St. Gregory, and Theotokion. Paragraph 14 details Sunday Matins: after Psalm 50 LXX, the Triadic Canon and hymns *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*, Trisagion Prayers, penitential troparia *Ἐλέησον ἡμᾶς*, Hexapsalmos, and "God is the Lord" with Troparia as at Vespers; Psalter and Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX); Resurrectional Kathismata, 3rd Stichologia Kathisma of the Forerunner with Theotokion; Evlogitaria, Hypakoe, Anavathmoi of the Tone, Prokeimenon; Canons of the Resurrection, the Forerunner, and St. Gregory; after Ode 3, Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos, followed by Kathismata of the Triodion, the Forerunner, and Theotokion; after Ode 6, Kontakion and Ikos of the Triodion, Menologion of the Forerunner, and Synaxarion of the Triodion; Katavasiai *Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου*; Sunday Morning Gospel sequence; *Τὴν τιμιωτέραν*; Exapostilaria of the Resurrection, Triodion, Forerunner, St. Gregory, and Theotokion; at the Praises, 3 Resurrectional Stichira, 3 of the Forerunner, and 2 of St. Gregory; Doxastikon of the Triodion *Τοῖς ἐν σκότει ἁμαρτημάτων* ("Unto those who walk in the darkness of sins"), Theotokion *Ὑπερευλογημένη*, Great Doxology, and *Σήμερον σωτηρία*.

[^274]: February 24: First and Second Finding of the Precious Head of the Forerunner — Situation X Conclusion (§ 15), Situation XI (§ 16), Concurrence Rules (§ 17), and Festal Occurrence Envelope: Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p429 (printed page 414) and facing English leaf p430 (printed page 415). Paragraph 15 concludes Situation X: at the Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great on the Second Sunday of Great Lent, Typika and Beatitudes (4 of the Tone, 4 from Ode 6 of the Triodion Canon); after the Little Entrance, Dismissal Troparia of the Resurrection, the Forerunner, St. Gregory Palamas, the Patron Saint of the Temple, and Kontakion *Τῇ ὑπερμάχῳ* ("To thee, the champion leader"); Epistle of the Forerunner and Gospel of the Sunday (Mark 2:1–12), Liturgy of St. Basil the Great, Communion Verse Psalm 148:1 LXX (*Αἰνεῖτε τὸν Κύριον*), and Dismissal. Paragraph 16 establishes Situation XI: when February 24 falls on the Third Sunday of Great Lent (Veneration of the Precious and Life-Giving Cross): on Saturday at Vespers, Kathisma 1, 10 Stichira at "Lord, I have cried" (4 Resurrectional, 3 Cross, 3 Forerunner), Doxastikon of the Triodion, first Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone; at the Aposticha, Resurrectional Stichira, Doxastikon of the Forerunner, and Triodion Theotokion *Ὁ συμμαχήσας Κύριε* ("O Lord, Who didst aid gentle David"); Dismissal Troparia of the Resurrection, the Forerunner, and the Holy Cross (*Σῶσον Κύριε τὸν λαόν σου*, Tone 1). On Sunday morning at Matins: "God is the Lord" with Troparia as at Vespers; Kathismata in order; 3rd Stichologia Kathisma of the Forerunner and Cross Kathisma *Ἐν Παραδείσῳ μὲν τὸ πρίν*; Canons of the Triodion (Cross) and the Forerunner; after Ode 3, Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos, and Kathismata of the Forerunner and Triodion; Exapostilaria of the Resurrection, Triodion, and Forerunner with Theotokion; at the Praises, 4 Resurrectional Stichira and 4 of the Holy Cross (the Praises of the Forerunner being omitted to preserve the primacy of the Cross veneration rite); Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great: after Little Entrance, Troparia of Resurrection, Cross, Forerunner, and Temple Patron; Epistle of the Forerunner and Gospel of the Sunday (Mark 8:34–9:1); Communion Verse Psalm 4:7 LXX (*Ἐσημειώθη ἐφ᾿ ἡμᾶς τὸ φῶς τοῦ προσώπου σου, Κύριε*), and Dismissal. Paragraph 17 provides the general rule for Monday occurrences during the Second, Third, or Fourth Weeks of Great Lent, directing the celebrant to St. Charalampos Situation Fourteen, Paragraphs 26 and 27, and indicating that all remaining unstated weekday occurrences likewise resolve according to the general rubrics established for St. Charalampos. The concluding rubric summarizes the liturgical occurrence envelope: according to the Paschal calculation table (Heortodromion), February 24 can fall within the Triodion season between Thursday of the Week of the Prodigal Son and Tuesday of the Fourth Week of Great Lent.

The rubric notes that on Thursday of the Second and Third Weeks, the Aposticha Idiomelon is chanted twice, Martyrikon once, 3 sequence Prosomia, and 4 of the Martyrs.

[^277]: March 9: Commemoration of the Holy Forty Martyrs of Sebastea — Situation II Conclusion, Situation III (§ 6), and Situation IV (§ 7): Concurrence on Friday or Saturday of the First Week of Great Lent. Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p435 (printed page 420) and facing English leaf p436 (printed page 421). Paragraph 6 establishes Situation III: when the feast falls on Friday of the First Week, on Thursday morning Vespers is chanted as in Situation I, Paragraph 1, and on Friday morning Matins and the Hours follow Situation I, Paragraph 2; at Friday Vespers, after Kathisma 18, 10 Stichira at "Lord, I have cried" combine the Idiomelon *Δεῦτε πιστοὶ* twice, 4 Prosomia of the Martyrs, and 4 of the Great Martyr Theodore the Recruit; Doxastikon *Ἐν ᾠδαῖς ἀσμάτων*, first Theotokion of the Tone occurring, Little Entrance with the Holy Gospel Book, "O Gladsome Light", Readings, *Κατευθυνθήτω*, Epistle and Gospel of the Martyrs, Presanctified Liturgy with Communion Verse Psalm 111:6 LXX, and Dismissal. On Friday evening, the Ikoi of the Akathist Hymn (First Stasis) are read with Kontakion *Πίστιν Χριστοῦ* ("Thy faith in Christ was like a breastplate"). The rubric notes that on Friday of the Second, Third, and Fourth Weeks the same order is kept, omitting the Stichira of St. Theodore and chanting the day's Idiomelon twice, 4 Martyrika, and 4 of the Martyrs. Paragraph 7 establishes Situation IV: when the feast falls on Saturday of the First Week (Theodore Saturday, commemorating the Miracle of the Kolyva): on Friday morning at Vespers, after Kathisma 18, 10 Stichira combine *Δεῦτε πιστοὶ* twice, 4 of the Martyrs, and 4 of St. Theodore; Doxastikon of St. Theodore *Ὀργάνῳ χρησάμενος*, first Theotokion of the Tone, Little Entrance with the censer, "O Gladsome Light", Readings, *Κατευθυνθήτω*, Presanctified Liturgy with Communion Verse Psalm 33:9 LXX (*Γεύσασθε καὶ ἴδετε*), and Dismissal.

[^278]: March 9: Commemoration of the Holy Forty Martyrs of Sebastea — Situation IV Conclusion and Situation V Opening (§§ 8–9): Concurrence on Theodore Saturday and Sundays 1 through 5 of Great Lent. Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p437 (printed page 422) and facing English leaf p438 (printed page 423). The conclusion of Situation IV notes that on Friday evening the Ikoi of the Theotokos (First Stasis of the Akathist) are read with Kontakion *Πᾶσαν στρατιάν* ("Every army"). On Saturday morning, Matins and the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom are celebrated according to St. Charalampos Situation Twelve, Paragraph 21, with Triodion Epistle (Hebrews 1:1–12), Gospel of the Martyrs, and Communion Verse Psalm 111:6 LXX. Paragraph 8 establishes Situation V: when the feast falls on the First, Second, Third, Fourth, or Fifth Sunday of Great Lent: on Saturday evening at Great Vespers, after Kathisma 1 of the Psalter, 10 Stichira combine 4 Resurrectional of the Tone, 3 of the Triodion, and 3 of the Martyrs; Doxastikon of the Triodion, first Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone; at the Aposticha, Resurrectional Stichira, Doxastikon of the Martyrs *Προφητικῶς ἀνεβόων*, Theotokion (*Ὢ θαύματος καινοῦ* on Sundays 2, 4, 5; Triodion Theotokia on Sundays 1 and 3); Dismissal Troparia of the Resurrection, the Martyrs, and the Triodion (*Τὴν ἄχραντον εἰκόνα σου* on Sunday 1; *Σῶσον Κύριε τὸν λαόν σου* on Sunday 3). [^279]: March 9: Commemoration of the Holy Forty Martyrs of Sebastea — Situation V Conclusion (§§ 9–10) and Situation VI (§ 11): Concurrence on Sundays 1 through 5 and Mondays of Weeks 2 through 6 of Great Lent. Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p439 (printed page 424) and facing English leaf p440 (printed page 425). Βασιλείου*, which the English facing text and liturgical context restore: "Epistle of the Saints and Gospel of the Sunday, and then the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great"); Liturgy of St. Basil the Great; Communion Verse: Psalm 4:7 LXX (*Ἐσημειώθη ἐφ᾿ ἡμᾶς*) on Sunday 3, and Psalm 148:1 LXX (*Αἰνεῖτε τὸν Κύριον*) on other Sundays; *Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς* (*Σῶσον Κύριε* on Sunday 3), and Dismissal. The kneeling troparion *Θεοτόκε Παρθένε* is omitted because the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts is celebrated on the morrow.

[^284]: March 9: Commemoration of the Holy Forty Martyrs of Sebastea — Situation XII Conclusion (§ 21, Festal Range Note); March 25: The Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos — Paramony Rubric and Situation I Opening (§ 1). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p449 (printed page 434) and facing English leaf p450 (printed page 435). Paragraph 21 concludes Situation XII: at the Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom, Typika and Beatitudes appoint 4 troparia from Ode 3 of the Theotokos and 4 from Ode 6 of the Saints; at the Little Entrance, the Third Antiphon refrain is chanted: "O come, let us worship and bow down before Christ; save us, O Son of God, wondrous art Thou in the Saints, who sing to Thee: Alleluia", followed by Troparion *Τὸ προσταχθὲν μυστικῶς*, that of the Saints, the Temple Patron, and Kontakion *Τῇ ὑπερμάχῳ*; Epistle of the Saints and Gospel *Ἀναστᾶσα Μαριάμ* (Luke 1:39–49, 56); Megalynarion at "Especially": *Ἅπας γηγενής*; Communion Verse: Psalm 115:4 LXX (*Ποτήριον σωτηρίου*); *Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*, and Dismissal. The general rubric notes that the Feast of the Forty Martyrs can fall within the Triodion cycle between Wednesday of the First Week of Great Lent and Great and Holy Monday of Passion Week. For March 25 (The Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos), the Paramony Rubric distinguishes cathedral/parish usage from monastic typika: when the Eve falls on Wednesday or Friday, parish churches do not celebrate the Presanctified Liturgy because they do not hold an all-night vigil (except on Great and Holy Tuesday and Wednesday), whereas monasteries celebrate the Presanctified Liturgy combined with Small Vespers according to ancient founder typika. Paragraph 1 establishes Situation I: when the Annunciation falls on Friday of the 3rd Week of Great Lent, on Wednesday morning at Vespers of the Presanctified, after Kathisma 18, at "Lord, I have cried", 10 Stichira combine the day's Idiomelon twice, Martyrikon once, 3 Prosomia of the sequence of the Triodion, and 4 Forefeast Prosomia *Κεκρυμμένον μυστήριον*; Doxastikon *Εὐαγγελίζεται ὁ Γαβριὴλ*, Little Entrance with "O Gladsome Light", Readings of the day, *Κατευθυνθήτω*, and the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts follows in due order.

Paragraph 7 details Saturday Matins: Dismissal Troparion of the Annunciation twice, of the Archangel once; Kathismata; Psalm 50 LXX; Canons of the Archangel and the Theotokos; after Ode 3, Kathisma of the Archangel and Theotokion; after Ode 6, Kontakion and Ikos of the Archangel.

Paragraph 13 details Saturday morning: Matins and Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom are celebrated for the Dead (All Souls Saturday of the 3rd Week) following the Typikon order.

[^298]: March 25: The Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos — Situation XI Conclusion (§§ 34–35 & Historical Synodical Note): Thursday morning Penitential Matins conclusion, plain Hours, Thursday evening Vespers with Presanctified Liturgy, and the landmark 1881 Synodical decree on the transfer of the Great Canon. Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p477 (printed page 462) and facing English leaf p478 (printed page 463). The Historical Note documents that in 1881, under Ecumenical Patriarch Joachim III, when the Annunciation fell on Wednesday of the 5th Week, a Synodical decree appointed the Great Canon to be chanted at Small Compline on Wednesday evening immediately following the Apodosis Vespers, followed on Thursday morning by Matins and Presanctified Liturgy.

Paragraph 38 details Thursday morning: following the Midnight Service, the Hexapsalmos, Alleluia with Isaiah 26:9 LXX verses (*Ἐκ νυκτὸς ὀρθρίζει*), Psalter, Octoechos and Triodion Kathismata; Psalm 50 LXX; Biblical Odes with Forefeast Canon *Ὁ κόσμος περιχαρῶς* (Odes 1 and 3), Forefeast Kathisma *Σήμερον ἅπασα*; Ode 4 combines Forefeast and Triodion; Odes 5 and 6 of Forefeast; after Ode 6, Heirmos, Litany, Kontakion *Ἐπελεύσει Πνεύματος τοῦ Παναγίου*, and Menologion; Beatitudes of the Triodion and Odes 7, 8, and 9; Katavasia of Ode 8 from Triodion; *Τὴν τιμιωτέραν*; Exapostilarion *Ὡς δῶρα προεόρτια*, *Σοὶ δόξα πρέπει*; Augmented Litany; Aposticha with Idiomelon *Ταῖς λῃστρικαῖς ἐφόδοις* twice, Martyrikon, and *Γλῶσσαν ἣν οὐκ ἔγνω*; Trisagion Prayers, Forefeast Dismissal Troparion *Σήμερον τῆς παγκοσμίου χαρᾶς*, *Ἐπουράνιε Βασιλεῦ*, and plain Hours; Thursday evening Great Vespers and Friday morning Festal Matins and Chrysostom Liturgy per Situation I §§ 3–5. Paragraph 39 details Friday evening: the Apodosis of the Feast takes place per Situation III § 18; after *Νῦν ἀπολύεις*, *Τὸ προσταχθὲν μυστικῶς* is sung twice slowly and once quickly, followed by the First Stasis of the Akathist Ikoi; Canon *Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου* through Ode 3; *Τῇ ὑπερμάχῳ* slowly and Second Stasis; Odes 4 to 6; *Τῇ ὑπερμάχῳ* quickly and Third Stasis; Odes 7 to 9; *Τῇ ὑπερμάχῳ* quickly and Fourth Stasis; Trisagion Prayers, Dismissal Troparion *Σήμερον τῆς σωτηρίας ἡμῶν τὸ κεφάλαιον* three times, and Dismissal.

[^301]: March 25: The Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos — Situation XIII Saturday of the Akathist (§§ 40–41, Historical Note) and Situation XIV Opening (§ 42): Concurrence on Friday of the 5th Week of Great Lent (celebration of the Akathist Saturday Service on the Paramony of the Annunciation) and Opening of Situation XIV (concurrence on Saturday of the Akathist). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p483 (printed page 468) and facing English leaf p484 (printed page 469). Paragraph 40 governs Saturday Matins: Midnight Service; *Τὸ προσταχθὲν μυστικῶς* and Hexapsalmos; at "God is the Lord", *Τὸ προσταχθὲν μυστικῶς* twice (the critical apparatus notes that the rubric specifying twice may represent a typographical erratum, since the dismissal troparion of a single festival is almost universally sung thrice); Kathisma 16 [Pss. 109–117 LXX]; Kathismata *Ὁ μέγας Γαβριήλ* and *Τῶν ἀσωμάτων λειτουργῶν* (from March 26); Psalm 50 LXX; Canons of the Akathist (*Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου*) and of the Archangel Gabriel; Kathisma *Ὁ μέγας στρατηγός*; at Odes 6, 7, 8, and 9, the Triodion Canons of the Martyrs are added; after Ode 6, Kontakion *Τῇ ὑπερμάχῳ*, Ikos *Ἄγγελος πρωτοστάτης*, Menologion, and Synaxarion of the Akathist; Katavasiai *Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου* with *Τὴν τιμιωτέραν*, and Katavasia of Ode 9 *Ἅπας γηγενής*; Exapostilarion *Τὸ ἀπ᾿ αἰῶνος σήμερον* twice; Praises with Prosomia *Κεκρυμμένον μυστήριον* to 4; "Glory... Both now and ever...": *Γλῶσσαν ἣν οὐκ ἔγνω*; Great Doxology, and *Τὸ προσταχθὲν μυστικῶς*. Paragraph 41 details the Divine Liturgy: Typika [Pss. 102, 145 LXX] and Beatitudes with 4 troparia from Ode 3 and 4 from Ode 6 of the Canon of the Theotokos; Little Entrance with Eisodikon *Δεῦτε προσκυνήσωμεν... ὁ ἐν Ἁγίοις θαυμαστός*; Troparia: *Τὸ προσταχθὲν μυστικῶς* and Kontakion *Τῇ ὑπερμάχῳ*; Epistle Hebrews 9:1–7; Gospel Luke 1:39–49, 56 (the printed English note mistakenly cited Luke 2, which is corrected in the text); Megalynarion *Ἅπας γηγενής*; Communion Verse Psalm 115:4 LXX (*Ποτήριον σωτηρίου λήψομαι*); and Dismissal. The Historical Note documents that this order was formally established in 1832 under Ecumenical Patriarch Constantine I. Paragraph 42 introduces Situation XIV: when the Annunciation occurs on Saturday of the Akathist, beginning with Thursday morning Vespers and the Psalter reading.

[^303]: March 25: The Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos — Situation XIV Conclusion (§ 43) and Apodosis (§§ 44–45), and Situation XV Opening (§ 46): Concurrence on Saturday of the Akathist (concluding Matins and Chrysostom Liturgy), Saturday evening Apodosis Vespers, Sunday Matins, Basil Liturgy, and Opening of Situation XV (concurrence on the 5th Sunday of Great Lent / Saint Mary of Egypt). Paragraph 45 details Sunday Matins: Triadic Canon and Hymns; at "God is the Lord", Troparia of Vespers; Kathisma 17 (Amomos); Resurrection Kathismata and one of the Feast; Evlogitaria, Hypakoe, Anavathmoi of the Tone, Prokeimenon; Canons of Resurrection and Feast; after Ode 3, Kathisma *Τῶν ἀσωμάτων λειτουργῶν*; after Ode 6, Resurrection Kontakion and Ikos, Menologion, and Synaxarion of the Triodion; Katavasiai *Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου*; Sunday Matins Gospel order; *Τὴν τιμιωτέραν*; Exapostilaria of Resurrection and two of the Feast; Praises combine 4 Resurrection and 4 Archangel (at which point the Apodosis of the Feast takes place); "Glory...": *Οὐκ ἔστιν ἡ βασιλεία τοῦ Θεοῦ*; "Both now and ever...": *Ὑπερευλογημένη*; Great Doxology, *Σήμερον σωτηρία*; and Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great with Typika and Beatitudes. Paragraph 46 introduces Situation XV: when the Annunciation falls on the 5th Sunday of Great Lent, beginning with Friday morning Vespers.

Paragraph 50 details Situation XVI: concurrence on the Friday before Lazarus Saturday. On Friday evening, at Great Vespers after the Introductory Psalm [Ps. 103 LXX], at "Lord, I have cried", 3 Prosomia of the Feast to 4 [repeating the first], Idiomelon *Τὴν ψυχωφελῆ πληρώσαντες* twice, and 4 Idiomela of Lazarus; "Glory...": *Τὴν ψυχωφελῆ πληρώσαντες* (great, slow melody); "Both now and ever...": *Ἀπεστάλη ἐξ Οὐρανοῦ*; Little Entrance, *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, and Prokeimenon Psalm 123:8 LXX. Aposticha: 3 Prosomia of Small Vespers (*Ἄγγελος πρωτοστάτης*); "Glory... Both now and ever...": *Ἐν τῷ μηνὶ τῷ ἕκτῳ*; Dismissal Troparion *Σήμερον τῆς σωτηρίας ἡμῶν τὸ κεφάλαιον* three times, and Dismissal. Canon of Lazarus is sung at Compline; on Saturday morning, Matins and Divine Liturgy of Lazarus are celebrated according to the Triodion order.

[^306]: March 25: The Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos — Situation XVII Opening (§§ 51–53): Concurrence on the Saturday of Lazarus (Thursday morning Vespers, Thursday evening Small Compline with Lazarus Canon, Friday morning Paramony Matins and plain Hours, Friday evening Festal Great Vespers, and Saturday Festal Matins through the Kathismata and Anavathmoi). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p493 (printed page 478) and facing English leaf p494 (printed page 479). In the evening, the Canon of Lazarus is sung with Small Compline; after Ode 9, Trisagion Prayers, Kontakion *Ἐπελεύσει Πνεύματος τοῦ Παναγίου*, *Κύριε ἐλέησον* (40 times), *Ὁ ἐν παντὶ καιρῷ*, *Ἄσπιλε, ἀμόλυντε*, and Dismissal. Aposticha: Idiomela of the Feast; "Glory... The Rubrical Note specifies that on both the Saturday of Lazarus and Palm Sunday, the Office of the Annunciation precedes both by reason of temporal priority and because the Annunciation is the fountainhead of our salvation and first beginning of all the Master Feasts of the Lord. Paragraph 53 begins Saturday Matins: Psalm 50 LXX, Litiya of the Feast, Trisagion Prayers, Dismissal Troparion of the Feast, and Hexapsalmos. At "God is the Lord", Dismissal Troparia as at Vespers, Psalter, and Polyeleos of the Feast. Kathismata: at 1st Stichologia, that of Lazarus; "Glory... Both now and ever...": of the Feast; at 2nd Stichologia, one of Lazarus and another of the Feast; at 3rd Stichologia, the two of the Feast; Anavathmoi (First Antiphon of Tone 4), and Prokeimenon of the Feast.

In the evening at the 9th Hour, Dismissal Troparion and Kontakion of the Feast; thus the Apodosis takes place per Patriarchs Constantine the Sinite and Gregory VI. Paragraph 55 introduces Situation XVIII: concurrence on Palm Sunday. On Friday at Vespers, after Introductory Psalm [Ps. 103 LXX] and Kathisma 18 [Pss. 119–133 LXX], at "Lord, I have cried", Idiomelon *Τὴν ψυχωφελῆ πληρώσαντες* twice, Martyrikon once, 4 Idiomela of Lazarus, and 3 Forefeast Prosomia; "Glory...": *Τὴν ψυχωφελῆ πληρώσαντες* (great, slow melody).

Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p509 (printed page 494) and facing English leaf p510 (printed page 495). Paragraph 68 concludes Holy Thursday Divine Liturgy: conclusion of the Eisodikon (*...who sing to Thee: Alleluia*); Dismissal Troparion of the Feast and Kontakion *Τῇ ὑπερμάχῳ*; Epistle 1 Corinthians 11:23–32 and composite Gospel of Holy Thursday [Matthew 26:1–20; John 13:3–17; Matthew 26:21–39; Luke 22:43–44; Matthew 26:40 – 27:2]; Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom; instead of the Cherubic Hymn (*Οἱ τὰ Χερουβίμ*), the Troparion *Τοῦ δείπνου σου τοῦ μυστικοῦ*; at "Especially", *Εὐαγγελίζου, γῆ, χαρὰν μεγάλην... Ὡς ἐμψύχῳ Θεοῦ κιβωτῷ*; Communion Verse Psalm 131:13 LXX (*Ἐξελέξατο Κύριος τὴν Σιών*); instead of *Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*, *Τοῦ δείπνου σου τοῦ μυστικοῦ* plainly, and Dismissal. In the evening, the Apodosis of the Feast is celebrated with Vespers per Situation III § 18. About the 1st hour of the night [7:00 PM], the Service of the Holy Passions (*Ἀκολουθία τῶν Ἁγίων Παθῶν*, Twelve Passion Gospels) is celebrated as prescribed in the Triodion. Paragraph 69 introduces Situation XXIII: concurrence on Holy and Great Friday or Holy and Great Saturday: the Feast is transferred and celebrated on Easter Sunday itself together with the Resurrection Office. Violakis transcribes the canonical decree of the Ecumenical Patriarchate: "The Holy and Great Church of Christ, having received this tradition from Patriarchs and Hierarchs from a century ago to the present, observing the disagreement and confusion occurring throughout all the churches in cities and villages, took thought especially concerning the great solemnity of the Annunciation. And indeed, for the cessation of the apparent anomaly, especially in the ears of the simple folk who do not perceive deeply the things of the Spirit, the Church ordained that, if the Feast falls on these two sorrowful days [Holy Friday or Holy Saturday], it should be transferred to the supreme day of Pascha itself named after the Lord. In order that joyous things may be sung and melodized together with joyous things, the Church by economy prevented the mixing of the bright with the sorrowful, in order also that no disharmony might be shown between: 'Today He is hung upon the tree' (*Σήμερον κρεμᾶται ἐπὶ ξύλου*) and: 'Today are the glad tidings of joy' (*Σήμερον χαρᾶς εὐαγγέλια*), and between: 'Pouring out tears of lamentation over Thee, O Pure One' (*Δακρυρρόους θρήνους ἐπὶ σὲ Ἁγνὴ*) and: 'Gabriel to the Virgin the glad tidings' (*Ὁ Γαβριὴλ τῇ Παρθένῳ τὸ Εὐαγγέλιον*). But these provisions, as has been said, pertain only to the churches in cities and villages..."


## Cohort 52 Footnotes

[^315]: March 25: The Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos — Situation XXIII Conclusion (§§ 69–70) and Situation XXIV Opening (§ 71): Patriarchal Decree Justification Regarding Monasteries, the Order of Annunciation Concurring on Great Saturday / Pascha (Holy Saturday Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great), and Opening of the Paschal Midnight Office. Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p511 (printed page 496) and facing English leaf p512 (printed page 497). Paragraph 69 concludes the canonical justification of the Ecumenical Patriarchate: while city and parish temples transfer the feast of the Annunciation to Easter Sunday when concurring on Great Friday or Great Saturday to prevent mixing sorrowful rites with joyful festal hymnody, the ancient Typika of the Holy Monasteries remain entirely unchanged, untouched, and unshakeable, out of reverence toward their venerable Founders and Fathers, and because of the distinct character and loftier spiritual asceticism of the monastic commonwealth. Paragraph 70 codifies the liturgical ordo when the Annunciation is transferred to Easter Sunday: on Holy and Great Saturday, after the Introductory Psalm [Ps. 103 LXX], at "Lord, I have cried" [Ps. 140 LXX], 3 Resurrection Stichira of Tone 1 (*Τὰς ἑσπερινὰς ἡμῶν εὐχάς*), 3 Idiomela of the Triodion (*Σήμερον ὁ ᾅδης*), and 4 Prosomia of the Feast (*Βουλὴν προαιώνιον*); "Glory...": *Τὴν Σήμερον μυστικῶς*; "Both now and ever...": *Ἀπεστάλη ἐξ οὐρανοῦ*. Entrance with the Holy Gospel, *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, and immediately without a Prokeimenon, four Old Testament Lessons: (1) Genesis 1:1–13; (2) Genesis 28:10–17 (of the Feast); (3) Jonah 1–4; and (4) Daniel 3:1–57 (the remaining 11 readings of the ancient vigil being omitted for brevity in parish usage). The Choirs sing in Tone 1 the Canticle *Τὸν Κύριον ὑμνεῖτε* with its scriptural verses; instead of the Trisagion, *Ὅσοι εἰς Χριστὸν ἐβαπτίσθητε* (Galatians 3:27); Epistle Romans 6:3–11; immediately in Grave Tone (Tone 7) the Priest, standing before the Royal Doors, intones *Ἀνάστα ὁ Θεὸς κρίνων τὴν γῆν* (Ps. 81:8 LXX) and scatters bay leaves throughout the Temple while the Choirs fulfill the verses of Psalm 81 LXX (*Ὁ Θεὸς ἔστη ἐν συναγωγῇ θεῶν*); Holy Gospel Matthew 28:1–20 (*Ὀψὲ Σαββάτων*), and the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great; instead of the Cherubic Hymn, the Troparion in Tone 5 *Σιγησάτω πᾶσα σὰρξ βροτεία*; Communion Hymn Psalm 77:65 LXX (*Ἐξηγέρθη ὡς ὁ ὑπνῶν Κύριος*); instead of *Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*, the irmologic Troparion in Tone 2 *Μνήσθητι εὔσπλαγχνε καὶ ἡμῶν*, and Dismissal. Paragraph 71 opens the Paschal Vigil: around the 4th hour of the night [10:00 PM], on the Sunday of Pascha, after...

[^316]: March 25: The Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos — Situation XXIV Continued (§ 71) and Opening of § 72: Paschal Midnight Office, Resurrection Procession, Paschal Matins Combined with Annunciation, and Opening of the Paschal Divine Liturgy. Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p513 (printed page 498) and facing English leaf p514 (printed page 499). Paragraph 71 details the full vigil sequence: *Εὐλογητὸς ὁ Θεός*, the Trisagion Prayers, Psalm 50 LXX, and the Holy Saturday Canon *Κύματι θαλάσσης*. At Ode 4, the Patriarch or presiding Hierarch takes the liturgical time (*καιρόν*), enters the Sanctuary, and vests in full pontifical vestments alongside concelebrating Hierarchs and Priests. After Ode 9, the clergy process from the Sanctuary into the narthex or outside platform, chanting first *Εὐαγγελίζου γῆ χαρὰν μεγάλην* once, then *Τὴν Ἀνάστασίν σου Χριστὲ Σωτήρ* continuously. The Deacon proclaims the petition, and the Patriarch reads the composite Morning Gospel of the Resurrection: Matthew 28:1–9, followed immediately by Matthew 28:16–20. After the exclamation *Δόξα τῇ Ἁγίᾳ καὶ Ὁμοουσίῳ*, the Patriarch intones *Χριστὸς ἀνέστη* three times, taken up by the Choirs, concluded with the Dismissal Troparion of the Feast *Σήμερον τῆς σωτηρίας ἡμῶν τὸ κεφάλαιον*. Entering the Temple after the Great Litany, the Paschal Canon is sung to 4 and the Canon of the Feast to 4; at each Ode, the Katavasia of Pascha, *Χριστὸς ἀνέστη* three times, *Ἀναστὰς ὁ Ἰησοῦς*, and the Little Litany. After Ode 3, the Ypakoi *Προλαβοῦσαι τὸν ὄρθρον*, and the Paschal Kontakion and Ikos; after Ode 6, the Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast, followed immediately by the Prokeimenon Psalm 95:2 LXX (*Εὐαγγελίζεσθε ἡμέραν ἐξ ἡμέρας*), *Πᾶσα πνοή*, and the Liturgy Gospel of the Feast Luke 1:24–38; *Ἀνάστασιν Χριστοῦ θεασάμενοι* three times; "Glory...": *Ταῖς τῆς Θεοτόκου*; "Both now and ever...": the same; Verse in Tone 2 Psalm 95:2 LXX, and the Idiomelon *Εὐαγγελίζεται ὁ Γαβριήλ*; *Σῶσον ὁ Θεὸς τὸν λαόν σου*, and the remaining Odes through Ode 8. Ode 9 is chanted with the respective Megalynaria of both Canons; Katavasia *Ὁ Ἄγγελος ἐβόα... Φωτίζου φωτίζου*; Exapostilaria: *Σαρκὶ ὑπνώσας*, *Ἀγγελικῶν δυνάμεων στρατηγός*, and *Σαρκὶ ὑπνώσας*. At the Praises, 4 Resurrection Stichira, 4 of the Feast, and the Paschal Stichira *Πάσχα ἱερόν* with their verses; "Glory...": *Τὸ ἀπ᾿ αἰῶνος μυστήριον*; "Both now and ever...": *Ἀναστάσεως ἡμέρα*; *Χριστὸς ἀνέστη* three times, and the Divine Liturgy begins. [^317]: March 25: The Annunciation of the Most Holy Theotokos — Situation XXIV Conclusion (§ 72), Patriarchal Historical Precedent of 1817 (Patriarch Cyril VI and the Definitive Kyrio-Pascha Codification), and Opening of Situation Twenty-Four [XXV] (§ 73): Bright Monday Concurrence. Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p515 (printed page 500) and facing English leaf p516 (printed page 501). Paragraph 72 concludes the Divine Liturgy of Kyrio-Pascha: Second Antiphon refrain *Ὁ ἀναστὰς ἐκ νεκρῶν*; Festal Eisodikon *Εὐαγγελίζεσθε ἡμέραν ἐξ ἡμέρας, σῶσον ἡμᾶς... ὁ ἀναστὰς ἐκ νεκρῶν*; *Χριστὸς ἀνέστη*; Dismissal Troparion *Σήμερον τῆς σωτηρίας*; Ypakoi *Προλαβοῦσαι τὸν ὄρθρον*; Paschal Kontakion *Εἰ καὶ ἐν τάφῳ*; baptismal hymn *Ὅσοι εἰς Χριστὸν ἐβαπτίσθητε* instead of the Trisagion; Epistle Acts 1:1–8 and Gospel John 1:1–17. At "Especially", the Paschal Megalynarion and Heirmos of Ode 9 *Ὁ Ἄγγελος ἐβόα... Φωτίζου φωτίζου*. Violakis provides critical footnote 4 noting that the Typikon of Constantine the Presbyter designated the Marian Megalynarion *Εὐαγγελίζου γῆ... Ὡς ἐμψύχῳ*, but the Ecumenical Patriarchal Typikon Committee firmly ratified the Paschal Megalynarion *Ὁ Ἄγγελος ἐβόα*, because Pascha is the Feast of Feasts and the supreme solemnity of the Universal Church. Communion Hymn *Σῶμα Χριστοῦ μεταλάβετε*; at the elevation, *Χριστὸς ἀνέστη*; instead of *Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*, *Χριστὸς ἀνέστη* three times plainly; the Paschal Catechetical Homily of Saint John Chrysostom, his Dismissal Troparion, and Dismissal. The historical note commemorates the canonical precedent of 1817 under Ecumenical Patriarch Cyril VI of Constantinople (martyred 1821), when the Annunciation coincided with Easter Sunday, establishing the definitive liturgical rubrics followed thereafter. Paragraph 73 introduces the concurrence on Bright Monday: on Easter Sunday evening at Agape Vespers, after *Δόξα τῇ Ἁγίᾳ καὶ Ὁμοουσίῳ*, *Χριστὸς ἀνέστη* with its verses; at "Lord, I have cried", 4 Resurrection Stichira of Tone 2 (*Τὸν πρὸ αἰώνων ἐκ Πατρός*) and 4 Prosomia of the Feast; "Glory... Both now and ever...": *Ἀπεστάλη ἐξ οὐρανοῦ*. On Bright Tuesday morning, only the Resurrection Office of the Pentecostarion is sung. The general rubric specifies that the order prescribed in §§ 73–76 governs concurrence on Bright Tuesday or Bright Wednesday as well, except that the Gospel read at Paschal Vespers is omitted on those days. The astronomical and calendar note observes that the Annunciation can concur within the Triodion from Friday of the Third Week of Great Lent through Bright Wednesday of the Pentecostarion, referencing the master Table of the Heortodromion. The codex then opens the Month of April with the Feast of Saint George the Trophy-Bearer (April 23). Situation One addresses concurrence on Holy and Great Saturday or Great Sunday [Easter Sunday]: the Saint's feast is transferred and celebrated on Bright Monday alongside the Resurrection Office. On Easter Sunday at Agape Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried" [Ps. 140 LXX], 4 Resurrection Stichira of Tone 2 (*Τὸν πρὸ αἰώνων ἐκ Πατρός*) and 4 Prosomia of the Saint; "Glory...": *Ἀξίως τοῦ ὀνόματος, στρατιῶτα Γεώργιε*; "Both now and ever...": the Dogmatikon *Παρῆλθεν ἡ σκιὰ τοῦ νόμου*. [^320]: Month of April: Commemoration of the Holy Glorious Great-Martyr George the Trophy-Bearer (April 23) — Situation One Conclusion (§§ 2–3) and General Bright Week Rubrics. Paragraph 2 concludes the Paschal Matins of Saint George on Bright Monday: after the Little Litany following Ode 6, the Anavathmoi (Tone 4, 1st Antiphon), Prokeimenon Psalm 91:13 LXX (*Δίκαιος ὡς φοῖνιξ ἀνθήσει*), Matins Gospel Luke 21:12–19 (*Προσέχετε ἀπὸ τῶν ἀνθρώπων*); *Ἀνάστασιν Χριστοῦ θεασάμενοι* once; "Glory...": *Ταῖς τοῦ Ἀθλοφόρου πρεσβείαις*; "Both now and ever...": *Ταῖς τῆς Θεοτόκου*; Psalm 50 LXX and Idiomelon *Τοῦ μεγάλου Βασιλέως* (noted that in Situation IV § 11 Violakis provides *Σήμερον ἡ οἰκουμένη πᾶσα*); intercessory prayer *Σῶσον ὁ Θεὸς τὸν λαόν σου*, *Κύριε ἐλέησον* 12 times, *Ἐλέει καὶ οἰκτιρμοῖς*. Φωτίζου φωτίζου*; Communion Hymn Psalm 111:6 LXX (*Εἰς μνημόσυνον αἰώνιον*) if the church is dedicated to Saint George (otherwise the Paschal Koinonikon *Σῶμα Χριστοῦ*, per 1998 Patriarchal Taxis, p. 291); *Χριστὸς ἀνέστη*, and Dismissal. At "God is the Lord", Dismissal Troparion of the Saint twice, of the Feast once; Psalter reading and Polyeleos; Sessional Hymns of the Saint, and one of the Feast in place of Theotokia; Anavathmoi (Tone 4, 1st Antiphon), Prokeimenon Psalm 91:13 LXX (*Δίκαιος ὡς φοῖνιξ ἀνθήσει*), *Πᾶσα πνοή*, and Matins Gospel of the Saint; *Ἀνάστασιν Χριστοῦ θεασάμενοι*, Psalm 50 LXX read; "Glory...": *Ταῖς τοῦ Ἀθλοφόρου*; "Both now and ever...": *Ταῖς τῆς Θεοτόκου*; Verse Psalm 50 LXX and Idiomelon *Σήμερον ἡ οἰκουμένη πᾶσα*; Canons of Feast and Saint; after Ode 3, Sessional Hymns of Saint and Feast; after Ode 6, Kontakion, Ikos, and Menologion of Saint; Katavasiai of Pascha; Magnificat (*Τὴν τιμιωτέραν*) followed by Katavasia of Ode 9 *Ὁ Ἄγγελος ἐβόα*; Exapostilarion of Saint and Feast. Praises: 4 Prosomia of Saint; "Glory...": of Saint; "Both now and ever...": of Feast; Great Doxology and Dismissal Troparion of Saint. Paragraph 12 details the Divine Liturgy: Paschal Antiphons and Eisodikon; Dismissal Troparia of Feast, Saint, and Temple Patron; Kontakion *Τῇ φιλοπράγμονι δεξιᾷ*; Epistle and Gospel of Saint; at "Especially", *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*; Communion Hymn Psalm 111:6 LXX (*Εἰς μνημόσυνον αἰώνιον*); *Χριστὸς ἀνέστη*, *Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*, and Dismissal. Violakis footnote 2 notes that this same order is kept on Tuesday through Friday of Thomas Week, with the 1st Stasis of the Psalter chanted, and 6 Stichira of the Saint with the daily Prokeimena at Vespers. Paragraph 13 opens Situation Five: concurrence on Saturday of the Apodosis of the Feast...

[^324]: Month of April: Commemoration of the Holy Glorious Great-Martyr George the Trophy-Bearer (April 23) — Situation Five Conclusion (§§ 13–15): Saturday of the Apodosis of Saint Thomas, and Situation Six Opening (§§ 16–17): Concurrence on Sunday of the Myrrhbearers (Third Sunday of Pascha). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p529 (printed page 514) and facing English leaf p530 (printed page 515). Paragraph 13 concludes Friday evening Vespers: at "Lord, I have cried" [Ps. 140 LXX], 4 Idiomela of the Feast and 4 of the Saint (noted that Pentecostarion text actually provides only 3 Stichira for Friday evening); "Glory...": of the Saint; "Both now and ever...": of the Feast. Little Entrance, *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, Prokeimenon and Old Testament Readings of the Saint; at the Aposticha, 3 Idiomela of the Feast; "Glory...": of the Saint; "Both now and ever...": of the Feast; Dismissal Troparion of Feast, Saint, and Feast, and Dismissal. Paragraph 14 details Saturday Matins: after Psalm 50 LXX, Litiya of the Saint; "Glory...": of the Saint; "Both now and ever...": of the Feast; Triadika, Trisagion, Dismissal Troparion of Feast, Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord", Dismissal Troparia as at Vespers; daily Psalter reading and Polyeleos of Saint etc., as in Situation IV § 11; Katavasiai of Pascha, Ode 9 of Feast and Saint, Katavasia *Ὁ Ἄγγελος ἐβόα*; Exapostilaria: first of Feast, of Saint, and second of Feast. Praises: 3 Prosomia of Feast, 3 of Saint; "Glory...": of Saint; "Both now and ever...": *Μεθ᾿ ἡμέρας ὀκτώ*; Great Doxology and Dismissal Troparion of Feast. Paragraph 15 details Divine Liturgy: Paschal Antiphons and Eisodikon; Dismissal Troparia of Feast, Saint, and Temple Patron; Kontakion *Τῇ φιλοπράγμονι δεξιᾷ*; Epistle and Gospel of Saint; at "Especially", *Σὲ τὴν φαεινὴν λαμπάδα*; Communion Hymn of the Feast Psalm 147:1 LXX (*Ἐπαίνει Ἱερουσαλήμ*); *Χριστὸς ἀνέστη*, *Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*, Dismissal, and Apodosis of the Feast of Thomas. Paragraph 16 opens Situation Six (concurrence on Sunday of the Myrrhbearers): Saturday evening Vespers after Introductory Psalm [Ps. 103 LXX] and Psalter reading, at "Lord, I have cried" [Ps. 140 LXX], 4 Resurrection Stichira of Tone 2, 2 of Myrrhbearers, and 4 of Saint; "Glory...": of Saint; "Both now and ever...": of Myrrhbearers. Little Entrance, *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, Prokeimenon, and Old Testament Readings of Saint; at the Aposticha, Resurrection Apostichon, Paschal Stichira *Πάσχα ἱερόν*; "Glory...": of Saint; "Both now and ever...": *Σὲ τὸν ἀναβαλλόμενον τὸ φῶς*; Dismissal Troparia: *Ὅτε κατῆλθες πρὸς τὸν θάνατον*, *Ὁ εὐσχήμων Ἰωσήφ*; "Glory...": of Saint; "Both now and ever...": *Ταῖς Μυροφόροις γυναιξί*, and Dismissal. Paragraph 17 opens Sunday Matins: after Psalm 50 LXX, Triadic Canon, Litiya of Myrrhbearers and Saint; "Glory...": of Saint; "Both now and ever...": *Ἠτήσατο Ἰωσήφ*; Triadika...
